# One hundred SaaS product dossiers

Research date: 2026-10-05. These are distinct candidate business workflows, not 100 validated markets or products to build. Global software delivery does not imply uniform legal, language or payment requirements. All estimates assume a focused first release, a full-time experienced builder and a capped pilot; no production SLA or audit certification is implied. Vendor statements establish category competition only. Proposed prices, channels, moats and scores are hypotheses.

[Decision report](decision-report.md) · [Ranked comparison](ranked-100.md) · [Evidence register](evidence-register.md) · [Scoring protocol](research-protocol.md)

<a id="s001"></a>
## S001 — Fixed-fee consulting engagement operations

**Rank 61/100 · Reserve · final 58/100** (base 66, challenge −8). Category: Professional services / consultancy delivery.

**Buyer:** Managing partner, 3–15-person strategy consultancies

**Job and payment hypothesis:** Protect fixed-fee margins while keeping client decisions and delivery synchronized. Less unrecoverable consultant time could justify payment.

**Current workaround:** Spreadsheets, shared documents and a general task board.

**Core record:** Engagement baseline with work packages, capacity commitments, approved changes and accepted deliverables.

**Full workflow:** Baseline scope and fee → Allocate consultant capacity → Review work and scope changes → Accept deliverables and reconcile margin

**Roles:** Partner; Engagement manager; Client sponsor.

**Entry hypothesis:** Start with repeatable diagnostic engagements; approval changes must update remaining effort and delivery commitments.

**Expansion:** Portfolio staffing and reusable engagement economics.

### Commercial evidence

- [Productive](https://productive.io/pricing/) — opened 2026-10-05. Lists budgeting, resource planning, tasks, time tracking, expenses and reporting. **Observed price:** not verified: extracted dynamic calculator totals conflict with displayed per-user amounts; currency and final commitment require confirmation.
- [Scoro](https://www.scoro.com/pricing/) — opened 2026-10-05. Combines work and financial apps; minimum five seats and two included core apps. **Observed price:** EUR 15/user/month starting price; minimum five users. Billing commitment and final app combination price not verified.

**Evidence limit:** Sources establish available alternatives, not unmet demand, customer willingness to switch, acquisition economics or our proposed price. Confidence: medium; official competitor evidence only.

### Economics and distribution

**Proposed plan:** subscription, USD 149/month; firm workspace; proposed monthly USD price. **34 accounts** reach at least $5,000 gross MRR before churn, discounts, taxes, fees and costs.

**Free boundary:** 14-day capped trial; no permanent hosted collaboration tier

**Paid boundary:** Persistent workflow, external collaboration, history and exports

**Lifetime:** Unsuitable for ongoing hosting, support and security obligations

**Acquisition test:** Hypothesis: offer a consent-based workflow clinic through Consulting Success community organizers; recruit partners with a live fixed-fee project.

**Retention:** Weekly staffing decisions and accumulated estimate-versus-actual history.

**Defensibility hypothesis:** Potential advantage is imported engagement history, not a generic dashboard.

### Delivery and operating risk

**First useful workflow:** Versioned engagement baseline; Capacity and time ledger; Client acceptance and margin close.

**Build estimate:** 12–18 full-time builder-weeks, excluding discovery, security certification and external procurement.

**Pilot cash:** USD 20–50/month; fits the $50 envelope under the stated assumptions. Three firms, 30 active engagements, 2 GB attachments; estimates exclude engineering, support, acquisition, taxes and payment fees.

**Cost drivers:** Database and files; cap uploads, no AI processing

**After revenue:** Up to $300 supports backups, monitoring and more file capacity

**Dependencies:** CSV time import; Accounting export, no payment execution.

**Global limits:** Currencies are labels; tax and invoicing remain in customer accounting systems.

**Risks:** Productive and Scoro already connect money and delivery; another PSA may add migration work.; Implementation consulting can erase subscription margins..

### Challenge and validation

**Independent challenge (−8):** Scope approval, effort and margin already sit inside general PSA products. The diagnostic-engagement label does not yet establish a separate economic record or migration benefit. First compare a configured Productive or Scoro engagement using the same historical change; kill if the outcome differs only in presentation.

**Test:** With five firms, replay one completed project and shadow a live one; seek three $149 monthly commitments tied to fewer unapproved hours.

**Kill gate:** Stop if firms require replacing their accounting system or see no advantage over configuring their PSA.

**Dimension scores:** pain 4/5, recurrence 4/5, budget 4/5, distribution 2/5, wedge 2/5, feasibility 3/5, global 4/5, cost 4/5.

**Score rationale:** Commercial job is clear; weak differentiation and difficult switching keep priority moderate.

**Sensitivity:** distribution-first rank 70; feasibility-first rank 62.

<a id="s002"></a>
## S002 — Design subscription agency operations

**Rank 35/100 · Reserve · final 64/100** (base 72, challenge −8). Category: Professional services / creative service delivery.

**Buyer:** Owner, 2–10-person design subscription agencies

**Job and payment hypothesis:** Deliver recurring creative requests within purchased capacity without losing revision or acceptance history. Recovering scheduling time and controlling revision work may justify a firm subscription.

**Current workaround:** Trello queues, Stripe records, Figma links and email.

**Core record:** Client subscription entitlement linked to queued briefs, asset revisions, approvals and capacity consumption.

**Full workflow:** Set service entitlement → Triage and schedule briefs → Produce and review revisions → Accept delivery and reset allowance

**Roles:** Agency owner; Designer; Client approver.

**Entry hypothesis:** Own queue policy and entitlement decisions across the full delivery cycle for asynchronous brand-design teams.

**Expansion:** Multiple service lanes and contractor allocation.

### Commercial evidence

- [ManyRequests](https://www.manyrequests.com/pricing) — opened 2026-10-05. Includes client portal, requests, billing, CRM, reporting, time tracking and design/video proofing. **Observed price:** not verified: page displays Core $59/month, but currency and toggle-specific billing basis are not explicit in extracted text; every plan includes one seat.
- [Filestage](https://filestage.io/pricing/) — opened 2026-10-05. Adjacent proofing alternative offers version comparison, review links and reminders. Free tier has one active project and five new files monthly. **Observed price:** Starter EUR 199/month includes ten seats; billing commitment not verified; extra seats sold in bundles of five.

**Evidence limit:** Sources establish available alternatives, not unmet demand, customer willingness to switch, acquisition economics or our proposed price. Confidence: medium; official competitor evidence only.

### Economics and distribution

**Proposed plan:** subscription, USD 99/month; firm workspace; proposed monthly USD price. **51 accounts** reach at least $5,000 gross MRR before churn, discounts, taxes, fees and costs.

**Free boundary:** 14-day capped trial; no permanent hosted collaboration tier

**Paid boundary:** Persistent workflow, external collaboration, history and exports

**Lifetime:** Unsuitable for ongoing hosting, support and security obligations

**Acquisition test:** Hypothesis: publish a queue-policy teardown in permissioned design-agency founder communities; invite owners to test their existing subscription rules.

**Retention:** Daily request queues and retained brand/revision decisions.

**Defensibility hypothesis:** Client-specific delivery history could become useful; portability remains mandatory.

### Delivery and operating risk

**First useful workflow:** Subscription entitlement ledger; Request queue and revision trail; Client acceptance and cycle report.

**Build estimate:** 10–16 full-time builder-weeks, excluding discovery, security certification and external procurement.

**Pilot cash:** USD 20–50/month; fits the $50 envelope under the stated assumptions. Three agencies, 100 requests and 5 GB files; estimates exclude engineering, support, acquisition, taxes and payment fees.

**Cost drivers:** Large assets and previews; use customer Figma links, cap files, no video transcoding

**After revenue:** Up to $300 adds storage, backup copies and worker isolation

**Dependencies:** CSV subscription import; Customer-owned Figma links.

**Global limits:** English-first; currency display and time zones needed; no design labor supplied.

**Risks:** ManyRequests already owns nearly this entire workflow: do not build merely for lower price.; A usable proofing experience can exceed the estimated effort..

### Challenge and validation

**Independent challenge (−8):** ManyRequests already bundles the request queue, billing, client portal and proofing. A more explicit entitlement policy may be a configuration improvement rather than a standalone product. Require an actual recurring dispute that current queue settings cannot represent, plus client willingness to use the proposed portal.

**Test:** Shadow three agencies for two billing cycles; test entitlement disputes against ManyRequests and seek three $99 monthly commitments.

**Kill gate:** Stop if existing queue settings solve the problem or clients refuse another portal.

**Dimension scores:** pain 4/5, recurrence 5/5, budget 3/5, distribution 3/5, wedge 2/5, feasibility 4/5, global 5/5, cost 4/5.

**Score rationale:** Frequent visible work and bounded pilot costs help; direct competition makes the wedge weak.

**Sensitivity:** distribution-first rank 30; feasibility-first rank 22.

<a id="s003"></a>
## S003 — Performance marketing campaign operations

**Rank 39/100 · Reserve · final 63/100** (base 69, challenge −6). Category: Professional services / marketing agency operations.

**Buyer:** Delivery director, paid-media agencies serving 10–40 clients

**Job and payment hypothesis:** Coordinate campaign budgets, creative approvals, launch checks and recurring optimization decisions. Avoided approval confusion and reporting assembly could fund the subscription.

**Current workaround:** Ads managers, spreadsheets, reporting dashboards and Slack.

**Core record:** Campaign plan linking budget authority, creative versions, deployment receipts and optimization decisions.

**Full workflow:** Approve plan and budget → Review launch assets → Record launch and checks → Review results and authorize changes

**Roles:** Account strategist; Media buyer; Client budget approver.

**Entry hypothesis:** Start with client-authorized budget changes and evidence of actual deployment across a complete campaign cycle.

**Expansion:** Experiment libraries and cross-client delivery capacity.

### Commercial evidence

- [AgencyAnalytics](https://agencyanalytics.com/pricing) — opened 2026-10-05. Offers reporting dashboards, data-source integrations, unlimited staff accounts and client access; adjacent to execution coordination. **Observed price:** USD 20 per client/month billed annually is displayed; minimum client commitment not verified.

**Evidence limit:** Sources establish available alternatives, not unmet demand, customer willingness to switch, acquisition economics or our proposed price. Confidence: medium; official competitor evidence only.

### Economics and distribution

**Proposed plan:** subscription, USD 129/month; firm workspace; proposed monthly USD price. **39 accounts** reach at least $5,000 gross MRR before churn, discounts, taxes, fees and costs.

**Free boundary:** 14-day capped trial; no permanent hosted collaboration tier

**Paid boundary:** Persistent workflow, external collaboration, history and exports

**Lifetime:** Unsuitable for ongoing hosting, support and security obligations

**Acquisition test:** Hypothesis: agency operations consultants can host a campaign handoff workshop; request anonymized launch packets before offering a pilot.

**Retention:** Weekly optimization and monthly campaign decisions accumulate context.

**Defensibility hypothesis:** Reusable decision history may help; advertising connectors alone are replicable.

### Delivery and operating risk

**First useful workflow:** Campaign register and budget authority; Creative approval and launch checklist; Results import and decision log.

**Build estimate:** 12–20 full-time builder-weeks, excluding discovery, security certification and external procurement.

**Pilot cash:** USD 20–50/month; fits the $50 envelope under the stated assumptions. Three agencies, 60 campaigns, CSV reporting; estimates exclude engineering, support, acquisition, taxes and payment fees.

**Cost drivers:** Ad API maintenance and media storage; postpone write access and use external assets

**After revenue:** Up to $300 funds bounded connectors, monitoring and imports

**Dependencies:** CSV metrics first; Read-only ad connectors only after demand.

**Global limits:** Platform permissions and advertising policies vary; no autonomous campaign spending.

**Risks:** AgencyAnalytics plus existing boards may suffice.; Without automatic metrics imports, duplicate entry may destroy adoption..

### Challenge and validation

**Independent challenge (−6):** Budget authorization is a credible recurring decision, but the pilot substitutes manual CSV receipts for the deployment evidence that creates its value. A reporting incumbent plus approval tickets may suffice. Test whether agencies pay while staff still reconcile ad platforms manually; do not count connector work as only a hosting expense.

**Test:** Run one monthly cycle for five agencies; measure duplicated work and seek three $129 commitments without ad-write automation.

**Kill gate:** Stop if buyers only value a reporting dashboard or require many live ad connectors immediately.

**Dimension scores:** pain 4/5, recurrence 5/5, budget 4/5, distribution 3/5, wedge 2/5, feasibility 2/5, global 4/5, cost 3/5.

**Score rationale:** Budget and recurrence are plausible; connector burden and incumbent reporting weaken feasibility.

**Sensitivity:** distribution-first rank 42; feasibility-first rank 59.

<a id="s004"></a>
## S004 — Bookkeeping practice close operations

**Rank 31/100 · Reserve · final 65/100** (base 70, challenge −5). Category: Professional services / accounting practice operations.

**Buyer:** Practice owner, 3–12-person outsourced bookkeeping firms

**Job and payment hypothesis:** Move each client through document collection, exception resolution, reviewer approval and monthly close. Reduced rework and fewer stalled closes may support recurring spend.

**Current workaround:** Accounting ledger plus email, recurring checklists and shared folders.

**Core record:** Client-period close file with source requests, unresolved transactions, reviewer decisions and release state.

**Full workflow:** Open client period → Collect and resolve exceptions → Review reconciliations → Release close pack and roll forward

**Roles:** Bookkeeper; Reviewer; Client contact.

**Entry hypothesis:** A close-first workspace for firms using multiple ledger products, with explicit reopened-period handling.

**Expansion:** Annual handover packs and capacity planning.

### Commercial evidence

- [Financial Cents](https://financial-cents.com/pricing/) — opened 2026-10-05. Offers workflow automation, client requests, file sharing, time tracking and month-end close as an add-on. **Observed price:** Team displays $49/user/month annually; monthly billing requires five users and all plans state a 12-month agreement. Currency not verified. Close add-on displays $5/client/month annually.
- [Content Snare](https://contentsnare.com/pricing/) — opened 2026-10-05. Adjacent intake product includes client requests, reminders, approvals and templates. **Observed price:** Basic USD 35/month billed annually or USD 42/month monthly; 20 active requests and two users included.

**Evidence limit:** Sources establish available alternatives, not unmet demand, customer willingness to switch, acquisition economics or our proposed price. Confidence: medium; official competitor evidence only.

### Economics and distribution

**Proposed plan:** subscription, USD 149/month; firm workspace; proposed monthly USD price. **34 accounts** reach at least $5,000 gross MRR before churn, discounts, taxes, fees and costs.

**Free boundary:** 14-day capped trial; no permanent hosted collaboration tier

**Paid boundary:** Persistent workflow, external collaboration, history and exports

**Lifetime:** Unsuitable for ongoing hosting, support and security obligations

**Acquisition test:** Hypothesis: partner with independent bookkeeping workflow trainers; offer a redacted close-file review through their opt-in audience.

**Retention:** Monthly close recurrence and carried-forward exception history.

**Defensibility hypothesis:** Cross-ledger close history is a possible advantage, not proven defensibility.

### Delivery and operating risk

**First useful workflow:** Period templates and requests; Exception resolution with reviewer gates; Locked close and controlled reopening.

**Build estimate:** 12–18 full-time builder-weeks, excluding discovery, security certification and external procurement.

**Pilot cash:** USD 20–50/month; fits the $50 envelope under the stated assumptions. Three firms, 60 client periods, 2 GB redacted files; estimates exclude engineering, support, acquisition, taxes and payment fees.

**Cost drivers:** Sensitive files, retention and accounting APIs; start with CSV and capped storage

**After revenue:** Up to $300 supports stronger backups and one validated ledger connector

**Dependencies:** CSV client and transaction import; Customer-owned ledger remains authoritative.

**Global limits:** No bookkeeping advice or ledger posting; local financial-data retention requirements remain customer-specific.

**Risks:** Financial Cents already offers the proposed core; configuring it may be superior.; Security review and ledger synchronization raise switching costs..

### Challenge and validation

**Independent challenge (−5):** The close-period record is distinct, but Financial Cents explicitly sells month-end close and request workflows. Reopening a period must reveal a reproducible incumbent gap. Redacted files establish usability only; production financial-data access and integration expectations remain separate adoption gates.

**Test:** Replay five redacted close files, then run a monthly cycle; require three $149 commitments and demonstrated reduction in repeated requests.

**Kill gate:** Stop if ledger-specific integrations are mandatory before value or existing close tools cover the gap.

**Dimension scores:** pain 4/5, recurrence 5/5, budget 4/5, distribution 3/5, wedge 2/5, feasibility 3/5, global 3/5, cost 3/5.

**Score rationale:** High recurrence, but strong direct overlap and sensitive data constrain the case.

**Sensitivity:** distribution-first rank 31; feasibility-first rank 41.

<a id="s005"></a>
## S005 — Independent audit engagement workspace

**Rank 80/100 · Defer · final 54/100** (base 60, challenge −6). Category: Professional services / assurance practice operations.

**Buyer:** Audit partner, small independent assurance firms

**Job and payment hypothesis:** Coordinate evidence requests, testing, review notes and controlled engagement completion. Less evidence chasing and clearer review ownership could justify payment.

**Current workaround:** Audit spreadsheets, document portals and Word workpapers.

**Core record:** Engagement file linking audit procedures, supplied evidence, preparer conclusions and reviewer sign-off.

**Full workflow:** Plan engagement and evidence requests → Assign procedures and document tests → Resolve reviewer notes → Freeze file and manage subsequent evidence

**Roles:** Audit manager; Preparer; Reviewer; Client evidence owner.

**Entry hypothesis:** Start with non-statutory operational assurance engagements using the firm’s own methodology.

**Expansion:** Reusable procedure libraries and portfolio scheduling.

### Commercial evidence

- [Suralink](https://www.suralink.com/) — opened 2026-10-05. Markets request-to-review accounting workflows: document request tracking, workpaper preparation, review collaboration and secure file sharing. **Observed price:** contact sales; currency, unit, billing commitment and minimum seats not verified.

**Evidence limit:** Sources establish available alternatives, not unmet demand, customer willingness to switch, acquisition economics or our proposed price. Confidence: medium; official competitor evidence only.

### Economics and distribution

**Proposed plan:** subscription, USD 199/month; firm workspace; proposed monthly USD price. **26 accounts** reach at least $5,000 gross MRR before churn, discounts, taxes, fees and costs.

**Free boundary:** 14-day capped trial; no permanent hosted collaboration tier

**Paid boundary:** Persistent workflow, external collaboration, history and exports

**Lifetime:** Unsuitable for ongoing hosting, support and security obligations

**Acquisition test:** Hypothesis: independent assurance trainers can convene an opt-in workpaper review clinic; request redacted operational audits.

**Retention:** Each engagement creates review history and reusable procedures.

**Defensibility hypothesis:** Firm-specific evidence lineage may matter; methodology expertise must remain with practitioners.

### Delivery and operating risk

**First useful workflow:** Evidence request register; Procedure workpapers and review notes; Sign-off, freeze and export.

**Build estimate:** 16–24 full-time builder-weeks, excluding discovery, security certification and external procurement.

**Pilot cash:** USD 60–150/month; exceeds the $50 envelope under the stated assumptions. Three redacted pilot engagements; estimates exclude engineering, support, acquisition, taxes and payment fees.

**Cost drivers:** Confidential evidence, access assurance and backup requirements exceed basic hosting alone

**After revenue:** Up to $300 permits isolated storage and monitoring, not certification

**Dependencies:** Document import and export; No statutory filing integration.

**Global limits:** Methodology and retention obligations differ; software provides no audit opinion.

**Risks:** Suralink already connects requests to reviews.; Professional confidentiality and irreversible file-finalization expectations create substantial assurance demands..

### Challenge and validation

**Independent challenge (−6):** The honest cash estimate exceeds the bootstrap cap before serving confidential evidence. Excluding statutory work reduces obligations but also weakens the budget inference from a full audit platform. Defer until a named operational-assurance niche accepts a metadata-only product and its actual security model.

**Test:** Walk through five closed operational audits with managers; seek three $199 conditional pilots after acceptable security review.

**Kill gate:** Defer if statutory methodology licensing or formal security attestations are prerequisites.

**Dimension scores:** pain 4/5, recurrence 4/5, budget 4/5, distribution 2/5, wedge 2/5, feasibility 2/5, global 2/5, cost 2/5.

**Score rationale:** Budget exists in the category; trust, localization and deep incumbent workflows impede bootstrap entry.

**Sensitivity:** distribution-first rank 84; feasibility-first rank 83.

<a id="s006"></a>
## S006 — Boutique legal matter operations

**Rank 92/100 · Defer · final 49/100** (base 57, challenge −8). Category: Professional services / legal practice operations.

**Buyer:** Managing partner, small advisory law firms

**Job and payment hypothesis:** Manage instructions, matter documents, internal review, client decisions and matter closure. A complete matter trail may reduce coordination time.

**Current workaround:** Email, folders, calendars and separate billing software.

**Core record:** Matter file with authorized instructions, parties, task dependencies, document versions and closure record.

**Full workflow:** Accept instructions and record parties → Plan matter work → Review documents and obtain decisions → Close matter and retain record

**Roles:** Partner; Paralegal; Client contact.

**Entry hypothesis:** Initially advisory matters with customer-defined deadlines; no court rules or trust accounting.

**Expansion:** Reusable matter playbooks and workload allocation.

### Commercial evidence

- [Clio](https://www.clio.com/pricing/) — opened 2026-10-05. Lists case management, collaboration, document storage and e-signature; FAQ states accounting is included across plans. **Observed price:** Starts at USD 49/user/month; billing basis and minimum seats not verified in opened FAQ.

**Evidence limit:** Sources establish available alternatives, not unmet demand, customer willingness to switch, acquisition economics or our proposed price. Confidence: medium; official competitor evidence only.

### Economics and distribution

**Proposed plan:** subscription, USD 129/month; firm workspace; proposed monthly USD price. **39 accounts** reach at least $5,000 gross MRR before churn, discounts, taxes, fees and costs.

**Free boundary:** 14-day capped trial; no permanent hosted collaboration tier

**Paid boundary:** Persistent workflow, external collaboration, history and exports

**Lifetime:** Unsuitable for ongoing hosting, support and security obligations

**Acquisition test:** Hypothesis: legal operations consultants can recruit opt-in boutique firms for a matter-process workshop.

**Retention:** Active matters and retained instruction history.

**Defensibility hypothesis:** Practice-specific templates might help, but Clio’s ecosystem is a stronger incumbent asset.

### Delivery and operating risk

**First useful workflow:** Matter and party register; Document review and client decisions; Deadline ownership and matter export.

**Build estimate:** 16–24 full-time builder-weeks, excluding discovery, security certification and external procurement.

**Pilot cash:** USD 60–120/month; exceeds the $50 envelope under the stated assumptions. Three firms using redacted advisory matters; estimates exclude engineering, support, acquisition, taxes and payment fees.

**Cost drivers:** Confidentiality, retention and security assurance; no automated legal research

**After revenue:** Up to $300 strengthens isolation and backups, not legal compliance

**Dependencies:** CSV matters; Customer billing system remains separate.

**Global limits:** Jurisdiction-specific duties and data handling; no legal advice or automatically calculated statutory deadlines.

**Risks:** Clio is affordable and substantially broader.; Conflict checking, trust accounts and court calendars may be required to switch..

### Challenge and validation

**Independent challenge (−8):** This is a crowded case-management workflow with its highest-value accounting and jurisdiction capabilities excluded. Advisory-only deadlines do not distinguish it from generic projects or Clio. Confidentiality acceptance and the already-failed cash gate should precede implementation, not follow a redacted demonstration.

**Test:** Demonstrate five redacted matters; require three $129 pilots that accept the defined scope and security controls.

**Kill gate:** Defer if buyers need jurisdiction rules or trust accounting before adoption.

**Dimension scores:** pain 4/5, recurrence 4/5, budget 4/5, distribution 2/5, wedge 1/5, feasibility 2/5, global 2/5, cost 2/5.

**Score rationale:** Important job but weak wedge and high trust burden make this a poor first build.

**Sensitivity:** distribution-first rank 92; feasibility-first rank 92.

<a id="s007"></a>
## S007 — Specialist permanent recruitment desk

**Rank 40/100 · Reserve · final 63/100** (base 69, challenge −6). Category: Professional services / recruitment agency operations.

**Buyer:** Owner, 2–8-person permanent-placement agencies

**Job and payment hypothesis:** Turn client vacancies into consented candidate submissions, interviews, offers and guarantee follow-through. Recovering recruiter coordination time and avoiding duplicate submissions might justify spend.

**Current workaround:** ATS spreadsheets, inboxes and separate client lists.

**Core record:** Vacancy mandate linked to candidate consent, submissions, interview decisions, placement and guarantee window.

**Full workflow:** Qualify vacancy and terms → Source and approve submissions → Coordinate interview and offer decisions → Confirm placement and track guarantee

**Roles:** Recruiter; Client hiring manager; Agency owner.

**Entry hypothesis:** Focus on candidate-controlled submissions and post-placement guarantees within one specialist desk.

**Expansion:** Reusable candidate relationships and multi-desk permissions.

### Commercial evidence

- [Manatal](https://www.manatal.com/pricing) — opened 2026-10-05. Supports jobs, candidate pipelines, client records and CSV/JSON imports; workflow automation appears in higher tiers. **Observed price:** Professional USD 15/user/month billed annually, 15 jobs and 10,000 candidates; minimum seats not stated. Enterprise USD 35/user/month annually.

**Evidence limit:** Sources establish available alternatives, not unmet demand, customer willingness to switch, acquisition economics or our proposed price. Confidence: medium; official competitor evidence only.

### Economics and distribution

**Proposed plan:** subscription, USD 99/month; firm workspace; proposed monthly USD price. **51 accounts** reach at least $5,000 gross MRR before churn, discounts, taxes, fees and costs.

**Free boundary:** 14-day capped trial; no permanent hosted collaboration tier

**Paid boundary:** Persistent workflow, external collaboration, history and exports

**Lifetime:** Unsuitable for ongoing hosting, support and security obligations

**Acquisition test:** Hypothesis: seek community-admin permission in specialist recruitment founder groups to run a vacancy handoff clinic.

**Retention:** New vacancies and placements renew candidate/client history.

**Defensibility hypothesis:** Consent and guarantee records could retain users; candidate sourcing is not supplied.

### Delivery and operating risk

**First useful workflow:** Vacancy and consent records; Client submission and interview workflow; Placement guarantee ledger.

**Build estimate:** 12–18 full-time builder-weeks, excluding discovery, security certification and external procurement.

**Pilot cash:** USD 20–50/month; fits the $50 envelope under the stated assumptions. Three agencies, 5,000 candidate records; estimates exclude engineering, support, acquisition, taxes and payment fees.

**Cost drivers:** Resume storage and outbound mail; cap attachments and avoid enrichment databases

**After revenue:** Up to $300 adds email integration and storage

**Dependencies:** CSV ATS import; Calendar links, no job-board aggregation.

**Global limits:** Consent, deletion and employment rules vary; no automated candidate rejection or scoring.

**Risks:** Manatal already offers inexpensive ATS functionality.; Client adoption and candidate privacy may outweigh any savings..

### Challenge and validation

**Independent challenge (−6):** Candidate consent and placement guarantees are coherent records, but a low-priced ATS already covers most of the desk. Prove a guarantee or submission decision that cannot be represented with existing fields and automation. Keep it separate from retained search only if both the buyer process and switching reason differ.

**Test:** Replay five completed placements and test two live vacancies per firm; seek three $99 commitments.

**Kill gate:** Stop if the only requested advantage is cheaper ATS seats or bundled sourcing data.

**Dimension scores:** pain 4/5, recurrence 5/5, budget 3/5, distribution 3/5, wedge 2/5, feasibility 3/5, global 4/5, cost 4/5.

**Score rationale:** Recurring workflow is real; low-cost incumbents sharply weaken differentiation.

**Sensitivity:** distribution-first rank 36; feasibility-first rank 42.

<a id="s008"></a>
## S008 — Retained executive search operations

**Rank 26/100 · Validate · final 66/100** (base 72, challenge −6). Category: Professional services / executive search delivery.

**Buyer:** Managing partner, 2–10-person retained-search boutiques

**Job and payment hypothesis:** Run confidential mandates with research coverage, off-limits rules, client calibration and staged placement decisions. Faster partner review and defensible client progress reporting may fund the subscription.

**Current workaround:** Private talent spreadsheets, presentation decks and email.

**Core record:** Search mandate with target-company map, relationship restrictions, candidate evidence and client decision history.

**Full workflow:** Agree mandate and target universe → Map coverage and contact permissions → Calibrate longlist and interviews → Record offer, placement and mandate review

**Roles:** Search partner; Research associate; Client sponsor.

**Entry hypothesis:** Own mandate-specific research coverage and client calibration for boutiques that bring their own candidate network.

**Expansion:** Cross-mandate relationship intelligence and research staffing.

### Commercial evidence

- [Clockwork](https://www.clockworkrecruiting.com/platform-pricing) — opened 2026-10-05. Offers target-company lists, research coverage reporting, client feedback and collaborative longlists alongside recruiting CRM. **Observed price:** Displays $149/user/month with annual/monthly toggle; currency and selected billing commitment not verified.
- [Loxo](https://loxo.co/pricing) — opened 2026-10-05. Core includes ATS, recruiting CRM, pipelines, reporting and email/calendar sync. **Observed price:** Core starts at $149/user/month billed annually and paid upfront; currency and minimum seats not verified.
- [Invenias by Bullhorn](https://www.bullhorn.com/products/invenias-executive-search/) — opened 2026-10-05. Executive-search platform describes target-company research, candidate longlists, executive insights, client collaboration and branded candidate presentations. This is a direct workflow substitute, not evidence of unmet boutique demand. **Observed price:** Not verified; opened product page offers a demo and pricing link but establishes no amount, currency, billing unit, commitment or minimum.

**Evidence limit:** Sources establish available alternatives, not unmet demand, customer willingness to switch, acquisition economics or our proposed price. Confidence: medium; official competitor evidence only.

### Economics and distribution

**Proposed plan:** subscription, USD 199/month; firm workspace; proposed monthly USD price. **26 accounts** reach at least $5,000 gross MRR before churn, discounts, taxes, fees and costs.

**Free boundary:** 14-day capped trial; no permanent hosted collaboration tier

**Paid boundary:** Persistent workflow, external collaboration, history and exports

**Lifetime:** Unsuitable for ongoing hosting, support and security obligations

**Acquisition test:** Hypothesis: independent executive-search coaches can introduce an opt-in mandate retrospective; demonstrate with anonymized research maps.

**Retention:** Multi-month mandates and reusable relationship restrictions.

**Defensibility hypothesis:** Permissioned research history may compound; proprietary talent data is not assumed.

### Delivery and operating risk

**First useful workflow:** Mandate and target-company map; Restricted candidate evidence and client calibration; Placement and engagement close.

**Build estimate:** 12–20 full-time builder-weeks, excluding discovery, security certification and external procurement.

**Pilot cash:** USD 20–50/month; fits the $50 envelope under the stated assumptions. Three firms, 5,000 contacts and 20 mandates; estimates exclude engineering, support, acquisition, taxes and payment fees.

**Cost drivers:** Candidate data and documents; no purchased enrichment or autonomous outreach

**After revenue:** Up to $300 adds backups, calendar sync and regional hosting

**Dependencies:** CSV research import; Customer-owned contact data.

**Global limits:** Cross-border candidate consent and confidentiality controls; human selection decisions only.

**Risks:** Clockwork already matches the wedge closely; a cheaper clone is insufficient.; Partners may require expensive enrichment and migration services..

### Challenge and validation

**Independent challenge (−6):** Suitable for comparative discovery, not a build recommendation. Clockwork already describes target coverage and client calibration; Invenias independently covers executive-search research, longlists and client presentation. The boutique and bring-your-own-network wedge is not established whitespace. Require paid commitments tied to a documented decision gap after replaying identical mandates in incumbent tools.

**Test:** Compare five past mandates against Clockwork; shadow three live searches and seek three $199 monthly commitments for demonstrably better calibration.

**Kill gate:** Stop if partners cannot identify a missing decision workflow or insist on bundled talent sourcing.

**Dimension scores:** pain 4/5, recurrence 4/5, budget 4/5, distribution 3/5, wedge 3/5, feasibility 3/5, global 4/5, cost 4/5.

**Score rationale:** Narrow buyer and valuable mandate records make validation plausible, but incumbent parity is a hard gate.

**Sensitivity:** distribution-first rank 23; feasibility-first rank 32.

<a id="s009"></a>
## S009 — Localization agency production operations

**Rank 16/100 · Validate · final 68/100** (base 75, challenge −7). Category: Professional services / language service operations.

**Buyer:** Operations owner, 2–8-manager translation agencies

**Job and payment hypothesis:** Quote multilingual work, assign linguists, control review, deliver files and reconcile vendor costs. Lower scheduling and reconciliation effort may support firm pricing.

**Current workaround:** CAT tools plus spreadsheets, email assignments and accounting exports.

**Core record:** Localization order containing language-pair jobs, word-count basis, rate versions, reviewer acceptance and vendor payable status.

**Full workflow:** Approve quote and source package → Assign translation and review jobs → Resolve quality issues and release delivery → Reconcile costs and client acceptance

**Roles:** Project manager; Linguist; Reviewer; Client approver.

**Entry hypothesis:** Start with recurring document localization and transparent rate/version handling across customer-owned CAT tools.

**Expansion:** Vendor performance history and reusable client pricing rules.

### Commercial evidence

- [Protemos](https://protemos.com/prices.html) — opened 2026-10-05. Agency pricing is per concurrent manager; unlimited manager, client and vendor accounts can be created. **Observed price:** EUR 249 per manager for three months or EUR 899 per manager annually; one-month trial; no additional seat minimum stated.
- [Protemos features](https://protemos.com/features.html) — opened 2026-10-05. Lists client prices, vendor rates, assigned jobs, deadlines, invoices, multi-currency and spreadsheet exports. **Observed price:** not verified on feature page; pricing source recorded separately.
- [Plunet](https://www.plunet.com/en/pricing/) — opened 2026-10-05. AgencyStart starts with two full users and 50 concurrent freelancer/client licenses; higher plans offer CAT interfaces. **Observed price:** contact sales; AgencyStart minimum two full-user licenses; currency and billing commitment not verified.
- [XTM Translation Business Management](https://xtm.ai/en-us/lsp-tbms) — opened 2026-10-05. LSP business-management product describes project templates, routing and tracking, client quote and file portal, vendor assignment, quote and invoice generation, and payment tracking. This is direct incumbent pressure on the full proposed workflow. **Observed price:** Not verified; opened product page establishes no amount, currency, billing unit, contract term or minimum.

**Evidence limit:** Sources establish available alternatives, not unmet demand, customer willingness to switch, acquisition economics or our proposed price. Confidence: medium; official competitor evidence only.

### Economics and distribution

**Proposed plan:** subscription, USD 149/month; firm workspace; proposed monthly USD price. **34 accounts** reach at least $5,000 gross MRR before churn, discounts, taxes, fees and costs.

**Free boundary:** 14-day capped trial; no permanent hosted collaboration tier

**Paid boundary:** Persistent workflow, external collaboration, history and exports

**Lifetime:** Unsuitable for ongoing hosting, support and security obligations

**Acquisition test:** Hypothesis: permissioned ProZ agency discussions or localization operations consultants can recruit firms for an order-cost reconciliation clinic.

**Retention:** Daily jobs, repeat client rate cards and vendor history.

**Defensibility hypothesis:** Accurate rate and quality history could become useful operational memory.

### Delivery and operating risk

**First useful workflow:** Order and rate calculation; Linguist/reviewer job workflow; Delivery acceptance and cost export.

**Build estimate:** 14–22 full-time builder-weeks, excluding discovery, security certification and external procurement.

**Pilot cash:** USD 20–50/month; fits the $50 envelope under the stated assumptions. Three agencies, 200 jobs monthly, 5 GB files; estimates exclude engineering, support, acquisition, taxes and payment fees.

**Cost drivers:** Document storage and CAT integrations; import analysis files, never host translation engines

**After revenue:** Up to $300 adds storage and one proven CAT connector

**Dependencies:** CAT analysis CSV; Accounting CSV export.

**Global limits:** Language scripts, currencies and withholding practices differ; no translation labor marketplace.

**Risks:** Protemos already implements most proposed stages at transparent pricing.; CAT compatibility and edge-case rates can consume support..

### Challenge and validation

**Independent challenge (−7):** Suitable for comparative discovery because order, vendor job, rate and acceptance form a coherent financial workflow. However Protemos, Plunet and XTM already cover the proposed lifecycle, including portals and vendor management. Rate-version transparency must beat an incumbent configuration, not merely spreadsheets. Test analysis-file portability before promising a CAT-neutral system.

**Test:** Replay 20 completed orders at five firms, then seek three $149 pilots that resolve a specific rate or review failure.

**Kill gate:** Stop if Protemos configuration resolves the issue or buyers require several live CAT connectors first.

**Dimension scores:** pain 4/5, recurrence 5/5, budget 4/5, distribution 3/5, wedge 3/5, feasibility 3/5, global 4/5, cost 4/5.

**Score rationale:** Concrete multi-role production records and bounded initial integrations merit validation; no unmet need is established.

**Sensitivity:** distribution-first rank 18; feasibility-first rank 26.

<a id="s010"></a>
## S010 — Qualitative research consultancy delivery

**Rank 51/100 · Reserve · final 60/100** (base 66, challenge −6). Category: Professional services / research consulting operations.

**Buyer:** Research director, 3–15-person qualitative consultancies

**Job and payment hypothesis:** Turn research briefs into traceable findings and client-approved recommendations across repeated studies. Reduced synthesis rework and easier evidence review may justify recurring spend.

**Current workaround:** Interview folders, spreadsheets, qualitative analysis tools and presentation decks.

**Core record:** Study evidence graph linking questions, sessions, coded excerpts, interpretations, review decisions and final claims.

**Full workflow:** Agree study questions and protocol → Collect consented session evidence → Code and peer-review findings → Publish evidence-linked client recommendations

**Roles:** Research lead; Analyst; Client stakeholder.

**Entry hypothesis:** Own consultancy handover and claim review across studies; customer provides transcripts initially.

**Expansion:** Cross-study learning with explicit client confidentiality boundaries.

### Commercial evidence

- [Dovetail](https://dovetail.com/pricing/) — opened 2026-10-05. Free plan contains one project and one channel; Enterprise offers repository governance, analysis and integrations. **Observed price:** Free USD 0; Enterprise contact sales, with minimum seats and contract commitment not verified.
- [Great Question](https://www.greatquestion.com/pricing) — opened 2026-10-05. Combines study methods, participant management and a research repository. **Observed price:** Self-serve displays $129/seat/month or $1,290/seat/year, up to five seats; currency not verified; incentives and external recruitment cost extra.

**Evidence limit:** Sources establish available alternatives, not unmet demand, customer willingness to switch, acquisition economics or our proposed price. Confidence: medium; official competitor evidence only.

### Economics and distribution

**Proposed plan:** subscription, USD 129/month; firm workspace; proposed monthly USD price. **39 accounts** reach at least $5,000 gross MRR before churn, discounts, taxes, fees and costs.

**Free boundary:** 14-day capped trial; no permanent hosted collaboration tier

**Paid boundary:** Persistent workflow, external collaboration, history and exports

**Lifetime:** Unsuitable for ongoing hosting, support and security obligations

**Acquisition test:** Hypothesis: ResearchOps community organizers could permit an evidence-handover workshop with consultancy leads.

**Retention:** Repeated studies and reusable coding decisions.

**Defensibility hypothesis:** Evidence lineage may help; avoid pooling confidential client research.

### Delivery and operating risk

**First useful workflow:** Study protocol and consent references; Coding and reviewer decisions; Claim-linked client delivery.

**Build estimate:** 12–20 full-time builder-weeks, excluding discovery, security certification and external procurement.

**Pilot cash:** USD 20–50/month; fits the $50 envelope under the stated assumptions. Three teams, text transcripts, 2 GB evidence; estimates exclude engineering, support, acquisition, taxes and payment fees.

**Cost drivers:** Recording/transcription costs; external links and manual transcripts initially

**After revenue:** Up to $300 adds bounded transcription only when paid usage supports it

**Dependencies:** Transcript import; Export to slides/document tools.

**Global limits:** Consent, data residency and deletion across studies require careful controls.

**Risks:** Dovetail and Great Question already cover analysis and collaboration.; A handover-only tool risks becoming an isolated feature..

### Challenge and validation

**Independent challenge (−6):** The core evidence-to-claim graph is a research-repository workflow already addressed by Dovetail and Great Question. Consultancy branding and final handover are not sufficient differentiation. Prove a repeated multi-client acceptance process buyers cannot run inside their current repository; manual transcripts must not create more duplicate evidence work than they remove.

**Test:** Replay five studies through brief-to-handover; seek three $129 commitments for fewer unsupported or repeatedly revised claims.

**Kill gate:** Stop if teams only want transcription or will not move study evidence from their repository.

**Dimension scores:** pain 4/5, recurrence 4/5, budget 3/5, distribution 3/5, wedge 2/5, feasibility 3/5, global 4/5, cost 4/5.

**Score rationale:** Research delivery is durable, but established repositories and migration friction weaken the entry case.

**Sensitivity:** distribution-first rank 46; feasibility-first rank 49.

## S011 — Research participant supplier operations

**Rank 34/100 · Reserve · final 65/100** (base 70, challenge −5). Category: Professional services / research recruitment services.

**Buyer:** Operations owner, participant recruitment boutiques with an existing consented panel

**Job and payment hypothesis:** Fill client research quotas while respecting participant consent, eligibility, attendance and incentive obligations. Less duplicate screening and fewer avoidable replacements could justify payment.

**Current workaround:** Panel spreadsheets, screener forms, calendar tools and payment records.

**Core record:** Recruitment order with quotas, screener versions, consented panel profiles, participation history and incentive settlement.

**Full workflow:** Define quota and screener → Invite eligible panel members → Confirm attendance and replacements → Reconcile incentives and refresh consent

**Roles:** Recruitment coordinator; Client researcher; Participant.

**Entry hypothesis:** Own multi-client quota fulfillment and participant over-contact controls; customers bring panel supply.

**Expansion:** Panel-quality review and repeat-client quota templates.

### Commercial evidence

- [Great Question](https://www.greatquestion.com/pricing) — opened 2026-10-05. Offers participant panels, eligibility rules, scheduling, incentives and study tools; this is adjacent to a specialist supplier operating across clients. **Observed price:** Self-serve displays $129/seat/month or $1,290/seat/year, maximum five seats; currency not verified. Enterprise has five-seat minimum and custom annual pricing; incentives extra.

**Evidence limit:** Sources establish available alternatives, not unmet demand, customer willingness to switch, acquisition economics or our proposed price. Confidence: medium; official competitor evidence only.

### Economics and distribution

**Proposed plan:** subscription, USD 149/month; firm workspace; proposed monthly USD price. **34 accounts** reach at least $5,000 gross MRR before churn, discounts, taxes, fees and costs.

**Free boundary:** 14-day capped trial; no permanent hosted collaboration tier

**Paid boundary:** Persistent workflow, external collaboration, history and exports

**Lifetime:** Unsuitable for ongoing hosting, support and security obligations

**Acquisition test:** Hypothesis: ask ResearchOps and qualitative-recruiter community organizers to permit a quota-planning clinic with established panel owners.

**Retention:** Repeated recruitment orders and consent/contact history.

**Defensibility hypothesis:** Reliable permissioned panel history may retain customers; no proprietary participant network is assumed.

### Delivery and operating risk

**First useful workflow:** Quota order and screener versioning; Eligibility, invitations and replacements; Attendance and incentive reconciliation.

**Build estimate:** 14–22 full-time builder-weeks, excluding discovery, security certification and external procurement.

**Pilot cash:** USD 20–50/month; fits the $50 envelope under the stated assumptions. Three suppliers, 5,000 profiles, 500 invitations monthly; estimates exclude engineering, support, acquisition, taxes and payment fees.

**Cost drivers:** Email and incentives; cap messaging, customer pays incentives directly

**After revenue:** Up to $300 supports message processing and audit storage

**Dependencies:** CSV panel import; Customer incentive-provider receipt import.

**Global limits:** Consent, sensitive screening data and incentive rules vary; avoid minors and medical studies initially.

**Risks:** Great Question already covers much of this workflow.; Fraud checks and recruitment supply can become an expensive service business..

### Challenge and validation

**Independent challenge (−5):** A supplier-owned quota ledger differs from a research repository, but panel access is the product prerequisite. Community visibility does not establish access to panel operators or their participant data. Validate replacement, over-contact and consent decisions using existing supply; abandon if recruitment sourcing or incentive financing is required.

**Test:** Replay five recruitment orders, then shadow live quotas; seek three $149 pilots with fewer avoidable replacement decisions.

**Kill gate:** Stop if value depends on us sourcing participants or financing incentives.

**Dimension scores:** pain 4/5, recurrence 5/5, budget 4/5, distribution 2/5, wedge 3/5, feasibility 3/5, global 3/5, cost 3/5.

**Score rationale:** Durable operational record, but distribution and sensitive participant data remain difficult.

**Sensitivity:** distribution-first rank 48; feasibility-first rank 48.

**Free boundary:** 14-day capped trial; no permanent hosted collaboration tier

## S013 — Remote back-office service operations

**Rank 18/100 · Validate · final 68/100** (base 72, challenge −4). Category: Professional services / outsourced business processing.

**Buyer:** Operations director, 5–30-person remote data-processing service firms

**Job and payment hypothesis:** Deliver contracted recurring work queues with consistent quality, turnaround commitments and accepted billable units. Less unbilled rework and clearer client acceptance could justify firm pricing.

**Current workaround:** Shared inboxes, task boards, sampling spreadsheets and client delivery folders.

**Core record:** Service order and work-item ledger with input version, processing result, QA sample, rework reason and accepted unit count.

**Full workflow:** Configure client service rules → Intake and assign batches → Process, sample and resolve rework → Client acceptance and period reconciliation

**Roles:** Service manager; Processor; Quality reviewer; Client approver.

**Expansion:** Quality trend analysis and cross-client capacity planning.

### Commercial evidence

- [Moxo](https://www.moxo.com/pricing) — opened 2026-10-05. Offers workflow builder, milestones, approvals, forms, decisions and file requests; adjacent configurable workflow substitute. **Observed price:** Team displays $5,000/year for 100 flows/year and unlimited seats; currency not verified. Scale is custom quote.
- [Process Street](https://www.process.st/pricing/) — opened 2026-10-05. Offers workflow runs, forms, approvals, enforced task order and role assignments; configurable substitute. **Observed price:** contact sales for Startup, Pro and Enterprise; Startup lists five users and ten guests; currency and billing minimum not verified.
- [Next Matter / DAIZY](https://www.daizy.com/pricing) — opened 2026-10-05. Configurable process-orchestration alternative describes workflow branching, approvals, forms, files, guest interfaces and dashboards. Unlimited workflows and external users are listed. It is an adjacent enterprise substitute, not proof of a small-provider budget. **Observed price:** Enterprise displays EUR 7,500 platform fee/month plus EUR 49/user/month and EUR 1/workflow run above 10,000 monthly. Billing commitment and minimum paid seats are not stated in the opened page. Custom enterprise pricing also offered.

**Evidence limit:** Sources establish available alternatives, not unmet demand, customer willingness to switch, acquisition economics or our proposed price. Confidence: medium; official competitor evidence only.

### Economics and distribution

**Proposed plan:** subscription, USD 199/month; firm workspace; proposed monthly USD price. **26 accounts** reach at least $5,000 gross MRR before churn, discounts, taxes, fees and costs.

**Free boundary:** 14-day capped trial; no permanent hosted collaboration tier

**Paid boundary:** Persistent workflow, external collaboration, history and exports

**Lifetime:** Unsuitable for ongoing hosting, support and security obligations

**Acquisition test:** Hypothesis: offer an opt-in batch-quality clinic through remote operations consultants serving established outsourcing firms.

**Retention:** Daily queues and monthly accepted-unit reconciliation.

**Defensibility hypothesis:** Client-specific exception and quality history may compound without requiring AI.

### Delivery and operating risk

**First useful workflow:** Versioned service rules and batch intake; Processing queue with sampled QA; Acceptance, rework and unit reconciliation.

**Build estimate:** 14–22 full-time builder-weeks, excluding discovery, security certification and external procurement.

**Pilot cash:** USD 20–50/month; fits the $50 envelope under the stated assumptions. Three providers, 20,000 text work items monthly, 2 GB files; estimates exclude engineering, support, acquisition, taxes and payment fees.

**Cost drivers:** Batch growth and attachments; limits, background imports, no browser automation

**After revenue:** Up to $300 adds workers, isolation and longer audit retention

**Dependencies:** CSV batches and results; Client-owned source systems remain authoritative.

**Global limits:** Initially nonsensitive catalog data; regulated processing and strict residency are separate gates.

**Risks:** Configuring Moxo or Process Street may already suffice; test against that option.; Custom client workflows could turn this into bespoke development..

### Challenge and validation

**Independent challenge (−4):** The strongest business-lane discovery candidate: accepted units and rework connect operational decisions to billing. Enterprise process products already support portals, approvals and routing, so they are substitutes rather than proof of a gap. The catalog-maintenance niche must share a stable unit and exception model across three providers; reject any need for customer-specific processing engines.

**Test:** Replay five real service batches; shadow three providers and seek three $199 commitments tied to reduced rework reconciliation.

**Kill gate:** Stop if each buyer requires a different processing engine or the team must perform the outsourced work.

**Dimension scores:** pain 4/5, recurrence 5/5, budget 4/5, distribution 2/5, wedge 3/5, feasibility 3/5, global 4/5, cost 4/5.

**Score rationale:** Concrete recurring unit economics and modest compute are attractive; unproven distribution is the main weakness.

**Sensitivity:** distribution-first rank 34; feasibility-first rank 31.

<a id="s014"></a>
## S014 — Virtual CFO engagement operations

**Rank 67/100 · Reserve · final 57/100** (base 63, challenge −6). Category: Professional services / financial advisory practice operations.

**Buyer:** Partner, small outsourced CFO firms serving recurring business clients

**Job and payment hypothesis:** Maintain approved financial assumptions, rolling scenarios and accountable management decisions across clients. Less spreadsheet-version confusion and repeated pack preparation may justify payment.

**Current workaround:** Ledger exports, spreadsheets, presentation packs and meeting notes.

**Core record:** Client planning cycle with versioned assumptions, scenarios, actuals mapping, reviewed forecasts and action commitments.

**Full workflow:** Import approved actuals → Revise assumptions and scenarios → Review forecast with client management → Record decisions and compare subsequent actuals

**Roles:** CFO adviser; Analyst; Client finance owner.

**Entry hypothesis:** Link customer-authored forecasts to management decisions; no autonomous financial recommendations or ledger replacement.

**Expansion:** Reusable sector planning models and multi-entity engagements.

### Commercial evidence

- [Fathom](https://www.fathomhq.com/pricing) — opened 2026-10-05. Offers branded reports, three-way forecasts, scenarios, consolidations and portfolio analysis. **Observed price:** not verified: dynamic currency selector is unresolved; rendered Gold example shows $450/month for 25 companies plus $17 each, but currency/final selected configuration is unclear.

**Evidence limit:** Sources establish available alternatives, not unmet demand, customer willingness to switch, acquisition economics or our proposed price. Confidence: medium; official competitor evidence only.

### Economics and distribution

**Proposed plan:** subscription, USD 179/month; firm workspace; proposed monthly USD price. **28 accounts** reach at least $5,000 gross MRR before churn, discounts, taxes, fees and costs.

**Free boundary:** 14-day capped trial; no permanent hosted collaboration tier

**Paid boundary:** Persistent workflow, external collaboration, history and exports

**Lifetime:** Unsuitable for ongoing hosting, support and security obligations

**Acquisition test:** Hypothesis: fractional-CFO communities may permit a forecast-version clinic with practitioners using recurring management meetings.

**Retention:** Monthly actuals and assumption revisions create longitudinal decision context.

**Defensibility hypothesis:** Client-specific planning history could retain accounts; spreadsheet import quality is crucial.

### Delivery and operating risk

**First useful workflow:** Versioned assumptions and actuals import; Scenario comparison and human review; Meeting decisions and next-cycle reconciliation.

**Build estimate:** 16–24 full-time builder-weeks, excluding discovery, security certification and external procurement.

**Pilot cash:** USD 20–50/month; fits the $50 envelope under the stated assumptions. Three firms, 30 clients, bounded spreadsheet models; estimates exclude engineering, support, acquisition, taxes and payment fees.

**Cost drivers:** Model evaluation and import complexity; support only defined CSV schemas initially

**After revenue:** Up to $300 supports scheduled imports and audit retention

**Dependencies:** CSV actuals; Customer forecasts imported through explicit schemas.

**Global limits:** Accounting conventions and currencies vary; qualified customer owns all financial judgments.

**Risks:** Fathom already provides sophisticated forecasting and reporting.; A thin meeting layer may be insufficient without comparable modeling..

### Challenge and validation

**Independent challenge (−6):** A forecast-to-decision wrapper risks becoming a meeting log beside Fathom or spreadsheets. If it evaluates arbitrary models it becomes a materially harder financial product; if it does not, its independent budget is uncertain. Require repeated version disputes to survive a configured incumbent replay, with a paid commitment to the explicitly bounded model format.

**Test:** Replay five forecast cycles, then shadow a monthly review; seek three $179 pilots for fewer version disputes.

**Kill gate:** Stop if value requires a full accounting engine or bespoke models for every client.

**Dimension scores:** pain 4/5, recurrence 4/5, budget 4/5, distribution 2/5, wedge 2/5, feasibility 2/5, global 3/5, cost 4/5.

**Score rationale:** Recurring budget is plausible, but modeling depth and incumbent functionality lower feasibility.

**Sensitivity:** distribution-first rank 73; feasibility-first rank 73.

<a id="s015"></a>
## S015 — Payroll bureau run operations

**Rank 62/100 · Defer · final 58/100** (base 64, challenge −6). Category: Professional services / payroll service administration.

**Buyer:** Bureau owner, outsourced payroll teams handling many employer accounts

**Job and payment hypothesis:** Collect changes, obtain employer approval, reconcile engine outputs and close each payroll run reliably. Fewer late changes and approval ambiguities may justify recurring spend.

**Current workaround:** Payroll engine, spreadsheets, emailed changes and separate approval records.

**Core record:** Employer payroll-run packet with input versions, approvals, engine-output references, exceptions and completion receipt.

**Full workflow:** Collect employer changes → Validate completeness and approve inputs → Import engine results and review exceptions → Authorize release externally and archive run

**Roles:** Payroll processor; Bureau reviewer; Employer approver.

**Entry hypothesis:** Own cross-client run coordination while licensed customer software performs payroll calculations and filing.

**Expansion:** Employer service schedules and exception trend analysis.

### Commercial evidence

- [BrightPay](https://brightsg.com/brightpay-cloud-payroll-software/) — opened 2026-10-05. Describes cloud payroll processing, automated calculations, legislative updates, multi-user collaboration and bureau workflows. **Observed price:** not verified: product page requires a configured price.
- [BrightPay pricing FAQ](https://payrollsupport.uk.brightsg.com/hc/en-gb/articles/36947262528017-FAQs-Pricing) — opened 2026-10-05. Pricing uses peak employer and employee counts; monthly arrears or annual advance billing is available. **Observed price:** not verified: amount/currency require calculator configuration; annual option prepays twelve months, excess usage charged separately.

**Evidence limit:** Sources establish available alternatives, not unmet demand, customer willingness to switch, acquisition economics or our proposed price. Confidence: medium; official competitor evidence only.

### Economics and distribution

**Proposed plan:** subscription, USD 199/month; firm workspace; proposed monthly USD price. **26 accounts** reach at least $5,000 gross MRR before churn, discounts, taxes, fees and costs.

**Free boundary:** 14-day capped trial; no permanent hosted collaboration tier

**Paid boundary:** Persistent workflow, external collaboration, history and exports

**Lifetime:** Unsuitable for ongoing hosting, support and security obligations

**Acquisition test:** Hypothesis: payroll process consultants can recruit opt-in bureau managers for a redacted run-retrospective workshop.

**Retention:** Weekly/monthly runs and employer change histories.

**Defensibility hypothesis:** Consistent approval lineage could retain firms; payroll engine integrations dominate switching.

### Delivery and operating risk

**First useful workflow:** Run calendar and change intake; Input/output approval gates; Exception and release-receipt archive.

**Build estimate:** 18–28 full-time builder-weeks, excluding discovery, security certification and external procurement.

**Pilot cash:** USD 75–200/month; exceeds the $50 envelope under the stated assumptions. Three bureaus with redacted run metadata; estimates exclude engineering, support, acquisition, taxes and payment fees.

**Cost drivers:** Sensitive salary data, assurance and engine adapters; minimize personal fields

**After revenue:** Up to $300 improves isolation and adapters, not payroll certification

**Dependencies:** Licensed payroll engine required, customer funded; Controlled CSV, no bank-file execution.

**Global limits:** Country-specific payroll engines, data privacy and deadlines make global rollout difficult.

**Risks:** BrightPay already includes bureau collaboration.; Reliable engine adapters and urgent payroll support may overwhelm a small team..

### Challenge and validation

**Independent challenge (−6):** The run packet is coherent, but the stated cash gate fails and payroll deadlines create support obligations that small hosting estimates do not represent. Customers may not accept a second approval record disconnected from their statutory engine. Require one-jurisdiction engine compatibility and explicit paid acceptance of the manual boundary first.

**Test:** Replay five redacted payroll runs in one jurisdiction; require three $199 pilots accepting no calculation or filing service.

**Kill gate:** Defer if bureaus require native engine synchronization, bank release or statutory calculations before payment.

**Dimension scores:** pain 5/5, recurrence 5/5, budget 4/5, distribution 2/5, wedge 2/5, feasibility 1/5, global 1/5, cost 2/5.

**Score rationale:** High operational pain does not overcome localization, support and security constraints.

**Sensitivity:** distribution-first rank 77; feasibility-first rank 84.

## S016 — Tax practice case operations

**Rank 93/100 · Defer · final 48/100** (base 53, challenge −5). Category: Professional services / tax practitioner administration.

**Buyer:** Practice owner, small firms managing individual and business tax cases

**Job and payment hypothesis:** Move recurring tax cases through organizer intake, missing evidence, preparer review, signature and filing receipt tracking. Fewer incomplete handoffs may save seasonal reviewer capacity.

**Current workaround:** Tax preparation package plus folders, spreadsheets and client email.

**Core record:** Tax-year case linking organizer answers, evidence, review exceptions, client authorization and external filing receipts.

**Full workflow:** Open case and request organizer → Resolve missing documents → Prepare externally and review → Record authorization, receipt and amendments

**Roles:** Preparer; Reviewer; Client taxpayer.

**Entry hypothesis:** Own family/entity case coordination and amendment history; tax calculations stay in the licensed customer engine.

**Expansion:** Year-to-year carry-forward records and workload planning.

### Commercial evidence

- [TaxDome](https://taxdome.com/pricing) — opened 2026-10-05. Offers tax organizers, documents, client portal, workflow automation, proposals and payments. **Observed price:** US page displays Essentials $800/seat/year with one-year commitment, solo only; Pro $1,000/seat/year. All plans upfront; currency appears as $ on US page, explicit currency confirmation not verified.

**Evidence limit:** Sources establish available alternatives, not unmet demand, customer willingness to switch, acquisition economics or our proposed price. Confidence: medium; official competitor evidence only.

### Economics and distribution

**Proposed plan:** subscription, USD 149/month; firm workspace; proposed monthly USD price. **34 accounts** reach at least $5,000 gross MRR before churn, discounts, taxes, fees and costs.

**Free boundary:** 14-day capped trial; no permanent hosted collaboration tier

**Paid boundary:** Persistent workflow, external collaboration, history and exports

**Lifetime:** Unsuitable for ongoing hosting, support and security obligations

**Acquisition test:** Hypothesis: independent tax-practice trainers can host an opt-in intake-to-review workshop using redacted prior-season cases.

**Retention:** Annual cases plus amendments; seasonality weakens steady monthly use.

**Defensibility hypothesis:** Accurate household/entity carry-forward history could help, but migration is costly.

### Delivery and operating risk

**First useful workflow:** Case and organizer versioning; Evidence requests and reviewer exceptions; Authorization and filing-receipt archive.

**Build estimate:** 16–24 full-time builder-weeks, excluding discovery, security certification and external procurement.

**Pilot cash:** USD 60–150/month; exceeds the $50 envelope under the stated assumptions. Three firms with redacted case metadata; estimates exclude engineering, support, acquisition, taxes and payment fees.

**Cost drivers:** Sensitive identifiers, evidence storage and seasonal load; avoid storing identity documents initially

**After revenue:** Up to $300 supports storage and backups, not tax preparation software

**Dependencies:** Customer tax-preparation engine required; CSV and document references.

**Global limits:** Country forms, deadlines and retention vary; no tax advice or filing execution.

**Risks:** TaxDome already owns the proposed lifecycle.; Avoiding sensitive documents may remove much of the value..

### Challenge and validation

**Independent challenge (−5):** TaxDome already combines organizers, evidence, portals and workflow without requiring this product to build a tax engine. The case-coordination wedge is therefore weak even before seasonality, identity data and the failed cash cap. Do not infer year-round subscription retention from a successful seasonal replay.

**Test:** Replay five cases including one amendment; seek three $149 commitments covering an entire season and off-season access.

**Kill gate:** Defer if buyers demand tax calculation, identity verification or government filing integrations.

**Dimension scores:** pain 4/5, recurrence 3/5, budget 4/5, distribution 2/5, wedge 1/5, feasibility 2/5, global 1/5, cost 2/5.

**Score rationale:** Large existing category, but weak differentiation, seasonality and jurisdiction dependence hurt fit.

**Sensitivity:** distribution-first rank 93; feasibility-first rank 93.
