## S017 — Remote architecture design practice operations

**Rank 68/100 · Reserve · final 57/100** (base 64, challenge −7). Category: Professional services / professional design practice.

**Buyer:** Practice principal, small architectural visualization and design-document teams

**Job and payment hypothesis:** Keep design phases, issue sets, client decisions and consultant effort aligned with agreed fees. Less rework on superseded documents and clearer phase economics may justify payment.

**Current workaround:** Project folders, PDF markups, spreadsheets and email transmittals.

**Core record:** Design commission with phase budgets, issued document sets, design decisions and consultant responsibilities.

**Full workflow:** Define commission phases and fee → Allocate consultants and plan issues → Review design changes and issue sets → Accept phase and reconcile effort

**Roles:** Principal; Project architect; Consultant; Client representative.

**Entry hypothesis:** Target remote design-document production; own phase acceptance and issued-set history without construction-site operations.

**Expansion:** Reusable phase budgets and design-decision libraries.

### Commercial evidence

- [Monograph](https://monograph.com/pricing) — opened 2026-10-05. Lists project accounting, unlimited projects and reports for architecture/engineering firms. **Observed price:** not verified: calculator renders $45–$490/month without an unambiguous selected plan, currency or billing commitment; ROI figures are vendor claims, not used.

**Evidence limit:** Sources establish available alternatives, not unmet demand, customer willingness to switch, acquisition economics or our proposed price. Confidence: medium; official competitor evidence only.

### Economics and distribution

**Proposed plan:** subscription, USD 149/month; firm workspace; proposed monthly USD price. **34 accounts** reach at least $5,000 gross MRR before churn, discounts, taxes, fees and costs.

**Free boundary:** 14-day capped trial; no permanent hosted collaboration tier

**Paid boundary:** Persistent workflow, external collaboration, history and exports

**Lifetime:** Unsuitable for ongoing hosting, support and security obligations

**Acquisition test:** Hypothesis: architecture practice-management advisers can invite principals to an opt-in phase-budget retrospective.

**Retention:** Long-running commissions and repeated document issue cycles.

**Defensibility hypothesis:** Linked issued-set and fee history may help; CAD file hosting is not the moat.

### Delivery and operating risk

**First useful workflow:** Commission phase budget; Issue-set and decision register; Consultant effort and phase acceptance.

**Build estimate:** 14–22 full-time builder-weeks, excluding discovery, security certification and external procurement.

**Pilot cash:** USD 20–50/month; fits the $50 envelope under the stated assumptions. Three practices, 30 commissions, PDF references; estimates exclude engineering, support, acquisition, taxes and payment fees.

**Cost drivers:** Large drawings; use customer storage links and cap uploaded PDFs

**After revenue:** Up to $300 adds document preview workers and storage

**Dependencies:** PDF/link references; CSV time and accounting exports.

**Global limits:** Professional liability and local design standards remain with clients; no engineering calculations.

**Risks:** Monograph plus existing document management may suffice.; BIM/CAD integration or construction administration could expand scope drastically..

### Challenge and validation

**Independent challenge (−7):** Phase fees and acceptance resemble the general consulting PSA candidate. Issued-set lineage is the potentially distinct record, but using linked PDFs may leave the source-of-truth problem in customer storage. Require proof of controlled issue and supersession decisions across a real consultant team before treating this as an additional product.

**Test:** Replay five completed phases and test one live issue cycle; seek three $149 commitments based on fewer superseded-set errors.

**Kill gate:** Stop if buyer value depends on BIM authoring, site inspections or local compliance calculations.

**Dimension scores:** pain 4/5, recurrence 4/5, budget 4/5, distribution 2/5, wedge 2/5, feasibility 3/5, global 3/5, cost 3/5.

**Score rationale:** Durable phase economics, but professional trust and incumbent accounting weaken the first-entry case.

**Sensitivity:** distribution-first rank 74; feasibility-first rank 66.

<a id="s018"></a>
## S018 — Private professional community operations

**Rank 85/100 · Defer · final 52/100** (base 58, challenge −6). Category: Professional services / community operations.

**Buyer:** Community director, independent professional networks with 200–2,000 existing members

**Job and payment hypothesis:** Turn recurring professional discussions into moderated participation and maintained reusable knowledge. Less repeated answering and fragmented moderation may justify hosted software spend.

**Current workaround:** Private chat groups, member spreadsheets and a separate knowledge wiki.

**Core record:** Community space linking member access, discussion threads, moderation decisions and approved knowledge articles.

**Full workflow:** Approve member access and orientation → Route discussions into appropriate groups → Moderate reports and resolve cases → Curate reviewed answers into knowledge → Review access, retire stale knowledge and export history

**Roles:** Community manager; Moderator; Member; Knowledge editor.

**Entry hypothesis:** Professional peer networks needing traceable discussion-to-knowledge curation, without courses, dues accounting or a supplied audience.

**Expansion:** Multiple private groups and editorial review queues within the same network.

### Commercial evidence

- [Discourse](https://www.discourse.org/pricing) — opened 2026-10-05. Hosted plans include members, chat, categories and groups. Free has two staff seats; Pro includes five staff seats, private categories, custom domain and API access. **Observed price:** Pro USD 100/month; Business USD 500/month. Free plan available. Pro includes five staff seats; annual commitment not stated on opened page.
- [Circle](https://circle.so/pricing) — opened 2026-10-05. Lists private spaces, moderation, member profiles, group chat, notifications and digests; Business adds workflows and APIs. **Observed price:** Professional USD 89/month based on annual billing, three admins and ten moderators; Business USD 199/month annually, five admins and fifteen moderators. Taxes excluded; transaction fees apply to payments.

**Evidence limit:** Sources establish available alternatives, not unmet demand, customer willingness to switch, acquisition economics or our proposed price. Confidence: medium; official competitor evidence only.

### Economics and distribution

**Proposed plan:** subscription, USD 79/month; community workspace; proposed monthly USD price. **64 accounts** reach at least $5,000 gross MRR before churn, discounts, taxes, fees and costs.

**Free boundary:** 14-day private trial capped at 50 members

**Paid boundary:** Discussion groups, moderation, knowledge lifecycle and retained history

**Lifetime:** Unsuitable for recurring hosting, notifications and abuse-response obligations

**Acquisition test:** Hypothesis: professional-community consultants could host an opt-in moderation and knowledge-handover clinic with existing network operators.

**Retention:** Daily discussions and recurring moderation/editorial reviews preserve community context.

**Defensibility hypothesis:** Permissioned discussion history may retain customers; the audience belongs to the operator.

### Delivery and operating risk

**First useful workflow:** Member access and orientation; Private discussion groups and moderation cases; Reviewed knowledge publishing, retirement and export.

**Build estimate:** 14–22 full-time builder-weeks, excluding discovery, security certification and external procurement.

**Pilot cash:** USD 20–50/month; fits the $50 envelope under the stated assumptions. Three communities, 500 total active members, 2 GB uploads and capped digests; estimates exclude engineering, support, moderation labor, acquisition, taxes and payment fees.

**Cost drivers:** Search indexing, notifications and abuse handling; text-first, no video hosting or paid AI. Customer supplies moderators.

**After revenue:** Up to $300 supports search workers, backups and email capacity; it does not fund human community management.

**Dependencies:** CSV member import; Email digests; no mandatory chat-platform migration connector.

**Global limits:** Language moderation, privacy and content policies vary; start with adult professional groups and operator-owned rules.

**Risks:** Discourse and Circle already provide mature communities and moderation; a configured incumbent may solve the entire problem.; Switching can destroy participation; operating the community ourselves would change the business..

### Challenge and validation

**Independent challenge (−6):** The full discussion/moderation/knowledge cycle is distinct from membership dues administration, resolving the duplicate. However Discourse and Circle already cover private community operations; discussion-to-knowledge curation may only require configuration. Participation migration is an adoption risk not solved by cheaper hosting. Reopen only after existing network operators demonstrate a repeated unsupported case and accept moving discussions.

**Test:** Replay five moderation and knowledge cases, then run one opt-in group for a month; seek three $79 commitments without supplying engagement labor.

**Kill gate:** Stop if configured Discourse solves the workflow or members will not move discussions.

**Dimension scores:** pain 3/5, recurrence 5/5, budget 3/5, distribution 2/5, wedge 1/5, feasibility 3/5, global 4/5, cost 3/5.

**Score rationale:** Recurring participation supports retention, but mature free/paid substitutes and migration risk make this a low-priority hypothesis.

**Sensitivity:** distribution-first rank 86; feasibility-first rank 76.

Offers scheduling, interpreter routing, rate cards, billing review and utilization reporting for language services. **Observed price:** contact sales; quoted factors include users, languages, session frequency/duration and support; currency, commitment and minimum not verified.

**Evidence limit:** Sources establish available alternatives, not unmet demand, customer willingness to switch, acquisition economics or our proposed price. Confidence: medium; official competitor evidence only.

### Economics and distribution

**Proposed plan:** subscription, USD 149/month; firm workspace; proposed monthly USD price. **34 accounts** reach at least $5,000 gross MRR before churn, discounts, taxes, fees and costs.

**Free boundary:** 14-day capped trial; no permanent hosted collaboration tier

**Paid boundary:** Persistent workflow, external collaboration, history and exports

**Lifetime:** Unsuitable for ongoing hosting, support and security obligations

**After revenue:** Up to $300 adds calendar sync and worker redundancy

<a id="s020"></a>
## S020 — Brand asset lifecycle operations

**Rank 91/100 · Defer · final 50/100** (base 56, challenge −6). Category: Professional services / digital asset management.

**Buyer:** Brand operations lead, small multi-brand service firms distributing assets to external partners

**Job and payment hypothesis:** Keep approved brand assets, permitted uses and current distribution versions synchronized. Fewer wrong-version requests and stale partner downloads might justify a subscription.

**Current workaround:** Cloud folders, approval emails and manually maintained brand portals.

**Core record:** Asset lineage with source file, reviewed versions, usage constraints, distribution permissions and retirement history.

**Full workflow:** Ingest asset and identify owner → Review versions and approve release → Record usage windows and partner permissions → Distribute controlled collections → Retire superseded assets and audit access

**Roles:** Brand manager; Creative contributor; Approver; External partner.

**Entry hypothesis:** Text and image brand libraries where permission expiry and replacement ownership matter across external distribution.

**Expansion:** Multiple brands, reusable usage rules and controlled partner portals.

### Commercial evidence

- [Dash](https://www.dash.app/pricing) — opened 2026-10-05. Includes version control, expiry/usage windows, guest-upload approval, metadata, collections and full exports. All features and unlimited users are included across plans. **Observed price:** Start Up GBP 79/month billed monthly, 10 GB storage and 250 monthly downloads; annual payment saves 10%. Extra storage GBP 25/month per 10 GB. Higher-tier calculator renderings are inconsistent, so not quoted.
- [Bynder](https://www.bynder.com/en/pricing/) — opened 2026-10-05. Offers digital asset management, asset approval workflows and distribution integrations; quotes depend on users, storage, usage, products and support. **Observed price:** contact sales; custom package. Currency, unit amounts, billing commitment and minimum seats not verified.

**Evidence limit:** Sources establish available alternatives, not unmet demand, customer willingness to switch, acquisition economics or our proposed price. Confidence: medium; official competitor evidence only.

### Economics and distribution

**Proposed plan:** subscription, USD 129/month; brand workspace; proposed monthly USD price. **39 accounts** reach at least $5,000 gross MRR before churn, discounts, taxes, fees and costs.

**Free boundary:** 14-day trial capped at 100 assets and 1 GB

**Paid boundary:** Version review, usage permissions, distribution, retirement and exports

**Lifetime:** Unsuitable for ongoing storage, delivery, security and preview processing

**Acquisition test:** Hypothesis: independent brand-management consultants could recruit opt-in teams for an expired-asset and partner-handover audit.

**Retention:** New campaigns, brand revisions and usage expiries recur across the retained library.

**Defensibility hypothesis:** Accurate asset and permission lineage may improve trust; storage alone is not defensible.

### Delivery and operating risk

**First useful workflow:** Asset ingest, metadata and version review; Usage constraints and controlled distribution; Retirement, replacement notices and full export.

**Build estimate:** 16–24 full-time builder-weeks, excluding discovery, security certification and external procurement.

**Pilot cash:** USD 40–100/month; exceeds the $50 envelope under the stated assumptions. Three brands, 10 GB total images/documents and bounded downloads; estimates exclude engineering, support, acquisition, taxes and payment fees.

**Cost drivers:** Object storage, backups, safe file previews and egress; exclude video transcoding, public CDN scale and automatic image generation.

**After revenue:** Up to $300 supports bounded preview workers, storage and monitoring; large video libraries need usage-priced plans.

**Dependencies:** Bulk file/metadata import; Downloadable collections; design-tool connectors deferred.

**Global limits:** Copyright and permitted-use interpretation remain customer-owned; privacy, retention and cross-border storage vary.

**Risks:** Dash already covers the proposed lifecycle at transparent small-business pricing.; Asset migration, preview fidelity and external download adoption can exceed the planned effort..

### Challenge and validation

**Independent challenge (−6):** The asset-version and distribution record is distinct from policy controls, resolving the duplicate. Dash already covers version control, usage windows and guest approvals at transparent small-business pricing. The proposed wedge must survive that direct comparison and justify asset migration; preview fidelity, egress and support exceed the cash cap. A bounded trial is not evidence of an economical production DAM business.

**Test:** Replay 100 assets through review, distribution and retirement at five teams; seek three $129 commitments for a specific failure existing DAM settings cannot resolve.

**Kill gate:** Stop if Dash configuration solves the issue or value requires video processing and many publishing integrations.

**Dimension scores:** pain 4/5, recurrence 4/5, budget 3/5, distribution 2/5, wedge 1/5, feasibility 2/5, global 4/5, cost 2/5.

**Score rationale:** A durable asset record qualifies as SaaS, but direct incumbent parity and file-processing costs warrant low priority.

**Sensitivity:** distribution-first rank 90; feasibility-first rank 89.

### Challenge and validation

**Independent challenge (−6):** Dradis already covers scoping, findings, review, reporting and retests, including self-hosting. The plan lacks an evidenced differentiator while handling particularly sensitive material and exceeding the cash cap. A metadata-only demonstration cannot settle deployment or assurance objections from security consultancies.

**Test:** Replay five redacted reports including retests; seek three $199 conditional pilots after security review.

**Kill gate:** Defer if credible demand requires air-gapped installs or independent attestations before revenue.

**Dimension scores:** pain 4/5, recurrence 4/5, budget 4/5, distribution 2/5, wedge 1/5, feasibility 2/5, global 3/5, cost 2/5.

**Score rationale:** Commercially important but direct mature competition and severe trust requirements make entry unattractive.

**Sensitivity:** distribution-first rank 89; feasibility-first rank 87.

<a id="s022"></a>
## S022 — Remote contract staffing operations

**Rank 63/100 · Defer · final 58/100** (base 64, challenge −6). Category: Professional services / staffing agency back office.

**Buyer:** Agency owner, small firms placing remote contractors with business clients

**Job and payment hypothesis:** Maintain assignments, approved hours, rate changes, extensions and reconciled payroll-provider handoff. Fewer rate errors and unapproved hours may justify payment.

**Current workaround:** ATS, timesheet spreadsheets and payroll/accounting exports.

**Core record:** Contract assignment ledger linking worker/client terms, approved time, rate-effective dates, extensions and settlement status.

**Full workflow:** Confirm assignment and rate terms → Collect and approve work periods → Resolve time/rate exceptions → Export settlement and extend or close assignment

**Roles:** Recruiter; Account manager; Contractor; Client approver.

**Entry hypothesis:** Remote professional contractors only; own assignment administration after placement without becoming employer or payroll processor.

**Expansion:** Assignment margin trends and extension planning.

### Commercial evidence

- [Bullhorn](https://www.bullhorn.com/products/applicant-tracking-crm/) — opened 2026-10-05. Offers staffing ATS/CRM, candidate and client records, placement workflows and ecosystem integrations; site also lists middle-office and time/expense products. **Observed price:** not verified: opened product page links pricing but gives no currency, billing unit, commitment or minimum seats.

**Evidence limit:** Sources establish available alternatives, not unmet demand, customer willingness to switch, acquisition economics or our proposed price. Confidence: medium; official competitor evidence only.

### Economics and distribution

**Proposed plan:** subscription, USD 199/month; firm workspace; proposed monthly USD price. **26 accounts** reach at least $5,000 gross MRR before churn, discounts, taxes, fees and costs.

**Free boundary:** 14-day capped trial; no permanent hosted collaboration tier

**Paid boundary:** Persistent workflow, external collaboration, history and exports

**Lifetime:** Unsuitable for ongoing hosting, support and security obligations

**Acquisition test:** Hypothesis: staffing back-office consultants can host a permissioned assignment-margin clinic for remote-contract agencies.

**Retention:** Weekly timesheets and recurring contract extensions.

**Defensibility hypothesis:** Rate and exception histories may be valuable; payroll integrations remain external.

### Delivery and operating risk

**First useful workflow:** Assignment and rate-effective ledger; Time approval and exceptions; Settlement export and extension workflow.

**Build estimate:** 18–26 full-time builder-weeks, excluding discovery, security certification and external procurement.

**Pilot cash:** USD 60–150/month; exceeds the $50 envelope under the stated assumptions. Three agencies with redacted assignment/time metadata; estimates exclude engineering, support, acquisition, taxes and payment fees.

**Cost drivers:** Payroll adapter accuracy, sensitive worker data and deadline support

**After revenue:** Up to $300 supports adapters and backups, not employer-of-record services

**Dependencies:** Customer payroll provider required; CSV time and settlement files.

**Global limits:** Employment, taxation and contractor classification remain qualified customer responsibilities.

**Risks:** Bullhorn’s middle-office ecosystem may solve the job already.; Country worker rules and accounting adapters create ongoing maintenance..

### Challenge and validation

**Independent challenge (−6):** Assignment and work-period records can differ from recruiting, but customers typically need trusted time-to-payroll reconciliation. Removing payroll processing does not remove adapter accuracy or deadline support. The declared cost gate fails; validate one external payroll handoff before splitting another staffing product from the recruitment family.

**Test:** Replay five assignments through a month-end and extension; seek three $199 pilots that accept external payroll.

**Kill gate:** Defer if payment requires statutory payroll, worker classification or employer-of-record responsibility.

**Dimension scores:** pain 5/5, recurrence 5/5, budget 4/5, distribution 2/5, wedge 2/5, feasibility 1/5, global 1/5, cost 2/5.

**Score rationale:** High recurrence and error cost, but integration and jurisdiction burdens dominate.

**Sensitivity:** distribution-first rank 78; feasibility-first rank 85.

<a id="s023"></a>
## S023 — Immigration practice case operations

**Rank 94/100 · Defer · final 47/100** (base 54, challenge −7). Category: Professional services / immigration practitioner administration.

**Buyer:** Practice owner, small licensed immigration firms in one initial jurisdiction

**Job and payment hypothesis:** Coordinate applicant evidence, family relationships, practitioner review, submission receipts and subsequent requests. Less duplicated evidence collection may justify a subscription.

**Current workaround:** Case-management package, questionnaires, folders and government portals.

**Core record:** Application case with family/entity relationships, evidence versions, practitioner decisions, receipt identifiers and follow-up requests.

**Full workflow:** Collect applicant circumstances and documents → Review completeness with practitioner → Record authorized submission externally → Track receipts, requests and case outcome

**Roles:** Practitioner; Caseworker; Applicant.

**Entry hypothesis:** Start with practice-authored workflows for one case type; no eligibility advice or government-form autofiling.

**Expansion:** Related-case reuse and practitioner workload planning.

### Commercial evidence

- [Docketwise](https://www.docketwise.com/pricing/) — opened 2026-10-05. Offers immigration forms, multilingual intake, case/priority-date tracking, document requests and client portal; Pro adds e-filing. **Observed price:** Basic displays $80/user/month in yearly section and $90/user/month monthly; currency and minimum seats not verified; annual commitment inferred from section, not quoted as confirmed.

**Evidence limit:** Sources establish available alternatives, not unmet demand, customer willingness to switch, acquisition economics or our proposed price. Confidence: medium; official competitor evidence only.

### Economics and distribution

**Proposed plan:** subscription, USD 149/month; firm workspace; proposed monthly USD price. **34 accounts** reach at least $5,000 gross MRR before churn, discounts, taxes, fees and costs.

**Free boundary:** 14-day capped trial; no permanent hosted collaboration tier

**Paid boundary:** Persistent workflow, external collaboration, history and exports

**Lifetime:** Unsuitable for ongoing hosting, support and security obligations

**Acquisition test:** Hypothesis: immigration practice-management trainers can invite licensed practitioners to an opt-in redacted case workshop.

**Retention:** Cases continue through repeated evidence requests and future applications.

**Defensibility hypothesis:** Permissioned family evidence lineage may help; jurisdiction knowledge is a major dependency.

### Delivery and operating risk

**First useful workflow:** Applicant/case relationships; Evidence and practitioner review; External receipt and follow-up tracking.

**Build estimate:** 18–28 full-time builder-weeks, excluding discovery, security certification and external procurement.

**Pilot cash:** USD 75–200/month; exceeds the $50 envelope under the stated assumptions. Three practices using redacted metadata; estimates exclude engineering, support, acquisition, taxes and payment fees.

**Cost drivers:** Identity documents, security assurance and changing government processes

**After revenue:** Up to $300 improves secure storage, not legal content maintenance

**Dependencies:** Customer government portal remains external; CSV and document references.

**Global limits:** Highly jurisdiction-specific; translation, immigration advice and statutory deadlines remain practitioner-owned.

**Risks:** Docketwise already implements intake through filing.; Avoiding forms and government integrations may eliminate switching value..

### Challenge and validation

**Independent challenge (−7):** This is a jurisdiction-specific legal case variant whose incumbent already supplies multilingual intake, forms and case tracking. Excluding maintained forms removes a major reason to switch, while confidential identity data and cost remain. Consolidate with legal case management and defer rather than counting a narrow case type as separate opportunity proof.

**Test:** Replay five closed cases with follow-up requests; seek three $149 pilots for reduced repeated document work.

**Kill gate:** Defer if customer demand requires eligibility advice, maintained official forms or filing automation.

**Dimension scores:** pain 4/5, recurrence 4/5, budget 4/5, distribution 2/5, wedge 1/5, feasibility 1/5, global 1/5, cost 2/5.

**Score rationale:** Persistent high-value cases, but global fit, trust and incumbent parity are poor.

**Sensitivity:** distribution-first rank 95; feasibility-first rank 98.

<a id="s024"></a>
## S024 — Travel advisory agency operations

**Rank 88/100 · Defer · final 51/100** (base 58, challenge −7). Category: Professional services / travel professional services.

**Buyer:** Agency owner, small custom-travel advisory teams

**Job and payment hypothesis:** Turn traveler briefs into approved itineraries, supplier confirmations, changes and completed-trip records. Less missed confirmation work and duplicated itinerary editing may justify subscription spend.

**Current workaround:** CRM, itinerary builder, supplier emails and spreadsheets.

**Core record:** Trip dossier with traveler preferences, itinerary versions, quoted components, confirmation references and change approvals.

**Full workflow:** Capture trip brief and propose options → Approve itinerary and budget → Record external supplier confirmations → Manage changes and close trip

**Roles:** Travel adviser; Agency assistant; Traveler approver.

**Entry hypothesis:** Own multi-adviser itinerary decisions and confirmation reconciliation; all booking and travel delivery remain with the agency.

**Expansion:** Reusable supplier knowledge and repeat-traveler preferences.

### Commercial evidence

- [Travefy](https://travefy.com/plans/pricing) — opened 2026-10-05. Core includes itinerary builder, CRM, website tools and mobile apps; annual team pricing distinguishes first and additional seats. **Observed price:** Core displays $39/month first seat on annual plan, $20/month additional seats; Premium $59 and $39 respectively. Currency not verified.

**Evidence limit:** Sources establish available alternatives, not unmet demand, customer willingness to switch, acquisition economics or our proposed price. Confidence: medium; official competitor evidence only.

### Economics and distribution

**Proposed plan:** subscription, USD 99/month; firm workspace; proposed monthly USD price. **51 accounts** reach at least $5,000 gross MRR before churn, discounts, taxes, fees and costs.

**Free boundary:** 14-day capped trial; no permanent hosted collaboration tier

**Paid boundary:** Persistent workflow, external collaboration, history and exports

**Lifetime:** Unsuitable for ongoing hosting, support and security obligations

**Acquisition test:** Hypothesis: independent travel-agency operations coaches can run an opt-in trip-file retrospective with adviser teams.

**Retention:** Active trips and repeat-traveler preference history.

**Defensibility hypothesis:** Verified supplier/decision history might help; destination content is not proprietary.

### Delivery and operating risk

**First useful workflow:** Trip brief and itinerary versions; Approval and confirmation register; Change handling and trip archive.

**Build estimate:** 14–22 full-time builder-weeks, excluding discovery, security certification and external procurement.

**Pilot cash:** USD 20–50/month; fits the $50 envelope under the stated assumptions. Three agencies, 100 active trips, linked documents; estimates exclude engineering, support, acquisition, taxes and payment fees.

**Cost drivers:** Sensitive traveler data and destination content; omit passport storage, customer supplier accounts

**After revenue:** Up to $300 supports calendar/email sync and storage

**Dependencies:** CSV client import; Supplier confirmations entered by agency; no GDS integration.

**Global limits:** Consumer protection and travel rules vary; software never books, collects trip funds or fulfills travel.

**Risks:** Travefy is inexpensive and broad.; Agencies may require booking inventory and commission automation before switching..

### Challenge and validation

**Independent challenge (−7):** Travefy offers itinerary, CRM and client presentation at a low entry price. Confirmation reconciliation could be useful, but without supplier connectivity its reliability depends on repeated manual work. The current evidence supports a feature request more than a replacement product; require a paid failed-incumbent comparison to reopen.

**Test:** Replay five trips with changes; seek three $99 pilots demonstrating fewer unmatched confirmation records.

**Kill gate:** Stop if value depends on inventory access, travel fulfillment or replacing GDS tools.

**Dimension scores:** pain 3/5, recurrence 4/5, budget 3/5, distribution 3/5, wedge 1/5, feasibility 3/5, global 3/5, cost 4/5.

**Score rationale:** Software delivery fits the model, but incumbent value and supplier dependencies weaken the wedge.

**Sensitivity:** distribution-first rank 75; feasibility-first rank 71.

<a id="s025"></a>
## S025 — Independent insurance brokerage operations

**Rank 97/100 · Defer · final 45/100** (base 53, challenge −8). Category: Professional services / brokerage administration.

**Buyer:** Operations head, small commercial insurance brokerages in one jurisdiction

**Job and payment hypothesis:** Coordinate customer risk information, insurer submissions, quote decisions, endorsements and renewal records. Less repeated rekeying and missed endorsement follow-through may justify recurring fees.

**Current workaround:** Agency management system, carrier portals and spreadsheets.

**Core record:** Policy placement dossier linking submitted risk facts, insurer terms, client instruction, bound-policy reference and endorsements.

**Full workflow:** Collect broker-reviewed risk information → Track insurer submissions and quotes → Record client instruction and external binding → Reconcile endorsements and prepare renewal

**Roles:** Broker; Account handler; Client representative.

**Entry hypothesis:** Initially one commercial line with customer-defined forms; administrative workflow without advice, pricing or binding authority.

**Expansion:** Historical placement evidence and carrier-specific workflows.

### Commercial evidence

- [Applied Epic](https://www1.appliedsystems.com/en-us/solutions/for-agents/agency-management-system/applied-epic/) — opened 2026-10-05. Describes prospecting, submissions, quoting, policy management, accounting and insurer connectivity across the policy lifecycle. **Observed price:** not verified: product page does not publish currency, billing unit, commitment or minimum seats.

**Evidence limit:** Sources establish available alternatives, not unmet demand, customer willingness to switch, acquisition economics or our proposed price. Confidence: medium; official competitor evidence only.

### Economics and distribution

**Proposed plan:** subscription, USD 249/month; firm workspace; proposed monthly USD price. **21 accounts** reach at least $5,000 gross MRR before churn, discounts, taxes, fees and costs.

**Free boundary:** 14-day capped trial; no permanent hosted collaboration tier

**Paid boundary:** Persistent workflow, external collaboration, history and exports

**Lifetime:** Unsuitable for ongoing hosting, support and security obligations

**Acquisition test:** Hypothesis: independent brokerage operations consultants can recruit opt-in account handlers for redacted placement reviews.

**Retention:** Annual renewals and ongoing endorsements create durable records.

**Defensibility hypothesis:** Permissioned policy history may retain users; carrier connectivity is the incumbent advantage.

### Delivery and operating risk

**First useful workflow:** Placement dossier and quote comparison; Client instruction and external policy receipt; Endorsement and renewal workflow.

**Build estimate:** 24–36 full-time builder-weeks, excluding discovery, security certification and external procurement.

**Pilot cash:** USD 150–400/month; exceeds the $50 envelope under the stated assumptions. Three brokerages, redacted placement metadata only; estimates exclude engineering, support, acquisition, taxes and payment fees.

**Cost drivers:** Security assurance, licensed forms, carrier interfaces and specialist support

**After revenue:** Up to $300 may cover hosting, but not required carrier integration or compliance costs

**Dependencies:** Carrier portals and licensed forms remain customer-owned; CSV only until integration terms are verified.

**Global limits:** Insurance licensing, forms and data rules are jurisdiction-specific; no recommendations or binding execution.

**Risks:** Applied Epic already embeds the entire policy lifecycle.; A useful replacement likely needs carrier downloads, accounting and regulated-data controls..

### Challenge and validation

**Independent challenge (−8):** The proposal exceeds both the bootstrap cap and, at its upper range, the later $300 operating allowance. Carrier interfaces and licensed forms are central to incumbent value; removing them risks generic dossier software. Budget, distribution and regulated-data access must be solved before a product pilot is credible.

**Test:** Review five placement/endorsement files with licensed brokers; seek three $249 conditional pilots without carrier integrations.

**Kill gate:** Defer if broker value depends on carrier connectivity, licensed forms or regulated transaction execution.

**Dimension scores:** pain 4/5, recurrence 5/5, budget 4/5, distribution 1/5, wedge 1/5, feasibility 1/5, global 1/5, cost 1/5.

**Score rationale:** Commercially durable, but acquisition, integration and operating cost fit are poor.

**Sensitivity:** distribution-first rank 100; feasibility-first rank 100.

<a id="s026"></a>
## S026 — Independent training-provider operations

**Rank 22/100 · Reserve · final 67/100** (base 73, challenge −6). Category: Education / delivery operations.

**Buyer:** Owner of an adult online training firm with 3–15 trainers.

**Job and payment hypothesis:** Close recurring corporate training orders without losing delivery or invoicing evidence. Avoid unreconciled delivery and delayed billing; savings must exceed another system’s cost.

**Current workaround:** Sheets, calendar invitations, an LMS and invoice software.

**Core record:** Training order linked to cohort, trainer allocation, attendance and accepted deliverables.

**Full workflow:** Agree order and delivery scope → Allocate trainers and enroll learners → Deliver sessions and assess completion → Resolve exceptions and approve client invoice

**Roles:** Delivery coordinator; Trainer; Client training manager.

**Entry hypothesis:** Order-to-client-acceptance traceability for firms retaining their existing LMS; Arlo already covers much of this.

**Expansion:** Trainer utilization, reusable client agreements and multi-program reporting.

### Commercial evidence

- [Arlo](https://www.arlo.co/pricing) — opened 2026-10-05. Direct training-operations competitor: course scheduling, registrations, CRM and customer/instructor portals appear in its plan comparison. **Observed price:** Simple displayed $118 per administrator/month billed annually, with $132 alternate monthly display; currency not verified. Paid registration $2.15 and free registration $1.07 displayed; implementation minimum not verified.
- [Teachfloor](https://www.teachfloor.com/pricing) — opened 2026-10-05. Direct cohort-learning alternative with courses, peer/group review and discussions. **Observed price:** Startup starts at $89/month; currency and minimum billing commitment not verified from opened page.

**Evidence limit:** Official descriptions establish category coverage, not dissatisfaction, switching demand or willingness to pay our proposed price. No customer interviews or paid pilots conducted. Confidence: medium; official competitor evidence only, customer demand unvalidated.

### Economics and distribution

**Proposed plan:** subscription, USD 149/month; workspace per month. **34 accounts** reach at least $5,000 gross MRR before churn, discounts, taxes, fees and costs.

**Free boundary:** 14-day trial with sample data; no permanent hosted team tier.

**Paid boundary:** Persistent workflow, permissions, history and operational exports.

**Lifetime:** Unsuitable for continuing hosted collaboration and support; setup fees would not count as MRR.

**Acquisition test:** Training Industry supplier directory: propose artifact walkthroughs to owner-led providers; access and conversion remain untested.

**Retention:** New cohorts reuse client terms, trainer availability and completion history.

**Defensibility hypothesis:** Reusable mappings between client deliverables and actual training evidence, if customers retain them.

### Delivery and operating risk

**First useful workflow:** Order/cohort registry; Trainer allocation and delivery log; Client acceptance and invoice CSV.

**Build estimate:** 10–16 full-time builder-weeks, excluding discovery, security certification and external procurement.

**Pilot cash:** USD 25–45/month; fits the $50 envelope under the stated assumptions. Three firms, 300 learners, text records and 1GB attachments; customers retain video/LMS subscriptions. Cash operating estimate; excludes engineering, support, acquisition, tax and payment fees.

**Cost drivers:** Attachments and transactional email; no streaming or content authoring.

**After revenue:** Up to $300/month after revenue for monitored backups, queues and measured usage; expand caps only after margins are observed.

**Dependencies:** CSV LMS completion import; Calendar export; Invoice CSV.

**Global limits:** Adult-only pilot; time zones, invoice conventions and learner privacy need localization.

**Risks:** Against building: Arlo already bundles registrations, CRM and portals.; Calendar complexity and duplicate LMS entry may outweigh value..

### Challenge and validation

**Independent challenge (−6):** Arlo already owns the commercial training cycle. A second system adds reconciliation work unless an existing LMS blocks a repeatedly valuable handoff. Do not infer a reachable budget from trainer count.

**Test:** With ten providers, reconstruct five completed orders; seek three $149/month commitments where reconciliation time falls by half.

**Kill gate:** Stop if three of five can reproduce the complete flow in Arlo without a material gap.

**Dimension scores:** pain 4/5, recurrence 5/5, budget 4/5, distribution 3/5, wedge 2/5, feasibility 3/5, global 4/5, cost 5/5.

**Score rationale:** Frequent paid delivery supports recurrence; mature integrated incumbents constrain differentiation.

**Sensitivity:** distribution-first rank 26; feasibility-first rank 23.

## S027 — Independent assessment-provider moderation

**Rank 57/100 · Defer · final 59/100** (base 66, challenge −7). Category: Education / assessment operations.

**Buyer:** Assessment director at a small professional examination provider.

**Job and payment hypothesis:** Release defensible assessment decisions across multiple assessors and appeals. Reduce re-marking administration and lost decision evidence.

**Current workaround:** Forms, shared rubrics, emailed marks and locked spreadsheets.

**Core record:** Candidate assessment case with rubric version, independent marks, moderation and appeal history.

**Full workflow:** Register case and freeze rubric → Allocate independent assessors → Reconcile discrepancies with moderator → Approve result and resolve appeals

**Roles:** Assessment administrator; Assessor; Moderator.

**Entry hypothesis:** Moderation and appeal continuity across imported assignments, without building an exam-delivery engine.

**Expansion:** Assessor calibration, panel capacity and longitudinal rubric quality.

### Commercial evidence

- [TestInvite](https://www.testinvite.com/lang/en/pricing.html) — opened 2026-10-05. Direct assessment platform: Advanced includes rubrics, multiple assessment stages and audit logs; Essential includes manual evaluation and controlled result release. **Observed price:** Advanced displayed $2,900/year for 500 testing credits and 10 teammates; annual basis. Currency not explicitly verified; overage and minimum additional credits not verified.

**Evidence limit:** Official descriptions establish category coverage, not dissatisfaction, switching demand or willingness to pay our proposed price. No customer interviews or paid pilots conducted. Confidence: medium; official competitor evidence only, customer demand unvalidated.

### Economics and distribution

**Proposed plan:** subscription, USD 199/month; workspace per month. **26 accounts** reach at least $5,000 gross MRR before churn, discounts, taxes, fees and costs.

**Free boundary:** 14-day trial with sample data; no permanent hosted team tier.

**Paid boundary:** Persistent workflow, permissions, history and operational exports.

**Lifetime:** Unsuitable for continuing hosted collaboration and support; setup fees would not count as MRR.

**Acquisition test:** Association of Test Publishers public member directory: identify small assessment providers for a moderation demonstration.

**Retention:** Each assessment cycle adds candidate cases and reusable calibration decisions.

**Defensibility hypothesis:** Consistent decision provenance and accepted rubric migration, rather than proprietary scoring.

### Delivery and operating risk

**First useful workflow:** Case/rubric versioning; Blind assessment and moderation; Appeal and release records.

**Build estimate:** 12–20 full-time builder-weeks, excluding discovery, security certification and external procurement.

**Pilot cash:** USD 25–45/month; fits the $50 envelope under the stated assumptions. Two providers and 200 de-identified low-stakes cases; no proctoring or identity verification. Cash operating estimate; excludes engineering, support, acquisition, tax and payment fees.

**Cost drivers:** Encrypted evidence and restricted access; video submissions remain external.

**After revenue:** Up to $300/month after revenue for monitored backups, queues and measured usage; expand caps only after margins are observed.

**Dependencies:** Bounded CSV marks import; Customer-controlled evidence links.

**Global limits:** Start with voluntary adult professional assessments; jurisdiction-specific accreditation remains outside scope.

**Risks:** TestInvite already offers rubrics and staged assessments.; Consequential decisions require human authority and security review..

### Challenge and validation

**Independent challenge (−7):** Human moderation and appeals are consequential workflows. A low infrastructure bill excludes domain assurance, permissions review and specialist sales; exam software may bundle the needed stages.

**Test:** Replay five past moderation cases with ten administrators; require three $199 pilots and agreement that provenance improves.

**Kill gate:** Stop if live use requires biometric proctoring, statutory certification or a full exam engine.

**Dimension scores:** pain 4/5, recurrence 4/5, budget 4/5, distribution 2/5, wedge 3/5, feasibility 2/5, global 3/5, cost 4/5.

**Score rationale:** Real decision complexity supports payment; trust and procurement reduce feasible initial reach.

**Sensitivity:** distribution-first rank 64; feasibility-first rank 69.

Currency not explicitly verified; standard workspace plan, annual option requires annual billing.

**Paid boundary:** Persistent workflow, permissions, history and operational exports.

**Lifetime:** Unsuitable for continuing hosted collaboration and support; setup fees would not count as MRR.

**Acquisition test:** LearnWorlds expert directory and Thinkific partner ecosystem: recruit implementation consultants for five customer-artifact demonstrations.

**Retention:** License changes and quarterly renewal discussions reuse entitlement and usage history.

**Defensibility hypothesis:** Trusted cross-platform import mappings and commercial account history, if migration is cheap enough.

### Delivery and operating risk

**First useful workflow:** Agreement and entitlement registry; Delegated seat allocation; Usage reconciliation and renewal packet.

**Build estimate:** 8–14 full-time builder-weeks, excluding discovery, security certification and external procurement.

**Dependencies:** Thinkific or LearnWorlds CSV export; Invoice CSV.

**Global limits:** Employer-controlled learner access, data deletion and regional tax documents require clear boundaries.

**Risks:** Against building: both incumbents already sell B2B groups and reports.; Customers may prefer one LMS rather than paying for an extra reconciliation layer..

### Challenge and validation

**Independent challenge (−6):** Cross-LMS contract reconciliation is unproven, while Thinkific and LearnWorlds already monetize B2B groups. Validate only publishers running multiple systems and repeated corporate renewals; single-LMS buyers do not establish this market.

**Test:** Show ten publishers a cross-LMS renewal packet using five real agreements; seek three $199 pilots and measurable monthly reconciliation savings.

**Kill gate:** Stop if fewer than three prospects actually use multiple systems or incumbents already reconcile their contract terms.

**Dimension scores:** pain 4/5, recurrence 5/5, budget 4/5, distribution 3/5, wedge 3/5, feasibility 3/5, global 4/5, cost 5/5.

**Score rationale:** Renewal economics and bounded metadata cost are attractive; cross-LMS pain remains a hypothesis, not an observed gap.

**Sensitivity:** distribution-first rank 14; feasibility-first rank 14.

<a id="s030"></a>
## S030 — Mentorship program operations

**Rank 59/100 · Reserve · final 58/100** (base 65, challenge −7). Category: Education / relationship programs.

**Buyer:** Program manager at a professional community running recurring adult mentoring programs.

**Job and payment hypothesis:** Keep mentor relationships progressing after initial matching. Less coordinator follow-up and fewer abandoned matches could support a recurring program budget.

**Current workaround:** Forms, matching spreadsheets, meeting links and quarterly surveys.

**Core record:** Mentorship relationship with goals, mutual acceptance, sessions, interventions and closure.

**Full workflow:** Enroll existing members and collect goals → Propose and accept matches → Record progress and coordinator interventions → Close, rematch or renew relationships

**Roles:** Program coordinator; Mentor; Mentee.

**Entry hypothesis:** Small professional communities that need relationship continuity without an enterprise HR deployment.

**Expansion:** Alumni mentoring, group mentoring and recurring program comparisons.

### Commercial evidence

- [Together](https://www.togetherplatform.com/pricing) — opened 2026-10-05. Direct mentorship-program platform with matching using goals, skills and interests, templates and outcome reporting. **Observed price:** Contact sales; customized pricing, amount/currency/unit/term not verified. Page says no initiation fees.

**Evidence limit:** Official descriptions establish category coverage, not dissatisfaction, switching demand or willingness to pay our proposed price. No customer interviews or paid pilots conducted. Confidence: medium; official competitor evidence only, customer demand unvalidated.

### Economics and distribution

**Proposed plan:** subscription, USD 99/month; workspace per month. **51 accounts** reach at least $5,000 gross MRR before churn, discounts, taxes, fees and costs.

**Free boundary:** 14-day trial with sample data; no permanent hosted team tier.

**Paid boundary:** Persistent workflow, permissions, history and operational exports.

**Lifetime:** Unsuitable for continuing hosted collaboration and support; setup fees would not count as MRR.

**Acquisition test:** European Mentoring and Coaching Council public community channels: offer a program-retrospective template and opt-in demonstration.

**Retention:** Relationship cycles and returning mentors create reusable history.

**Defensibility hypothesis:** Program-specific matching preferences and intervention practices, with explicit participant consent.

### Delivery and operating risk

**First useful workflow:** Participant/goal registry; Mutual match acceptance and progress log; Coordinator interventions and closure.

**Build estimate:** 8–12 full-time builder-weeks, excluding discovery, security certification and external procurement.

**Pilot cash:** USD 25–45/month; fits the $50 envelope under the stated assumptions. Three programs, 300 adults; text records, no call recording. Cash operating estimate; excludes engineering, support, acquisition, tax and payment fees.

**Cost drivers:** Notification volume and sensitive relationship notes; restrict retention and staff visibility.

**After revenue:** Up to $300/month after revenue for monitored backups, queues and measured usage; expand caps only after margins are observed.

**Dependencies:** CSV roster import; Calendar links.

**Global limits:** Exclude minors and employment evaluation; consent and access expectations differ.

**Risks:** Together already covers matching and outcomes.; Volunteer programs may lack budget or require continuous service support..

### Challenge and validation

**Independent challenge (−7):** Program outcomes may depend on facilitation and participant motivation rather than software. A subscription must survive volunteer budgets and Together existing matching/outcome features.

**Test:** Ten program managers review five completed cycles; seek three $99 commitments and lower coordinator handling time in one cycle.

**Kill gate:** Stop if engagement depends mainly on human facilitation rather than missing workflow infrastructure.

**Dimension scores:** pain 3/5, recurrence 4/5, budget 3/5, distribution 3/5, wedge 2/5, feasibility 4/5, global 4/5, cost 5/5.

**Score rationale:** Buildable and recurring, but budget and behavior-change value are weaker than revenue-linked workflows.

**Sensitivity:** distribution-first rank 50; feasibility-first rank 35.

<a id="s031"></a>
## S031 — Remote tutoring agency operations

**Rank 25/100 · Reserve · final 66/100** (base 73, challenge −7). Category: Education / tutoring businesses.

**Buyer:** Owner of a remote adult-language tutoring agency with 10–40 contractors.

**Job and payment hypothesis:** Reconcile lesson delivery, client balances and tutor pay across recurring arrangements. Reduce balance disputes and unapproved compensation; proposed value must beat inexpensive incumbents.

**Current workaround:** Calendars, prepaid-balance sheets, video links and accounting exports.

**Core record:** Learning service agreement linking learner, tutor, lesson ledger, balance and approved compensation.

**Full workflow:** Agree learning package and tutor assignment → Schedule and record lesson outcome → Apply cancellation rules and reconcile balances → Approve client invoice and tutor-pay export

**Roles:** Agency coordinator; Tutor; Client account owner.

**Entry hypothesis:** Explicit disputed-lesson resolution for agencies selling corporate language packages.

**Expansion:** Corporate account hierarchies and substitute-tutor handovers.

### Commercial evidence

- [TutorCruncher](https://tutorcruncher.com/pricing) — opened 2026-10-05. Direct tutoring business competitor; opened page links tutor, client and student management, billing/payroll and scheduling. **Observed price:** Not verified: pricing page opened but dynamic prices were not exposed. Currency, unit and minimum commitment unconfirmed.
- [Teachworks](https://www.teachworks.com/pricing) — opened 2026-10-05. Direct competitor links scheduling, billing, payroll and package balances; lesson charges count each attending student separately. **Observed price:** USD explicitly stated. Top Starter table shows $16.49/month plus $0.320/student lesson, billed after each month; a second rendered table shows $15 plus $0.25. Conflicting page rates require vendor confirmation. No setup/cancellation fees stated.

**Evidence limit:** Official descriptions establish category coverage, not dissatisfaction, switching demand or willingness to pay our proposed price. No customer interviews or paid pilots conducted. Confidence: medium; official competitor evidence only, customer demand unvalidated.

### Economics and distribution

**Proposed plan:** subscription, USD 129/month; workspace per month. **39 accounts** reach at least $5,000 gross MRR before churn, discounts, taxes, fees and costs.

**Free boundary:** 14-day trial with sample data; no permanent hosted team tier.

**Paid boundary:** Persistent workflow, permissions, history and operational exports.

**Lifetime:** Unsuitable for continuing hosted collaboration and support; setup fees would not count as MRR.

**Acquisition test:** The Tutors’ Association public member listings: identify remote agencies and offer one anonymized monthly-close reconstruction.

**Retention:** Weekly lessons and monthly pay approval retain the ledger.

**Defensibility hypothesis:** Accurate imported balances and agreed exception policies, without locking data in.

### Delivery and operating risk

**First useful workflow:** Agreement and lesson ledger; Cancellation/dispute workflow; Approved billing and pay CSV.

**Build estimate:** 10–16 full-time builder-weeks, excluding discovery, security certification and external procurement.

**Pilot cash:** USD 25–45/month; fits the $50 envelope under the stated assumptions. Three agencies and 1,000 lessons/month; customer-owned meeting tools, adults only. Cash operating estimate; excludes engineering, support, acquisition, tax and payment fees.

**Cost drivers:** Calendar synchronization, notifications and payment support; no payroll execution.

**After revenue:** Up to $300/month after revenue for monitored backups, queues and measured usage; expand caps only after margins are observed.

**Dependencies:** Calendar ICS; Invoice and compensation CSV.

**Global limits:** Time-zone changes, contractor classification and invoices vary; product records decisions without giving legal advice.

**Risks:** Against building: Teachworks and TutorCruncher already cover this business broadly.; Low competitor pricing weakens a generic replacement..

### Challenge and validation

**Independent challenge (−7):** TutorCruncher and Teachworks already own the full agency record. A disputed-lesson feature alone is too narrow; prove frequent revenue or contractor-pay reconciliation failures before replacement.

**Test:** Reconcile five real closes across ten agencies; require three $129 pilots and unresolved incumbent-specific exception pain.

**Kill gate:** Stop if disputed lessons are rare or implementation costs exceed three months of subscription.

**Dimension scores:** pain 4/5, recurrence 5/5, budget 3/5, distribution 4/5, wedge 2/5, feasibility 3/5, global 4/5, cost 5/5.

**Score rationale:** Clear recurring money workflow; strong existing low-cost software keeps wedge score low.

**Sensitivity:** distribution-first rank 13; feasibility-first rank 19.

## S032 — Curriculum governance for online colleges

**Rank 89/100 · Defer · final 51/100** (base 60, challenge −9). Category: Education / academic operations.

**Buyer:** Academic director at a small independent adult vocational college.

**Job and payment hypothesis:** Publish consistent course/program changes without losing approval and dependency history. Reduce incorrect program requirements and manual catalog reconciliation.

**Current workaround:** Word proposals, committee emails and catalog spreadsheets.

**Full workflow:** Propose curriculum change → Assess dependent courses and programs → Route faculty and committee approvals → Publish approved catalog version

**Roles:** Curriculum coordinator; Faculty proposer; Committee approver.

**Entry hypothesis:** Compact governance for small online colleges with a limited program catalog.

**Expansion:** Syllabus synchronization and scheduled teach-out transitions.

### Commercial evidence

- [Kuali Curriculum Management](https://kuali-ccm.zendesk.com/hc/en-us/articles/360003358014-Overview) — opened 2026-10-05. Direct competitor maintains canonical program/course versions and routes proposals through customized approvals. **Observed price:** Not verified: official product documentation publishes no amount, currency, billing unit or minimum term.

**Evidence limit:** Official descriptions establish category coverage, not dissatisfaction, switching demand or willingness to pay our proposed price. No customer interviews or paid pilots conducted. Confidence: medium; official competitor evidence only, customer demand unvalidated.

### Economics and distribution

**Proposed plan:** subscription, USD 199/month; workspace per month. **26 accounts** reach at least $5,000 gross MRR before churn, discounts, taxes, fees and costs.

**Free boundary:** 14-day trial with sample data; no permanent hosted team tier.

**Paid boundary:** Persistent workflow, permissions, history and operational exports.

**Lifetime:** Unsuitable for continuing hosted collaboration and support; setup fees would not count as MRR.

**Acquisition test:** Association for Learning Technology community: research small provider curriculum teams for artifact-based interviews.

**Retention:** Every academic revision depends on prior approved versions.

**Defensibility hypothesis:** Institution-specific dependency histories and reliable release semantics.

### Delivery and operating risk

**First useful workflow:** Course/program version graph; Proposal and approval routing; Catalog export and change log.

**Build estimate:** 12–20 full-time builder-weeks, excluding discovery, security certification and external procurement.

**Pilot cash:** USD 25–45/month; fits the $50 envelope under the stated assumptions. Two colleges, 300 curriculum records, no student transcripts or hosted courses. Cash operating estimate; excludes engineering, support, acquisition, tax and payment fees.

**Cost drivers:** Complex dependency migrations and institution-specific approval rules dominate cost beyond hosting.

**After revenue:** Up to $300/month after revenue for monitored backups, queues and measured usage; expand caps only after margins are observed.

**Dependencies:** CSV catalog import; Static catalog export.

**Global limits:** Qualification frameworks and catalog obligations differ; no accreditation guarantees.

**Risks:** Kuali already maintains canonical versions and approvals.; Academic procurement and custom rules challenge a small team..

### Challenge and validation

**Independent challenge (−9):** Curriculum governance has country/program-specific rules and institutional procurement. Integration and migration can dominate nominal hosting cost; small catalog size does not establish easy sales.

**Test:** Use five historical catalog changes from ten directors; seek three $199 commitments and prove all downstream changes remain visible.

**Kill gate:** Stop if useful adoption requires replacing the student information system.

**Dimension scores:** pain 4/5, recurrence 4/5, budget 3/5, distribution 2/5, wedge 2/5, feasibility 2/5, global 2/5, cost 5/5.

**Score rationale:** A substantial record exists; switching, approval variability and difficult access reduce attractiveness.

**Sensitivity:** distribution-first rank 88; feasibility-first rank 80.

It also offers employer projects, which this proposal would not supply. **Observed price:** Contact sales/demo; amount, currency, billing unit and minimum commitment not verified.

**Evidence limit:** Official descriptions establish category coverage, not dissatisfaction, switching demand or willingness to pay our proposed price. No customer interviews or paid pilots conducted. Confidence: medium; official competitor evidence only, customer demand unvalidated.

### Economics and distribution

**Proposed plan:** subscription, USD 179/month; workspace per month. **28 accounts** reach at least $5,000 gross MRR before churn, discounts, taxes, fees and costs.

**Full workflow:** Receive learner or instructor referral → Agree support plan → Coordinate advisor actions and progress → Close case or revise learning pathway

**Roles:** Advisor; Learner; Program coordinator.

**Expansion:** Re-enrollment workflows and aggregated program improvement evidence.

### Commercial evidence

- [Watermark Student Success & Engagement](https://www.watermarkinsights.com/solutions/student-success/) — opened 2026-10-05. Direct competitor combines learner pathways, interventions, milestones and SIS/LMS integrations. Outcome improvement claims on this page are vendor claims, not independent evidence. **Observed price:** Get a quote; amount, currency, billing unit and minimum commitment not verified.

**Evidence limit:** Official descriptions establish category coverage, not dissatisfaction, switching demand or willingness to pay our proposed price. No customer interviews or paid pilots conducted. Confidence: medium; official competitor evidence only, customer demand unvalidated.

### Economics and distribution

**Proposed plan:** subscription, USD 199/month; workspace per month. **26 accounts** reach at least $5,000 gross MRR before churn, discounts, taxes, fees and costs.

**Free boundary:** 14-day trial with sample data; no permanent hosted team tier.

**Paid boundary:** Persistent workflow, permissions, history and operational exports.

**Lifetime:** Unsuitable for continuing hosted collaboration and support; setup fees would not count as MRR.

**Acquisition test:** NACADA professional community: seek voluntary adult-program workflow interviews, without assuming institutional access.

**Cost drivers:** Access control, sensitive-data operations and contractual requirements exceed a casual shared-host pilot.

**Dimension scores:** pain 5/5, recurrence 5/5, budget 4/5, distribution 1/5, wedge 2/5, feasibility 2/5, global 2/5, cost 2/5.

Currency not explicitly named; annual commitment and minimum four users shown.

**Evidence limit:** Official descriptions establish category coverage, not dissatisfaction, switching demand or willingness to pay our proposed price. No customer interviews or paid pilots conducted. Confidence: medium; official competitor evidence only, customer demand unvalidated.

### Economics and distribution

**Proposed plan:** subscription, USD 99/month; workspace per month. **51 accounts** reach at least $5,000 gross MRR before churn, discounts, taxes, fees and costs.

**Free boundary:** 14-day trial with sample data; no permanent hosted team tier.

**Paid boundary:** Persistent workflow, permissions, history and operational exports.

## S036 — Independent journal editorial operations

**Buyer:** Managing editor of a small independent open-access journal portfolio.

**Job and payment hypothesis:** Move manuscripts through review, revisions and publication decisions without losing versions or conflicts. Reduce editorial coordination and avoid maintaining their own server; budget is uncertain.

**Current workaround:** Email, submission forms, shared folders or a locally maintained OJS installation.

**Core record:** Manuscript case with versions, reviewer assignments, conflict declarations and editorial decisions.

**Full workflow:** Receive and screen submission → Assign reviewers and record conflicts → Manage revisions and editorial decision → Transfer approved version to production

**Expansion:** Portfolio workload allocation and production handoffs.

### Commercial evidence

- [Scholastica](https://scholasticahq.com/pricing/) — opened 2026-10-05. Direct journal platform with manuscript assignment, versioning, anonymization and peer-review communication; publishing services priced separately. **Observed price:** Peer review displayed $10/submission plus $500/year; submissions billed monthly, annual fee yearly. Currency not explicitly named; page says no contracts/setup fees. Volume discounts separate.

**Evidence limit:** Scholastica pricing was opened; OJS is a named substitute requiring separate current deployment/pricing verification, not a newly verified source. Confidence: medium; official competitor evidence only, customer demand unvalidated.

### Economics and distribution

**Proposed plan:** subscription, USD 149/month; workspace per month. **34 accounts** reach at least $5,000 gross MRR before churn, discounts, taxes, fees and costs.

**Free boundary:** 14-day trial with sample data; no permanent hosted team tier.

**Paid boundary:** Persistent workflow, permissions, history and operational exports.

**Lifetime:** Unsuitable for continuing hosted collaboration and support; setup fees would not count as MRR.

**Acquisition test:** Directory of Open Access Journals publisher listings: identify independent editorial teams for opt-in workflow interviews.

**Retention:** Each manuscript remains active through several rounds and the decision archive persists.

**Defensibility hypothesis:** Reliable migrated review history and editorial service quality, not a novel workflow.

### Delivery and operating risk

**First useful workflow:** Submission/version register; Reviewer assignment and decisions; Production export.

**Build estimate:** 12–20 full-time builder-weeks, excluding discovery, security certification and external procurement.

**Pilot cash:** USD 25–45/month; fits the $50 envelope under the stated assumptions. Two journals, 100 active manuscripts and 2GB documents; no typesetting or plagiarism service bundled. Cash operating estimate; excludes engineering, support, acquisition, tax and payment fees.

**Cost drivers:** Document retention and editor support; third-party checks require customer contracts.

**After revenue:** Up to $300/month after revenue for monitored backups, queues and measured usage; expand caps only after margins are observed.

**Dependencies:** CSV submission import; Document bundle export.

**Global limits:** Publication ethics, anonymity and data retention need explicit policies.

**Independent challenge (−8):** Commercial workflow evidence coexists with a substantial free OJS substitute. Volunteer budgets, migration and editorial support make an inexpensive general replacement unattractive.

**Kill gate:** Stop if budgets cannot fund migration or free OJS already satisfies the workflow.

**Dimension scores:** pain 3/5, recurrence 4/5, budget 2/5, distribution 2/5, wedge 2/5, feasibility 2/5, global 4/5, cost 4/5.
