**Core record:** API version contract with registered consumers and migration decisions.

**Full workflow:** Propose contract → Assess consumers → Approve change → Track migration → Retire version

**Roles:** API owner; Consumer integration owner.

**Entry hypothesis:** Explicit consumer acceptance and retirement evidence beyond schema linting.

**Expansion:** Compatibility commitments across related APIs and SDK releases.

### Commercial evidence

- [Postman](https://www.postman.com/pricing/) — opened 2026-10-05. API specifications, collections, mocks and team collaboration; SDK distribution included in Team. **Observed price:** Free $0; Team displayed $19/user/month billed annually; Solo $9/month annually. Dollar ISO code/seat minimum not stated.
- [GitHub](https://github.com/pricing) — opened 2026-10-05. Free repositories and issue workflows; Team adds repository rules and reviewers. **Observed price:** Free tier; Team USD 4/user/month with first-twelve-month qualifier. Later renewal and commitment unverified.

**Evidence limit:** Vendor offers establish alternatives, not unmet demand, our pricing, acquisition or achieved outcomes. Confidence: medium; official competitor evidence only.

### Economics and distribution

**Proposed plan:** subscription, USD 149/month; workspace. **34 accounts** reach at least $5,000 gross MRR before churn, discounts, taxes, fees and costs.

**Free boundary:** One public contract and five consumers.

**Paid boundary:** Ten private contracts, consumer reviews and migration history.

**Lifetime:** No lifetime hosted collaboration; continuing security, storage and support need recurring funding.

**Acquisition test:** Offer API-first SaaS teams a retrospective of one delayed deprecation through developer communities.

**Retention:** Each breaking change and support-window review.

**Defensibility hypothesis:** Consumer-specific compatibility decisions and migration history.

### Delivery and operating risk

**First useful workflow:** Contract versions; Consumer review portal; Retirement workflow.

**Build estimate:** 10–14 full-time builder-weeks, excluding discovery, security certification and external procurement.

**Pilot cash:** USD 20–50/month; fits the $50 envelope under the stated assumptions. Three teams, thirty contracts, 500 consumers; schemas only, no API traffic. Labor, acquisition, payment fees and taxes excluded.

**Cost drivers:** Schema history and notifications; limit size and sending volume.

**After revenue:** Up to $300 after revenue funds backups and measured capacity; preserve usage caps.

**Dependencies:** OpenAPI upload; CI contract callback.

**Global limits:** Redact internal endpoints/examples; protect consumer identities and access scopes.

**Risks:** Postman already owns team API workspaces.; Git issues and email may be sufficient..

### Challenge and validation

**Independent challenge (−6):** External API-consumer commitments can differ from internal data contracts, but a portal requiring every consumer to acknowledge changes faces adoption risk. Consumer inventories cannot rely on manually maintained lists alone. Require named external owners and actual migration evidence; schema changes and notifications alone are an API-tool feature.

**Test:** Ten interviews, five deprecation walkthroughs; three $149/month pilots with named external consumer participation.

**Kill gate:** Stop if consumers will not acknowledge changes or providers cannot identify actual consumers.

**Dimension scores:** pain 4/5, recurrence 4/5, budget 4/5, distribution 3/5, wedge 3/5, feasibility 3/5, global 4/5, cost 5/5.

**Score rationale:** Clear persistent record; external participation and installed API tooling are hurdles.

**Sensitivity:** distribution-first rank 20; feasibility-first rank 21.

<a id="s065"></a>
## S065 — Test fixture lifecycle manager

**Rank 45/100 · Reserve · final 61/100** (base 69, challenge −8). Category: Developer / data / IT operations.

**Buyer:** QA/platform manager maintaining integration-test environments

**Job and payment hypothesis:** Keep approved test datasets reproducible across releases without copying production data blindly. Less test flakiness and setup work could justify payment.

**Current workaround:** Seed scripts, shared SQL files and undocumented fixtures.

**Core record:** Fixture release with scenario coverage, generation recipe and environment usage.

**Full workflow:** Specify scenario → Prepare synthetic recipe → Review safety → Publish fixture version → Track refresh and retirement

**Roles:** QA engineer; Data/platform reviewer.

**Entry hypothesis:** Versioned, reviewed deterministic fixtures for one database stack; no hosted generative AI.

**Expansion:** Scenario libraries and customer-run refresh policies.

### Commercial evidence

- [Tonic Structural](https://www.tonic.ai/pricing) — opened 2026-10-05. De-identification and test-data workflows; separate Fabricate product generates synthetic data. **Observed price:** Structural custom pricing, minimum unverified. Adjacent Fabricate Plus displayed $29/month with $25 credits and usage overage; dollar ISO code not stated.

**Evidence limit:** Vendor offers establish alternatives, not unmet demand, our pricing, acquisition or achieved outcomes. Confidence: medium; official competitor evidence only.

### Economics and distribution

**Proposed plan:** subscription, USD 129/month; workspace. **39 accounts** reach at least $5,000 gross MRR before churn, discounts, taxes, fees and costs.

**Free boundary:** One local recipe and export.

**Paid boundary:** Twenty fixture releases with reviews and usage history.

**Lifetime:** No lifetime hosted collaboration; continuing security, storage and support need recurring funding.

**Acquisition test:** Ask backend testing communities to reproduce a flaky integration test using a sanitized fixture recipe.

**Retention:** Schema changes and integration-test refreshes.

**Defensibility hypothesis:** Reviewed domain scenarios and reproducible compatibility history.

### Delivery and operating risk

**First useful workflow:** Recipe versioning; Safety review; Customer-run refresh evidence.

**Build estimate:** 10–14 full-time builder-weeks, excluding discovery, security certification and external procurement.

**Pilot cash:** USD 20–50/month; fits the $50 envelope under the stated assumptions. Three teams, sixty recipes, metadata only; datasets generated locally. Labor, acquisition, payment fees and taxes excluded.

**Cost drivers:** Runner support and accidental sensitive uploads; forbid source datasets.

**After revenue:** Up to $300 after revenue funds backups and measured capacity; preserve usage caps.

**Dependencies:** PostgreSQL recipe manifests; Customer CLI.

**Global limits:** No anonymization guarantee; reject production values and require user-reviewed synthetic examples.

**Risks:** Tonic supplies mature test-data workflows.; Seed scripts and free fake-data libraries cost little..

### Challenge and validation

**Independent challenge (−8):** Versioned deterministic recipes, review and retirement are already natural Git/CI workflows. The separate SaaS must own cross-environment fixture usage and accepted scenario coverage, not just wrap seed files. Customer-side generation saves infrastructure but shifts difficult reproducibility and troubleshooting into support; validate one stack through two real schema changes.

**Test:** Ten interviews, five fixture walkthroughs; three $129/month pilots maintaining fixtures through two schema changes.

**Kill gate:** Stop if teams want only a generator or secure reproducibility requires taking custody of production data.

**Dimension scores:** pain 3/5, recurrence 5/5, budget 3/5, distribution 3/5, wedge 3/5, feasibility 3/5, global 4/5, cost 5/5.

**Score rationale:** Recurring record is coherent; behavior change and free code libraries constrain demand.

**Sensitivity:** distribution-first rank 41; feasibility-first rank 46.

**Rank 77/100 · Defer · final 55/100** (base 65, challenge −10). Category: Developer / data / IT operations.

**Full workflow:** Request purpose → Approve scope → Issue lease → Record activity → Revoke and verify

**Roles:** Database operator; Security approver.

**Entry hypothesis:** One-database-stack access with transparent customer-run enforcement; security hypothesis only.

**Expansion:** Policy exceptions and controlled emergency access.

### Commercial evidence

- [StrongDM](https://www.strongdm.com/pricing) — opened 2026-10-05. Just-in-time approval, runtime authorization and access audit/session records. **Observed price:** Contact sales; per-user single-SKU stated; amount, currency and commitment unverified.
- [Bytebase](https://www.bytebase.com/pricing/) — opened 2026-10-05. Database change and access governance; Community allows twenty users and ten instances. **Observed price:** Community free; Pro displayed $20/user/month, ten instances, cancel anytime; Enterprise annual. Dollar ISO code not stated.

**Evidence limit:** Vendor offers establish alternatives, not unmet demand, our pricing, acquisition or achieved outcomes. Confidence: medium; official competitor evidence only.

### Economics and distribution

**Proposed plan:** subscription, USD 249/month; workspace. **21 accounts** reach at least $5,000 gross MRR before churn, discounts, taxes, fees and costs.

**Free boundary:** Local policy simulation only.

**Paid boundary:** Twenty named operators and enforced lease history.

**Lifetime:** No lifetime hosted collaboration; continuing security, storage and support need recurring funding.

**Acquisition test:** Offer database consultants a design review of one existing access-request process; no live credentials.

**Retention:** Every production investigation and access review.

**Defensibility hypothesis:** Trustworthy enforcement and audit consistency, requiring substantial assurance.

### Delivery and operating risk

**First useful workflow:** Lease state machine; Customer-side enforcement; Revocation reconciliation.

**Build estimate:** 18–26 full-time builder-weeks, excluding discovery, security certification and external procurement.

**Pilot cash:** USD 120–300/month; exceeds the $50 envelope under the stated assumptions. Safe proxy and recovery testing exceed a metadata pilot; no claim that production assurance fits $50. Labor, acquisition, payment fees and taxes excluded.

**Cost drivers:** Security testing, high availability and session logs; capped sessions, customer-held logs.

**After revenue:** Up to $300 after revenue funds backups and measured capacity; preserve usage caps.

**Dependencies:** Customer-controlled database proxy; Identity provider.

**Global limits:** Credential compromise and false revocation are critical risks; independent security review required.

**Risks:** StrongDM and Bytebase already govern access.; Native database roles and tickets are cheaper substitutes..

### Challenge and validation

**Independent challenge (−10):** Correctly cost-gated. This is security enforcement, not inexpensive approval tracking: outage behavior, scope correctness and revocation need adversarial review and recovery testing. StrongDM, Bytebase and native roles make a weak feature wedge especially dangerous. Do not count conditional security demonstrations as production readiness.

**Test:** Ten interviews and five isolated lab demos; three conditional $249/month commitments after verified failure/recovery tests.

**Kill gate:** Defer unless enforcement remains safe during outages and independent review fits funded capacity.

**Dimension scores:** pain 5/5, recurrence 5/5, budget 4/5, distribution 2/5, wedge 2/5, feasibility 1/5, global 3/5, cost 1/5.

**Score rationale:** High-value pain does not offset severe security and operational entry costs.

**Sensitivity:** distribution-first rank 85; feasibility-first rank 96.

**Rank 95/100 · Defer · final 46/100** (base 58, challenge −12). Category: Developer / data / IT operations.

**Job and payment hypothesis:** Resolve architectural proposals and revisit consequential decisions. Avoiding repeated debates might justify payment; evidence is weak.

**Core record:** Decision record with alternatives, dissent, owner and review outcome.

**Full workflow:** Submit proposal → Gather affected teams → Resolve objections → Accept decision → Review consequences

**Roles:** Proposing engineer; Architecture reviewer.

**Entry hypothesis:** Time-bounded cross-team decisions and consequence reviews rather than a general wiki.

**Expansion:** Decision dependencies and exception review portfolios.

### Commercial evidence

- [Slite](https://slite.com/pricing) — opened 2026-10-05. Collaborative documents, verification, ownership, history and recurring workflows. **Observed price:** Basic displayed $10/user/month billed yearly; Pro $20 yearly basis. Dollar ISO code/seat minimum not stated.
- [GitHub](https://github.com/pricing) — opened 2026-10-05. Free repositories and issue workflows; Team adds repository rules and reviewers. **Observed price:** Free tier; Team USD 4/user/month with first-twelve-month qualifier. Later renewal and commitment unverified.

**Evidence limit:** Vendor offers establish alternatives, not unmet demand, our pricing, acquisition or achieved outcomes. Confidence: medium; official competitor evidence only.

### Economics and distribution

**Proposed plan:** subscription, USD 69/month; workspace. **73 accounts** reach at least $5,000 gross MRR before churn, discounts, taxes, fees and costs.

**Free boundary:** Ten public decisions and solo use.

**Paid boundary:** Private team reviews and complete decision history.

**Lifetime:** No lifetime hosted collaboration; continuing security, storage and support need recurring funding.

Slite and free ADR repositories are adequate candidates to falsify the wedge; stop unless a budget owner identifies repeated measurable decision failure.

**Global limits:** Reproduction logs can contain secrets; redact locally and enforce case-level access.

### Challenge and validation

**Independent challenge (−8):** Independent check found Linear’s native Zendesk integration creates/links issues and reopens customer tickets when engineering completes or cancels work. Much of the claimed release-to-customer closure is already bundled. A separate product needs verified reproductions across affected versions and customer retest acceptance beyond ticket sync, with no manual double entry.

**Test:** Ten interviews, five real-case demos; three $149/month pilots closing cases across two releases.

**Kill gate:** Stop if simple ticket synchronization solves the problem or buyers cannot authorize required APIs.

**Dimension scores:** pain 4/5, recurrence 5/5, budget 4/5, distribution 3/5, wedge 3/5, feasibility 3/5, global 4/5, cost 4/5.

**Score rationale:** Persistent technical ownership has potential; connector maintenance and incumbent overlap matter.

**Sensitivity:** distribution-first rank 25; feasibility-first rank 33.

<a id="s071"></a>
## S071 — Supported-release operations

**Rank 17/100 · Reserve · final 68/100** (base 73, challenge −5). Category: Developer / data / IT operations.

**Buyer:** Release engineering manager at a commercial software vendor maintaining LTS branches

**Job and payment hypothesis:** Coordinate backports and support commitments across supported software versions. Fewer missed backports and unsupported customer promises could justify payment.

**Current workaround:** Branch spreadsheets, release tickets and customer-version notes.

**Core record:** Supported release line with backport decisions, test matrix and retirement evidence.

**Full workflow:** Register support matrix → Triage backport → Approve target branches → Collect validation → Publish/retire release

**Roles:** Release manager; Support engineer.

**Entry hypothesis:** LTS branch decisions and support evidence across existing build systems.

**Expansion:** Patch portfolios and customer-version migration planning.

### Commercial evidence

- [Octopus Deploy](https://octopus.com/pricing/overview) — opened 2026-10-05. Release snapshots, runbooks, promotion and approvals; cloud and self-hosted editions. **Observed price:** Cloud Professional default USD 4,330/year; Server USD 2,080/year; first paid size twenty projects. Free ten projects; paid annual commitment.
- [GitHub](https://github.com/pricing) — opened 2026-10-05. Free repositories and issue workflows; Team adds repository rules and reviewers. **Observed price:** Free tier; Team USD 4/user/month with first-twelve-month qualifier. Later renewal and commitment unverified.

**Evidence limit:** Vendor offers establish alternatives, not unmet demand, our pricing, acquisition or achieved outcomes. Confidence: medium; official competitor evidence only.

### Economics and distribution

**Proposed plan:** subscription, USD 149/month; workspace. **34 accounts** reach at least $5,000 gross MRR before churn, discounts, taxes, fees and costs.

**Free boundary:** One public support matrix.

**Paid boundary:** Ten release lines and retained backport decisions.

**Lifetime:** No lifetime hosted collaboration; continuing security, storage and support need recurring funding.

**Acquisition test:** Invite maintainers of commercial self-hosted software to reconstruct one multi-branch security patch.

**Retention:** Patch releases and support-window transitions.

**Defensibility hypothesis:** Historical branch compatibility and accepted backport exceptions.

### Delivery and operating risk

**First useful workflow:** Support matrix; Backport decision queue; Branch evidence/sign-off.

**Build estimate:** 10–14 full-time builder-weeks, excluding discovery, security certification and external procurement.

**Pilot cash:** USD 20–50/month; fits the $50 envelope under the stated assumptions. Three vendors, thirty release lines, 10,000 metadata events; no build hosting. Labor, acquisition, payment fees and taxes excluded.

**Cost drivers:** CI integration and logs; store result pointers rather than binaries.

**After revenue:** Up to $300 after revenue funds backups and measured capacity; preserve usage caps.

**Dependencies:** Git tags/PR links; CI result manifests.

**Global limits:** Do not host proprietary binaries initially; support obligations remain vendor-controlled.

**Risks:** Octopus includes release workflow and a free tier.; GitHub issues and Actions may suffice..

### Challenge and validation

**Independent challenge (−5):** LTS backport decisions across supported customer versions form a credible specialist record. Octopus and Git workflows still own most release execution, and many vendors maintain only one version. Prove active support obligations on at least two branches and an omitted-backport problem; keep binaries and builds outside the hosted service.

**Test:** Ten interviews, five patch histories; three $149/month pilots coordinating two release lines.

**Kill gate:** Stop if vendors support only one version or existing release tools already maintain the whole matrix.

**Dimension scores:** pain 4/5, recurrence 4/5, budget 4/5, distribution 3/5, wedge 3/5, feasibility 3/5, global 4/5, cost 5/5.

**Score rationale:** Specific buyer and recurring record; small addressable segment and build-tool overlap.

**Sensitivity:** distribution-first rank 17; feasibility-first rank 18.

<a id="s072"></a>
## S072 — Developer lab cohort operations

**Rank 79/100 · Defer · final 54/100** (base 62, challenge −8). Category: Developer / data / IT operations.

**Buyer:** Developer education lead at a software infrastructure vendor

**Job and payment hypothesis:** Deliver repeatable hands-on labs and resolve learner environment failures. Fewer failed workshops and repeated instructor setup could justify payment.

**Current workaround:** Lab repositories, video calls and instructor spreadsheets.

**Core record:** Lab version/cohort record with assignments, execution evidence and assistance cases.

**Full workflow:** Publish lab revision → Enroll cohort → Launch customer-owned environment → Track exercises → Resolve and assess

**Roles:** Technical instructor; Developer learner.

**Entry hypothesis:** Customer-funded environments and reproducible lab versions for small technical workshops.

**Expansion:** Instructor runbooks and reusable cohort assessments.

### Commercial evidence

- [Instruqt](https://instruqt.com/) — opened 2026-10-05. Guided sandbox lab authoring, browser delivery and outcome tracking. **Observed price:** Not verified; demo entry points but no amount, unit or minimum on opened page.

**Evidence limit:** Vendor offers establish alternatives, not unmet demand, our pricing, acquisition or achieved outcomes. Confidence: medium; official competitor evidence only.

### Economics and distribution

**Proposed plan:** subscription, USD 149/month; workspace. **34 accounts** reach at least $5,000 gross MRR before churn, discounts, taxes, fees and costs.

**Free boundary:** One local lab, five learners.

**Paid boundary:** Three concurrent cohorts and instructor assistance history.

**Lifetime:** No lifetime hosted collaboration; continuing security, storage and support need recurring funding.

**Acquisition test:** Invite infrastructure-tool developer educators to dry-run one existing lab with five learners.

**Retention:** Each workshop and product-version update.

**Defensibility hypothesis:** Tested exercise compatibility and recurring failure resolutions.

### Delivery and operating risk

**First useful workflow:** Lab version manifests for one supported container runtime; Bounded customer-run launcher with preflight checks and versioned result receipts; Learner progress and exercise evidence; Instructor assistance cases and completion assessment.

**Build estimate:** 12–18 full-time builder-weeks, excluding discovery, security certification and external procurement.

**Pilot cash:** USD 20–50/month; fits the $50 envelope under the stated assumptions. Three authors, sixty learners, metadata only; customer provisions/pays sandbox compute. Labor, acquisition, payment fees and taxes excluded. Maximum 1000 exercise receipts and 1GB metadata/logs, with secrets redacted; full environment images remain customer-hosted.

**Cost drivers:** Runner compatibility and support; time-box exercises and never fund learner cloud usage.

**After revenue:** Up to $300 after revenue funds backups and measured capacity; preserve usage caps.

**Dependencies:** One supported customer-run container launcher; no general remote control; Customer sandbox account with customer-funded compute.

**Global limits:** Execution isolation, learner identity consent and cloud-cost responsibility must be explicit.

**Risks:** Instruqt already provides complete hosted labs.; Free lab repositories plus video calls compete..

### Challenge and validation

**Independent challenge (−8):** Distinct from general course operations only if reproducible lab versions and assistance cases are essential. Customer-funded compute addresses infrastructure cost, but heterogeneous learner machines, sandbox permissions and setup failures can dominate support. A generic cohort board does not validate the promised lab lifecycle; require one supported stack and measured setup success.

**Test:** Ten interviews, five lab dry-runs; three $149/month commitments with measured setup/support reduction.

**Kill gate:** Stop if buyers demand included hosted compute or reliable setup cannot work within one supported stack.

**Dimension scores:** pain 3/5, recurrence 4/5, budget 4/5, distribution 3/5, wedge 2/5, feasibility 2/5, global 4/5, cost 3/5.

**Score rationale:** Complete recurring workflow, but environment reliability and support are expensive.

**Sensitivity:** distribution-first rank 69; feasibility-first rank 79.

<a id="s073"></a>
## S073 — Analytics request delivery workspace

**Rank 76/100 · Defer · final 55/100** (base 65, challenge −10). Category: Developer / data / IT operations.

**Buyer:** Analytics manager supporting several business departments

**Job and payment hypothesis:** Turn recurring analysis requests into reviewed, reusable decision evidence. Less duplicated analysis and ambiguous requests could free analyst time.

**Current workaround:** Chat requests, SQL notebooks and forgotten dashboard links.

**Core record:** Analysis engagement with question, agreed metric definitions, review and refresh responsibility.

**Full workflow:** Clarify question → Agree definitions → Link analysis → Peer review → Publish and revisit

**Roles:** Analytics engineer; Business requester.

**Entry hypothesis:** Request-to-accepted-answer ownership across existing query tools, initially recurring operational analyses.

**Expansion:** Reusable definitions and decision-linked analysis portfolios.

### Commercial evidence

- [Hex](https://hex.tech/pricing/) — opened 2026-10-05. Collaborative data projects and apps; Team adds scheduled runs and version history. **Observed price:** Professional displayed $36/editor/month; Team $75/editor/month; Community free. Billing term/dollar ISO code not stated; larger compute metered.

**Evidence limit:** Vendor offers establish alternatives, not unmet demand, our pricing, acquisition or achieved outcomes. Confidence: medium; official competitor evidence only.

### Economics and distribution

**Proposed plan:** subscription, USD 99/month; workspace. **51 accounts** reach at least $5,000 gross MRR before churn, discounts, taxes, fees and costs.

**Free boundary:** Five public/sample requests.

**Paid boundary:** Fifty active requests, peer review and refresh history.

**Lifetime:** No lifetime hosted collaboration; continuing security, storage and support need recurring funding.

**Acquisition test:** Invite analytics community leads to map five repeated requests from a recent month.

**Retention:** New questions and recurring analysis refreshes.

**Defensibility hypothesis:** Accepted definitions and reuse history, if consumers return.

### Delivery and operating risk

**First useful workflow:** Request scoping; Artifact/review linkage; Refresh ownership.

**Build estimate:** 7–11 full-time builder-weeks, excluding discovery, security certification and external procurement.

**Pilot cash:** USD 20–50/month; fits the $50 envelope under the stated assumptions. Three teams, 150 requests, metadata and 1GB exports; customer runs queries. Labor, acquisition, payment fees and taxes excluded.

**Cost drivers:** Attachments and notebook APIs; retain links, not warehouse results.

**After revenue:** Up to $300 after revenue funds backups and measured capacity; preserve usage caps.

**Dependencies:** Notebook/dashboard links; CSV request import.

**Global limits:** Prevent sensitive dataset uploads; access to linked artifacts remains separately authorized.

**Risks:** Hex already supplies collaboration and scheduled work.; Shared task boards plus notebooks cost little..

### Challenge and validation

**Independent challenge (−10):** Analysis scoping, review and refresh ownership are coherent but resemble ordinary project tracking plus notebook links. Hex already supplies collaboration/version history, and leaving data execution outside means little unique operational evidence is captured. Require business requesters to reuse accepted definitions without analyst-mediated administration.

**Test:** Ten interviews, five request histories; three $99/month pilots showing reuse or less clarification over two cycles.

**Kill gate:** Stop if analysts view the workspace as duplicate project administration or requesters do not return.

**Dimension scores:** pain 3/5, recurrence 4/5, budget 3/5, distribution 3/5, wedge 2/5, feasibility 4/5, global 4/5, cost 5/5.

**Score rationale:** Coherent record, but weak differentiation from notebook and task platforms.

**Sensitivity:** distribution-first rank 61; feasibility-first rank 50.

<a id="s074"></a>
## S074 — Software entitlement operations

**Rank 73/100 · Defer · final 56/100** (base 66, challenge −10). Category: Developer / data / IT operations.

**Buyer:** Founder of a small commercial desktop or self-hosted developer-tool vendor

**Job and payment hypothesis:** Issue, renew and support software licenses reliably. Reliable renewals and fewer activation tickets could justify payment.

**Current workaround:** Database rows, payment webhooks and manual activation support.

**Core record:** Customer entitlement with activation leases, version rights and revocation history.

**Full workflow:** Define license policy → Issue entitlement → Activate installation → Renew or transfer → Revoke and investigate

**Roles:** Vendor operator; Customer administrator.

**Entry hypothesis:** Simple offline-friendly entitlement policies for one software distribution model.

**Expansion:** Reseller issuance and controlled release eligibility.

### Commercial evidence

- [Keygen](https://keygen.sh/pricing/) — opened 2026-10-05. Software licensing and distribution API; volume-based cloud pricing and free self-hosted CE. **Observed price:** Cloud amounts not verified because slider prices absent; paid EE offered, term/minimum unverified.

**Evidence limit:** Vendor offers establish alternatives, not unmet demand, our pricing, acquisition or achieved outcomes. Confidence: medium; official competitor evidence only.

### Economics and distribution

**Proposed plan:** subscription, USD 99/month; workspace. **51 accounts** reach at least $5,000 gross MRR before churn, discounts, taxes, fees and costs.

**Free boundary:** Fifty development licenses, no production SLA.

**Paid boundary:** One thousand production entitlements and operator history.

**Lifetime:** No lifetime hosted collaboration; continuing security, storage and support need recurring funding.

**Acquisition test:** Offer indie developer-tool communities a migration rehearsal from a homegrown license table.

**Retention:** Every activation, subscription change and support case.

**Defensibility hypothesis:** Reliable policy migration and activation compatibility, not secret algorithms.

### Delivery and operating risk

**First useful workflow:** Entitlement ledger; Signed activation policy; Renewal/revocation workflow.

**Build estimate:** 12–18 full-time builder-weeks, excluding discovery, security certification and external procurement.

**Pilot cash:** USD 20–50/month; fits the $50 envelope under the stated assumptions. Three vendors, 3,000 licenses, 30,000 validations/day cap; offline grace reduces traffic. Labor, acquisition, payment fees and taxes excluded.

**Cost drivers:** Validation traffic and key security; quotas, signed offline leases and rate limits.

**After revenue:** Up to $300 after revenue funds backups and measured capacity; preserve usage caps.

**Dependencies:** Payment event import; One client SDK.

**Global limits:** Secure signing-key custody and outage behavior require review; no payment processing or anti-piracy guarantee.

**Risks:** Keygen offers a mature API and free self-hosted CE.; Custom signed keys may suffice for tiny vendors..

### Challenge and validation

**Independent challenge (−10):** Even modest activation volume requires signing-key custody, rotation, replay defenses, offline grace and safe outages. Thirty thousand validations/day is only a hosting estimate, not affordable availability or security assurance. Mature Keygen/free self-hosted alternatives weaken the wedge. Secure lifecycle review and SDK support must be funded before production pilots.

**Test:** Ten interviews, five table migrations; three $99/month pilots with safe renewal and offline-grace demonstrations.

**Kill gate:** Stop if vendors require many SDKs or production availability/security exceeds the bootstrap budget.

**Dimension scores:** pain 4/5, recurrence 5/5, budget 3/5, distribution 3/5, wedge 2/5, feasibility 2/5, global 4/5, cost 3/5.

**Score rationale:** Recurring operational value; security, mature incumbents and SDK support are major constraints.

**Sensitivity:** distribution-first rank 63; feasibility-first rank 77.

<a id="s075"></a>
## S075 — Customer incident communication operations

**Rank 98/100 · Defer · final 43/100** (base 55, challenge −12). Category: Developer / data / IT operations.

**Buyer:** Support operations lead at a multi-product B2B SaaS

**Job and payment hypothesis:** Maintain consistent incident and maintenance communications for distinct customer audiences. Fewer inconsistent updates could reduce support load; willingness to pay is uncertain.

**Current workaround:** Status pages, email drafts and manual audience lists.

**Core record:** Communication incident with audience impact, approved updates and delivery history.

**Full workflow:** Classify customer impact → Draft update → Approve audience/message → Publish with delivery record → Close and review

**Roles:** Incident communicator; Technical incident owner.

**Entry hypothesis:** Audience-specific approval and update accountability above a customer-owned incident process.

**Expansion:** Maintenance campaigns and customer communication histories.

### Commercial evidence

- [Atlassian Statuspage](https://www.atlassian.com/software/statuspage/pricing) — opened 2026-10-05. Incidents, maintenance, components and audiences; free public plan includes one hundred subscribers. **Observed price:** Public Hobby displayed $29/month, 250 subscribers/five members; audience-specific starts $300/month. Dollar ISO code/minimum term not stated.
- [Instatus](https://instatus.com/pricing) — opened 2026-10-05. Free public page includes two hundred subscribers; paid tiers expand audiences and notifications. **Observed price:** Free Starter; paid amounts unverified in dynamic table; yearly option advertises 25% discount.

**Evidence limit:** Vendor offers establish alternatives, not unmet demand, our pricing, acquisition or achieved outcomes. Confidence: medium; official competitor evidence only.

### Economics and distribution

**Proposed plan:** subscription, USD 79/month; workspace. **64 accounts** reach at least $5,000 gross MRR before churn, discounts, taxes, fees and costs.

**Free boundary:** One public page, fifty subscribers.

**Paid boundary:** Five audiences, approvals and capped email delivery.

**Lifetime:** No lifetime hosted collaboration; continuing security, storage and support need recurring funding.

**Acquisition test:** Invite B2B support operations communities to replay one incident with conflicting customer updates.

**Retention:** Incidents and scheduled maintenance.

**Defensibility hypothesis:** Audience-impact history, conditional on accurate customer mappings.

### Delivery and operating risk

**First useful workflow:** Impact/audience register; Message approval; Delivery and closeout.

**Build estimate:** 8–12 full-time builder-weeks, excluding discovery, security certification and external procurement.

**Pilot cash:** USD 20–50/month; fits the $50 envelope under the stated assumptions. Three teams, 1,500 subscribers, 10,000 emails/month; no SMS or uptime monitoring. Labor, acquisition, payment fees and taxes excluded.

**Cost drivers:** Email delivery and incident-time load; quotas and separate delivery queue.

**After revenue:** Up to $300 after revenue funds backups and measured capacity; preserve usage caps.

**Dependencies:** Incident webhook; Customer CSV audiences.

**Global limits:** Subscriber consent, deletion and regional email rules; avoid contractual emergency-delivery guarantees.

**Risks:** Statuspage and Instatus provide strong free/cheap alternatives.; Incident platforms already bundle communications..

### Challenge and validation

**Independent challenge (−12):** Audience approval is mostly a slice of incident communications already offered by status/incident platforms. It also overlaps S062’s incident record. Independent email delivery must remain available during customer outages; excluding monitoring/SMS does not remove delivery obligations. Defer absent repeated audience-isolation failures that incumbents cannot resolve.

**Test:** Ten interviews, five incident replays; three $79/month commitments after a complete update cycle.

**Kill gate:** Stop if existing status tools solve audience approval or delivery reliability requires costs above the cap.

**Dimension scores:** pain 3/5, recurrence 4/5, budget 3/5, distribution 2/5, wedge 1/5, feasibility 3/5, global 4/5, cost 3/5.

**Score rationale:** Complete communications workflow, but weak differentiation and availability obligations.

**Sensitivity:** distribution-first rank 97; feasibility-first rank 94.

## S076 — People operations for remote service firms

**Rank 23/100 · Reserve · final 67/100** (base 67, challenge −0). Category: Business administration and governance.

**Buyer:** Operations lead at a 20–80-person remote firm without an HR specialist

**Job and payment hypothesis:** Keep employee changes, leave, documents and departures consistent Hypothesis: Less chasing and fewer missed employee changes warrant a team subscription

**Current workaround:** Sheets, shared folders and manager email

**Core record:** Employee lifecycle case and effective-dated personnel record

**Full workflow:** Import roster → Collect joining documents → Approve leave and role changes → Coordinate offboarding → Export audit history

**Roles:** HR administrator; Manager; Employee.

**Entry hypothesis:** Preconfigured approvals for firms using external payroll; no salary calculation or automated employment decisions

**Expansion:** Country-specific templates only with qualified partners; payroll export adapters

### Commercial evidence

- [Huma](https://humahr.com/plans-pricing) — opened 2026-10-05. Free personnel records and documents; paid Standard adds absence, signing and onboarding workflows. **Observed price:** Free; Standard/Premium contact sales; price depends on company size.

**Evidence limit:** Vendor features and price do not prove an underserved segment, our conversion, revenue, or retention. Confidence: medium: official category evidence only.

### Economics and distribution

**Proposed plan:** subscription, USD 99/month; organization/month; proposed USD, scope-capped pilot plan. **51 accounts** reach at least $5,000 gross MRR before churn, discounts, taxes, fees and costs.

**Free boundary:** 14-day trial with synthetic data

**Paid boundary:** Team workflows, guest collaboration, history and exports

**Lifetime:** No unlimited hosted lifetime plan; an offline template/export pack could be sold separately, with no MRR credit.

**Acquisition test:** Proposed: recruit five fractional-HR advisers from LinkedIn to replay anonymized client cases

**Retention:** Each hire, leave request and role change creates repeat use

**Defensibility hypothesis:** Hypothesis: Adviser templates and accurate employee history, contingent on trusted migration

### Delivery and operating risk

**First useful workflow:** Import roster; Collect joining documents; Approve leave and role changes; Coordinate offboarding; Export audit history; Roles, audit history, import/export and backup recovery.

**Build estimate:** 10–16 full-time builder-weeks, excluding discovery, security certification and external procurement.

**Pilot cash:** USD 20–50/month; fits the $50 envelope under the stated assumptions. Engineering estimate for 3–5 small design-partner accounts, not a measured quote; labor, tax, payment fees, acquisition and audits excluded. Pilot quota hypothesis: maximum five organizations, 250 active cases, 10000 structured child records, 2GB aggregate attachments, 20MB per file and 2000 transactional emails/month; no bulk marketing, video or uncapped AI. Exceeding a cap pauses new imports pending repricing; these limits are not measured capacity results.

**Cost drivers:** Bound document storage, email and exports; no paid AI in core.

**After revenue:** Up to $300/month could fund managed database/backups, monitoring and bounded integrations after paying usage; not enterprise service levels.

**Dependencies:** CSV imports; explicit email delivery.

**Global limits:** Employment templates vary by country; begin with configurable records rather than legal claims

**Risks:** Free HR tools and payroll suites bundle this; Sensitive employee records require strong access and deletion controls.

### Challenge and validation

**Independent challenge (−0):** The free personnel-record substitute and weak wedge are already reflected in base scores, so no second deduction. Configurable approvals are not yet a reason to migrate sensitive records. Fractional HR advisers are a reachable-channel hypothesis, not demonstrated access. Require one approved roster migration and paid continuation after an offboarding cycle; a synthetic one-month demo does not establish willingness to entrust real employee documents.

**Test:** Run one month of employee-change coordination for three firms at proposed $99/month

**Kill gate:** Stop if advisers require payroll processing or no firm pays without it

**Dimension scores:** pain 4/5, recurrence 5/5, budget 3/5, distribution 3/5, wedge 2/5, feasibility 3/5, global 2/5, cost 4/5.

**Score rationale:** Analyst judgment: recurrence and budget reflect the workflow; distribution/wedge discount established alternatives. Security and integration scope constrain feasibility.

**Sensitivity:** distribution-first rank 21; feasibility-first rank 25.

### Delivery and operating risk

**First useful workflow:** Approve requisition; Collect consented applications; Schedule structured interviews; Compare human scorecards; Approve offer and archive; Roles, audit history, import/export and backup recovery.

**Build estimate:** 10–16 full-time builder-weeks, excluding discovery, security certification and external procurement.

Engineering estimate for 3–5 small design-partner accounts, not a measured quote; labor, tax, payment fees, acquisition and audits excluded. Pilot quota hypothesis: maximum five organizations, 250 active cases, 10000 structured child records, 2GB aggregate attachments, 20MB per file and 2000 transactional emails/month; no bulk marketing, video or uncapped AI. Exceeding a cap pauses new imports pending repricing; these limits are not measured capacity results.

**Proposed plan:** subscription, USD 149/month; organization/month; proposed USD, scope-capped pilot plan. **34 accounts** reach at least $5,000 gross MRR before churn, discounts, taxes, fees and costs.

**Free boundary:** 14-day trial with synthetic data

**Paid boundary:** Team workflows, guest collaboration, history and exports

**Lifetime:** No unlimited hosted lifetime plan; an offline template/export pack could be sold separately, with no MRR credit.

**Acquisition test:** Proposed: people-operations consultants demonstrate one cycle to their existing client segment

**Retention:** Quarterly reviews plus monthly plan follow-through

**Defensibility hypothesis:** Hypothesis: Organization-specific rubrics and longitudinal records

### Delivery and operating risk

**First useful workflow:** Set role expectations; Collect employee and peer input; Calibrate human reviews; Agree development plan; Track follow-through; Roles, audit history, import/export and backup recovery.

**Build estimate:** 10–16 full-time builder-weeks, excluding discovery, security certification and external procurement.

**Pilot cash:** USD 20–50/month; fits the $50 envelope under the stated assumptions. Engineering estimate for 3–5 small design-partner accounts, not a measured quote; labor, tax, payment fees, acquisition and audits excluded. Pilot quota hypothesis: maximum five organizations, 250 active cases, 10000 structured child records, 2GB aggregate attachments, 20MB per file and 2000 transactional emails/month; no bulk marketing, video or uncapped AI. Exceeding a cap pauses new imports pending repricing; these limits are not measured capacity results.

**Cost drivers:** Bound document storage, email and exports; no paid AI in core.

**After revenue:** Up to $300/month could fund managed database/backups, monitoring and bounded integrations after paying usage; not enterprise service levels.

**Dependencies:** CSV imports; explicit email delivery.

**Global limits:** Local employment and works-council requirements can affect rollout

**Risks:** Employees may resist another tool; Incumbents include more capabilities; Performance data is sensitive.

### Challenge and validation

**Independent challenge (−0):** Base scores already capture sensitive records, weak wedge and adoption friction; no additional deduction. <a id="s079"></a>
## S079 — Compensation review planning

**Rank 70/100 · Defer · final 57/100** (base 57, challenge −0). Category: Business administration and governance.

**Buyer:** Finance/HR pair at a 100–400-person international software firm

**Job and payment hypothesis:** Approve budget-constrained pay changes with traceable decisions Hypothesis: Reduce spreadsheet errors and repeated finance reconciliation

**Current workaround:** Versioned salary spreadsheets and private emails

**Core record:** Compensation cycle with proposed awards and approval versions

**Full workflow:** Import pay bands → Allocate review budgets → Collect manager proposals → Model scenarios and approve → Export letters and payroll changes

**Roles:** Compensation owner; Finance approver; Manager.

**Entry hypothesis:** Bring-your-own salary bands; deterministic review planning without buying benchmark datasets

**Expansion:** Equity-grant planning metadata and total-reward statements, without issuing securities

### Commercial evidence

- [Pave](https://www.pave.com/pricing) — opened 2026-10-05. Pave lists compensation planning and total-rewards modules alongside market-data offerings. **Observed price:** Market Data Lite free; paid packages quote-based; no verified numeric planning price.

**Evidence limit:** Vendor features and price do not prove an underserved segment, our conversion, revenue, or retention. Confidence: medium: official category evidence only.

### Economics and distribution

**Proposed plan:** subscription, USD 249/month; organization/month; proposed USD, scope-capped pilot plan. **21 accounts** reach at least $5,000 gross MRR before churn, discounts, taxes, fees and costs.

**Free boundary:** 14-day trial with synthetic data

**Paid boundary:** Team workflows, guest collaboration, history and exports

**Lifetime:** No unlimited hosted lifetime plan; an offline template/export pack could be sold separately, with no MRR credit.

**Acquisition test:** Proposed: compensation consultants and CFO networks; run an anonymized review spreadsheet workshop

**Retention:** Quarterly changes and annual compensation cycles

**Defensibility hypothesis:** Hypothesis: Trusted cycle audit trail; incumbents own data advantage

### Delivery and operating risk

**First useful workflow:** Import pay bands; Allocate review budgets; Collect manager proposals; Model scenarios and approve; Export letters and payroll changes; Roles, audit history, import/export and backup recovery.

**Build estimate:** 12–20 full-time builder-weeks, excluding discovery, security certification and external procurement.

**Pilot cash:** USD 50–120/month; exceeds the $50 envelope under the stated assumptions. Engineering estimate for 3–5 small design-partner accounts, not a measured quote; labor, tax, payment fees, acquisition and audits excluded. Pilot quota hypothesis: maximum five organizations, 250 active cases, 10000 structured child records, 2GB aggregate attachments, 20MB per file and 2000 transactional emails/month; no bulk marketing, video or uncapped AI. Exceeding a cap pauses new imports pending repricing; these limits are not measured capacity results.

**Cost drivers:** Sensitive records require hardened access, independent review and monitoring; these costs exceed baseline assumptions.

**After revenue:** Up to $300/month could fund managed database/backups, monitoring and bounded integrations after paying usage; not enterprise service levels.

**Dependencies:** CSV imports; explicit email delivery.

**Global limits:** Currency, labor rules and pay-equity reporting vary; no compliance guarantee

**Risks:** Seasonal demand; Extremely sensitive salaries; Customers may require benchmark data and enterprise security.

### Challenge and validation

**Independent challenge (−0):** Sensitive pay data, seasonal demand and security constraints already lower feasibility and cost. No extra penalty. A 100–400-person international company may require SSO, granular visibility and integration assurance before a pilot; the stated cash range excludes independent review. Synthetic planning proves calculation behavior only. Require acceptable procurement terms and demonstrated paid annual usage before treating this as bootstrap-feasible.

**Test:** Pilot one budget cycle with synthetic then approved anonymized data; test annual equivalent of $249/month

**Kill gate:** Stop if purchase depends on licensed salary benchmarks or security certification beyond budget

**Dimension scores:** pain 4/5, recurrence 3/5, budget 4/5, distribution 2/5, wedge 2/5, feasibility 2/5, global 2/5, cost 2/5.

**Score rationale:** Analyst judgment: recurrence and budget reflect the workflow; distribution/wedge discount established alternatives. Security and integration scope constrain feasibility.

**Sensitivity:** distribution-first rank 71; feasibility-first rank 74.

## S080 — Policy and control operations for small B2B firms

**Rank 6/100 · Validate · final 72/100** (base 77, challenge −5). Category: Business administration and governance.

**Buyer:** Operations director at a 30–150-person client-audited service firm

**Job and payment hypothesis:** Keep policies, recurring control execution and exceptions connected Hypothesis: Reduce recurring evidence collection and expose unowned control work

**Current workaround:** Wiki plus sign-off spreadsheets and scattered evidence

**Core record:** Versioned policy linked to control runs and exceptions

**Full workflow:** Draft and approve policy → Assign acknowledgements → Run recurring controls → Investigate exceptions → Produce evidence bundle

**Roles:** Policy owner; Control operator; Reviewer.

### Commercial evidence

- [SweetProcess](https://www.sweetprocess.com/) — opened 2026-10-05. Platform connects policies, procedures, tasks, manager approval and version history. **Observed price:** Page shows $82.50/month billed yearly for up to10 members and $4.17/additional member; currency code not explicit.
- [Process Street](https://www.process.st/pricing/) — opened 2026-10-05. Lists document/policy control and workflow operations, with approvals, enforced task order, role assignments, scheduled workflows and workflow revisions. **Observed price:** contact sales for Startup, Pro and Enterprise; Startup lists five users and ten guests. Currency, billing commitment and monetary minimum not verified.

**Evidence limit:** Vendor features and price do not prove an underserved segment, our conversion, revenue, or retention. Confidence: medium: official category evidence only.

### Economics and distribution

**Proposed plan:** subscription, USD 129/month; organization/month; proposed USD, scope-capped pilot plan. **39 accounts** reach at least $5,000 gross MRR before churn, discounts, taxes, fees and costs.

**Free boundary:** 14-day trial with synthetic data

**Paid boundary:** Team workflows, guest collaboration, history and exports

**Lifetime:** No unlimited hosted lifetime plan; an offline template/export pack could be sold separately, with no MRR credit.

**Acquisition test:** Proposed: small ISO implementation consultants show an evidence-to-policy demo to existing clients

**Retention:** Scheduled controls, staff changes and client audits

**Defensibility hypothesis:** Hypothesis: Customer-specific policy/control/evidence graph

### Delivery and operating risk

**First useful workflow:** Draft and approve policy; Assign acknowledgements; Run recurring controls; Investigate exceptions; Produce evidence bundle; Roles, audit history, import/export and backup recovery.

**Build estimate:** 8–14 full-time builder-weeks, excluding discovery, security certification and external procurement.

**Pilot cash:** USD 20–50/month; fits the $50 envelope under the stated assumptions. Engineering estimate for 3–5 small design-partner accounts, not a measured quote; labor, tax, payment fees, acquisition and audits excluded. Pilot quota hypothesis: maximum five organizations, 250 active cases, 10000 structured child records, 2GB aggregate attachments, 20MB per file and 2000 transactional emails/month; no bulk marketing, video or uncapped AI. Exceeding a cap pauses new imports pending repricing; these limits are not measured capacity results.

**Cost drivers:** Bound document storage, email and exports; no paid AI in core.

**After revenue:** Up to $300/month could fund managed database/backups, monitoring and bounded integrations after paying usage; not enterprise service levels.

**Dependencies:** CSV imports; explicit email delivery.

**Global limits:** Framework mapping must be customer-approved; no automated legal or audit determinations

**Risks:** Procedures and audit suites already overlap this workflow; Onboarding could become consulting-heavy.

### Challenge and validation

**Independent challenge (−5):** A worthwhile validation candidate, but Process Street already combines policy/document control with workflow execution, approvals and permissions. This new comparison weakens the claimed policy-to-execution distinction. Test three concrete recurring controls and evidence lineage against configured incumbents, not a static wiki. Consultant introductions remain unverified; consultant setup labor must be separated from software value. Validate means discovery and paid-pilot testing, not build approval.

**Test:** Replay one recent client evidence request; charge $129/month for three recurring control cycles

**Kill gate:** Stop if teams only need a static policy library or cannot distinguish this from their wiki

- [Procurify](https://www.procurify.com/pricing/) — opened 2026-10-05. Base purchasing includes requests, orders, vendors, approvals, budgets and accounting integrations. AP invoice matching and contracts are separate add-ons; Basic requesting/receiving users are unlimited. **Observed price:** contact sales; quotes depend on products, add-ons, Pro users and integrations. Currency, monetary price, billing commitment and minimum Pro seats not verified.

**Evidence limit:** Vendor features and price do not prove an underserved segment, our conversion, revenue, or retention. Confidence: medium: official category evidence only.

**Acquisition test:** Proposed: QuickBooks/Xero advisory firms; demonstrate a service-purchase approval cycle

**Retention:** Every purchase and monthly invoice reconciliation

**Defensibility hypothesis:** Hypothesis: Approval configurations and historical commitment-to-invoice matching

### Delivery and operating risk

**First useful workflow:** Request service; Compare quotations; Approve budget and vendor; Track service acceptance; Match invoice and export; Roles, audit history, import/export and backup recovery.

**Build estimate:** 10–16 full-time builder-weeks, excluding discovery, security certification and external procurement.

Engineering estimate for 3–5 small design-partner accounts, not a measured quote; labor, tax, payment fees, acquisition and audits excluded. Pilot quota hypothesis: maximum five organizations, 250 active cases, 10000 structured child records, 2GB aggregate attachments, 20MB per file and 2000 transactional emails/month; no bulk marketing, video or uncapped AI. Exceeding a cap pauses new imports pending repricing; these limits are not measured capacity results.

QuickBooks/Xero advisers are a channel hypothesis and may expect native synchronization. Prove a CSV workflow remains useful through two purchase cycles before funding adapters.

Pilot quota hypothesis: maximum five organizations, 250 active cases, 10000 structured child records, 2GB aggregate attachments, 20MB per file and 2000 transactional emails/month; no bulk marketing, video or uncapped AI. Exceeding a cap pauses new imports pending repricing; these limits are not measured capacity results.

**Cost drivers:** Document conversion, e-sign API and secure audit storage; cap files and use customer e-sign accounts.

**After revenue:** Up to $300/month could fund managed database/backups, monitoring and bounded integrations after paying usage; not enterprise service levels.

### Commercial evidence

- [Rocketlane](https://www.rocketlane.com/pricing) — opened 2026-10-05. Includes customer portals, approvals, project management and unlimited customer members. **Observed price:** Standard $49/team member/month billed annually; minimum5 members, $245/month equivalent; currency code not explicit.
- [Dock](https://www.dock.us/pricing) — opened 2026-10-05. Offers customer onboarding/client portals, shared workspaces and free external collaborators. Standard includes five internal users, unlimited workspaces and Salesforce/HubSpot integrations; Free lists ten workspaces. **Observed price:** Standard displays $350/month for five internal users and $50/month extra seats; currency and Standard billing commitment not verified. Premium displays $1,000/month billed annually, ten users. Free internal-user entitlement is unclear in the table.

**Evidence limit:** Vendor features and price do not prove an underserved segment, our conversion, revenue, or retention. Confidence: medium: official category evidence only.

### Economics and distribution

**Retention:** Every new implementation and recurring handover

**Defensibility hypothesis:** Hypothesis: Reusable customer-specific implementation templates and completion evidence

### Delivery and operating risk

**First useful workflow:** Convert sales handoff; Agree success criteria; Collect customer prerequisites; Coordinate go-live milestones; Accept and hand over; Roles, audit history, import/export and backup recovery.

**Build estimate:** 8–14 full-time builder-weeks, excluding discovery, security certification and external procurement.

**Pilot cash:** USD 20–50/month; fits the $50 envelope under the stated assumptions. Engineering estimate for 3–5 small design-partner accounts, not a measured quote; labor, tax, payment fees, acquisition and audits excluded. Pilot quota hypothesis: maximum five organizations, 250 active cases, 10000 structured child records, 2GB aggregate attachments, 20MB per file and 2000 transactional emails/month; no bulk marketing, video or uncapped AI. Exceeding a cap pauses new imports pending repricing; these limits are not measured capacity results.

**Cost drivers:** Bound document storage, email and exports; no paid AI in core.

**After revenue:** Up to $300/month could fund managed database/backups, monitoring and bounded integrations after paying usage; not enterprise service levels.

**Global limits:** Global remote workflow; timezone and data-processing agreements still matter

The complete MVP loop is now included. Run prerequisite-to-acceptance cycles with customer guests and test whether manual sales handoffs remain acceptable after setup.

## S086 — Grant portfolio operations for small nonprofits

**Rank 5/100 · Reserve · final 72/100** (base 77, challenge −5). Category: Business administration and governance.

**Buyer:** Grants manager at a nonprofit managing10–40 active applications and awards

**Job and payment hypothesis:** Carry commitments from proposal through reporting and closeout Hypothesis: Reduce repeated reporting assembly and missed award commitments

**Current workaround:** Funding spreadsheets, shared narrative documents and finance exports

**Core record:** Grant application and awarded-obligation record

**Full workflow:** Import chosen opportunities → Assign proposal evidence → Approve submission packet → Track award commitments and spend → Prepare funder reports

**Roles:** Grant writer; Program lead; Finance reviewer.

**Entry hypothesis:** Bring-your-own funding sources; emphasize post-award responsibility without paid discovery data

**Expansion:** Funder relationship history, reusable outcomes and accounting imports

### Commercial evidence

- [Instrumentl](https://www.instrumentl.com/pricing) — opened 2026-10-05. Full-cycle grant platform spans discovery, applications and post-award management. **Observed price:** Discover $299/month annual or$326 monthly for3users; Full Lifecycle $999 annual-equivalent or$1159 monthly for15users; dollar currency code not explicit.
- [Grantseeker](https://grantseeker.io/pricing?lg_redirect=1) — opened 2026-10-05. Classic Premium lists grant tracking, program budgeting, document storage, report series, grant timelines and Excel export. Pro adds configurable workflows, permissions and account integrations. **Observed price:** Premium displays $19.99/month and Prospecting $51.99/month; currency, seat minimum and selected annual/monthly toggle basis not verified. Pro uses custom formula-based pricing.

**Evidence limit:** Vendor features and price do not prove an underserved segment, our conversion, revenue, or retention. Confidence: medium: official category evidence only.

### Economics and distribution

**Proposed plan:** subscription, USD 129/month; organization/month; proposed USD, scope-capped pilot plan. **39 accounts** reach at least $5,000 gross MRR before churn, discounts, taxes, fees and costs.

**Free boundary:** 14-day trial with synthetic data

**Paid boundary:** Team workflows, guest collaboration, history and exports

**Lifetime:** No unlimited hosted lifetime plan; an offline template/export pack could be sold separately, with no MRR credit.

**Acquisition test:** Proposed: grant-writing consultants and nonprofit finance networks; analyze awarded-grant reporting calendars

**Retention:** Applications plus monthly spend and quarterly reporting

**Defensibility hypothesis:** Hypothesis: Reusable funder narrative/evidence and linked program outcomes

### Delivery and operating risk

**First useful workflow:** Import chosen opportunities; Assign proposal evidence; Approve submission packet; Track award commitments and spend; Prepare funder reports; Roles, audit history, import/export and backup recovery.

**Build estimate:** 10–16 full-time builder-weeks, excluding discovery, security certification and external procurement.

**Pilot cash:** USD 20–50/month; fits the $50 envelope under the stated assumptions. Engineering estimate for 3–5 small design-partner accounts, not a measured quote; labor, tax, payment fees, acquisition and audits excluded. Pilot quota hypothesis: maximum five organizations, 250 active cases, 10000 structured child records, 2GB aggregate attachments, 20MB per file and 2000 transactional emails/month; no bulk marketing, video or uncapped AI. Exceeding a cap pauses new imports pending repricing; these limits are not measured capacity results.

**Cost drivers:** Bound document storage, email and exports; no paid AI in core.

**After revenue:** Up to $300/month could fund managed database/backups, monitoring and bounded integrations after paying usage; not enterprise service levels.

**Dependencies:** CSV imports; explicit email delivery.

**Global limits:** Grant conditions vary; no eligibility/funding guarantee or legal compliance claim

**Risks:** Many customers buy primarily for a funding database; Small nonprofits may have weak software budgets.

### Challenge and validation

**Independent challenge (−5):** New low-end competitor evidence materially weakens the Instrumentl-only price comparison: Grantseeker Classic lists grant tracking, budgeting, report series and timelines at a displayed $19.99/month. The proposed $129 must therefore win a specific post-award responsibility workflow, not merely tracking without discovery. Require paid use after a real funder report. Distribution score four is not customer-validated; grant consultants may sell services rather than software.

**Test:** Manage five awarded grants across three nonprofits; ask for$129/month after one reporting cycle

**Kill gate:** Stop if willingness to pay depends on a licensed funder database

**Dimension scores:** pain 4/5, recurrence 5/5, budget 3/5, distribution 4/5, wedge 3/5, feasibility 4/5, global 4/5, cost 4/5.

**Score rationale:** Analyst judgment: recurrence and budget reflect the workflow; distribution/wedge discount established alternatives. Security and integration scope constrain feasibility.

**Sensitivity:** distribution-first rank 4; feasibility-first rank 3.

**Retention:** Repeated calls and post-award reporting

**Defensibility hypothesis:** Hypothesis: Program-specific review rubrics and award history

### Delivery and operating risk

**First useful workflow:** Publish application requirements; Collect submissions; Assign conflict-aware reviewers; Record human award decisions; Track milestones and reports; Roles, audit history, import/export and backup recovery.

**Build estimate:** 12–20 full-time builder-weeks, excluding discovery, security certification and external procurement.

**Pilot cash:** USD 30–80/month; exceeds the $50 envelope under the stated assumptions. Engineering estimate for 3–5 small design-partner accounts, not a measured quote; labor, tax, payment fees, acquisition and audits excluded. Pilot quota hypothesis: maximum five organizations, 250 active cases, 10000 structured child records, 2GB aggregate attachments, 20MB per file and 2000 transactional emails/month; no bulk marketing, video or uncapped AI. Exceeding a cap pauses new imports pending repricing; these limits are not measured capacity results.

**Rank 56/100 · Reserve · final 59/100** (base 64, challenge −5). Category: Business administration and governance.

**Buyer:** Fundraising lead at a small nonprofit with recurring donors

**Job and payment hypothesis:** Turn donations into accurate stewardship and renewal work Hypothesis: Reduce missed follow-ups and duplicate donor administration

**Current workaround:** Donation processor exports and an email list

**Core record:** Donor relationship with gift restrictions and stewardship commitments

**Full workflow:** Import settled donations → Segment by supporter intent → Assign acknowledgement tasks → Track restricted-gift reporting → Plan repeat-giving outreach

**Expansion:** Campaign attribution and major-gift relationship planning

### Commercial evidence

- [Donorbox](https://donorbox.org/pricing) — opened 2026-10-05. Standard and Pro provide fundraising tools; Pro adds analytics/API and reduced platform fees. **Observed price:** Standard $0/month plus2.95–3.95% platform fees; Pro $150/month plus1.75–2% and separate payment fees; currency code not explicit.

**Evidence limit:** Vendor features and price do not prove an underserved segment, our conversion, revenue, or retention. Confidence: medium: official category evidence only.

### Economics and distribution

**Proposed plan:** subscription, USD 79/month; organization/month; proposed USD, scope-capped pilot plan. **64 accounts** reach at least $5,000 gross MRR before churn, discounts, taxes, fees and costs.

**Free boundary:** 14-day trial with synthetic data

**Paid boundary:** Team workflows, guest collaboration, history and exports

**Lifetime:** No unlimited hosted lifetime plan; an offline template/export pack could be sold separately, with no MRR credit.

**Acquisition test:** Proposed: nonprofit CRM migration consultants and fundraisers; reconcile anonymized donor exports

**Retention:** Donations, acknowledgements and recurring stewardship commitments

**Defensibility hypothesis:** Hypothesis: Clean merged donor history and organization-specific stewardship plans

### Delivery and operating risk

**First useful workflow:** Import settled donations; Segment by supporter intent; Assign acknowledgement tasks; Track restricted-gift reporting; Plan repeat-giving outreach; Roles, audit history, import/export and backup recovery.

**Build estimate:** 10–16 full-time builder-weeks, excluding discovery, security certification and external procurement.

**Pilot cash:** USD 20–50/month; fits the $50 envelope under the stated assumptions. Engineering estimate for 3–5 small design-partner accounts, not a measured quote; labor, tax, payment fees, acquisition and audits excluded. Pilot quota hypothesis: maximum five organizations, 250 active cases, 10000 structured child records, 2GB aggregate attachments, 20MB per file and 2000 transactional emails/month; no bulk marketing, video or uncapped AI. Exceeding a cap pauses new imports pending repricing; these limits are not measured capacity results.

**Cost drivers:** Bound document storage, email and exports; no paid AI in core.

**After revenue:** Up to $300/month could fund managed database/backups, monitoring and bounded integrations after paying usage; not enterprise service levels.

**Dependencies:** CSV imports; explicit email delivery.

**Global limits:** Tax receipts and donation processing stay with existing providers

### Challenge and validation

**Independent challenge (−5):** Donorbox is primarily fundraising-platform evidence; the record lacks a direct donor-CRM comparison covering restricted gifts and stewardship. That evidence gap leaves the stated processor-neutral wedge untested. Cross-source identity matching and restriction history are central, not incidental imports. Seek organizations with several actual donation systems and recurring donor obligations; general fundraising consultants are not yet a proven referral channel.

**Test:** Run a30-day stewardship calendar for three charities; test$79/month

**Kill gate:** Stop if processor-native donor features already satisfy every interviewed team

**Dimension scores:** pain 3/5, recurrence 5/5, budget 3/5, distribution 3/5, wedge 2/5, feasibility 3/5, global 3/5, cost 4/5.

## S089 — Supplier sustainability evidence operations

**Rank 50/100 · Defer · final 61/100** (base 61, challenge −0). Category: Business administration and governance.

**Job and payment hypothesis:** Collect supplier declarations and close evidence gaps across renewal cycles Hypothesis: Reduce repetitive supplier chasing and preserve review history

**Current workaround:** Supplier questionnaires in email and scattered certifications

**Core record:** Supplier assessment with evidence validity and remediation commitments

**Full workflow:** Segment suppliers → Request evidence and consent → Review declarations → Assign corrective actions → Reassess at renewal

**Roles:** Procurement owner; Supplier contact; Internal reviewer.

**Entry hypothesis:** Bring-your-own questionnaires and transparent evidence expiry; no sustainability rating or certification

**Expansion:** Customer-specific questionnaires and supplier risk review boards

### Commercial evidence

- [EcoVadis](https://ecovadis.com/plans-pricing/) — opened 2026-10-05. Enterprise sustainability platform lists supplier engagement and assessments with unlimited users and suppliers. **Observed price:** Annual subscription quote; amount depends on company size; no numeric public amount verified.

**Evidence limit:** Vendor features and price do not prove an underserved segment, our conversion, revenue, or retention. Confidence: medium: official category evidence only.

### Economics and distribution

**Proposed plan:** subscription, USD 249/month; organization/month; proposed USD, scope-capped pilot plan. **21 accounts** reach at least $5,000 gross MRR before churn, discounts, taxes, fees and costs.

**Free boundary:** 14-day trial with synthetic data

**Paid boundary:** Team workflows, guest collaboration, history and exports

**Lifetime:** No unlimited hosted lifetime plan; an offline template/export pack could be sold separately, with no MRR credit.

**Acquisition test:** Proposed: independent procurement consultants serving export-oriented service firms

**Retention:** Supplier changes, expiring evidence and annual customer requests

**Defensibility hypothesis:** Hypothesis: Accepted supplier evidence and review decisions, requiring cross-company trust

### Delivery and operating risk

**First useful workflow:** Segment suppliers; Request evidence and consent; Review declarations; Assign corrective actions; Reassess at renewal; Roles, audit history, import/export and backup recovery.

**Build estimate:** 12–20 full-time builder-weeks, excluding discovery, security certification and external procurement.

**Pilot cash:** USD 40–100/month; exceeds the $50 envelope under the stated assumptions. Engineering estimate for 3–5 small design-partner accounts, not a measured quote; labor, tax, payment fees, acquisition and audits excluded. Pilot quota hypothesis: maximum five organizations, 250 active cases, 10000 structured child records, 2GB aggregate attachments, 20MB per file and 2000 transactional emails/month; no bulk marketing, video or uncapped AI. Exceeding a cap pauses new imports pending repricing; these limits are not measured capacity results.

**Cost drivers:** External-user access, document retention and security review; limited volume pilot only.

**After revenue:** Up to $300/month could fund managed database/backups, monitoring and bounded integrations after paying usage; not enterprise service levels.

**Dependencies:** CSV imports; explicit email delivery.

**Global limits:** Regulatory statements require buyer counsel; no certification or authoritative sustainability score

**Risks:** Supplier participation is hard; Buyers may demand recognized external ratings; Data verification can turn into consulting.

### Challenge and validation

**Independent challenge (−0):** The distinction from recognized sustainability ratings is explicit, but the buyer may pay precisely for standardized accepted evidence rather than another collection workflow. Base scores already capture weak wedge, localization and cost; no second deduction. Require a customer reporting obligation that accepts customer-defined questionnaires. Otherwise this is a supplier document portal with no proven standalone budget.

**Test:** Replay one supplier review cycle using customer-provided documents; test three $249/month pilots

**Kill gate:** Stop if customers pay only for recognized ratings or authoritative regulatory compliance

**Dimension scores:** pain 4/5, recurrence 4/5, budget 4/5, distribution 2/5, wedge 2/5, feasibility 2/5, global 2/5, cost 3/5.

- [Responsive](https://www.responsive.io/pricing) — opened 2026-10-05. Emerging edition lists unlimited response projects, content/collaboration, integrations and access controls. Platform subscription is annual; user licenses and add-ons are separate components. **Observed price:** Starting amount displays $10,000, but currency and the exact scope/unit of that starting amount are not verified. Platform fee is annual; minimum user licenses not stated.

**Evidence limit:** Vendor features and price do not prove an underserved segment, our conversion, revenue, or retention. Confidence: medium: official category evidence only.

### Economics and distribution

**Proposed plan:** subscription, USD 199/month; organization/month; proposed USD, scope-capped pilot plan. **26 accounts** reach at least $5,000 gross MRR before churn, discounts, taxes, fees and costs.

**Paid boundary:** Team workflows, guest collaboration, history and exports

**Acquisition test:** Proposed: bid-writing consultants and proposal-management communities; inspect redacted bid workbooks

**Retention:** Repeated bids and answer-review expiry cycles

**Defensibility hypothesis:** Hypothesis: Approved answer provenance and customer-specific exception history

### Delivery and operating risk

**First useful workflow:** Qualify opportunity; Import requirements and owners; Draft from approved evidence; Review exceptions and approvals; Assemble submission and record outcome; Roles, audit history, import/export and backup recovery.

**Build estimate:** 10–16 full-time builder-weeks, excluding discovery, security certification and external procurement.

**Pilot cash:** USD 20–50/month; fits the $50 envelope under the stated assumptions. Engineering estimate for 3–5 small design-partner accounts, not a measured quote; labor, tax, payment fees, acquisition and audits excluded. Pilot quota hypothesis: maximum five organizations, 250 active cases, 10000 structured child records, 2GB aggregate attachments, 20MB per file and 2000 transactional emails/month; no bulk marketing, video or uncapped AI. Exceeding a cap pauses new imports pending repricing; these limits are not measured capacity results.

**Cost drivers:** Bound document storage, email and exports; no paid AI in core.

**After revenue:** Up to $300/month could fund managed database/backups, monitoring and bounded integrations after paying usage; not enterprise service levels.

**Dependencies:** CSV imports; explicit email delivery.

**Global limits:** Export restrictions and sensitive bid information require organization-specific controls

**Risks:** Incumbents offer broad response suites; AI tools lower drafting willingness to pay; Document formats vary.

### Challenge and validation

**Independent challenge (−0):** A reasonable discovery candidate because recurring bids create a clear owner, reusable evidence and expensive coordination. The second source confirms formidable direct competition: Responsive sells centralized content and collaboration even to emerging proposal teams. No extra penalty beyond the existing wedge/feasibility discount. A pilot must include document roundtrip, exception approval and final submission assembly, and quantify reduced review work rather than AI draft volume.

**Test:** Concierge-coordinate two concurrent real bids; ask three firms for $199/month

**Kill gate:** Stop if buyers value draft generation only or require bespoke document parsing per bid

**Sensitivity:** distribution-first rank 9; feasibility-first rank 12.

## S091 — B2B customer support operations

**Rank 42/100 · Defer · final 63/100** (base 63, challenge −0). Category: Business administration and governance.

**Buyer:** Support lead at a 10–60-person B2B SaaS company

**Job and payment hypothesis:** Resolve contracted customer requests through a shared support lifecycle Hypothesis: Prevent missed customer commitments and reduce duplicate handling across a team

**Current workaround:** Shared mailbox, account spreadsheet and scattered help articles

**Full workflow:** Receive and identify customer → Check contracted support terms → Assign and collaborate on response → Confirm customer resolution → Update reusable knowledge and service report
