### Commercial evidence

- [Help Scout](https://www.helpscout.com/pricing/) — opened 2026-10-05. Free offers five users, one inbox and one Docs site; paid plans add workflow, SLA and company-level support features. **Observed price:** Standard displays $25/user/month, Plus $45 and Pro $75; page has monthly/annual toggle but displayed selection and currency code not verified. Pro minimum ten users; free has 100 monthly contacts.

**Evidence limit:** Vendor features and price do not prove an underserved segment, our conversion, revenue, or retention. Confidence: medium: official category evidence only.

### Economics and distribution

**Proposed plan:** subscription, USD 99/month; organization/month; proposed USD, scope-capped pilot plan. **51 accounts** reach at least $5,000 gross MRR before churn, discounts, taxes, fees and costs.

**Free boundary:** Sample-data sandbox; trial capped at 100 imported conversations

**Paid boundary:** Team workflows, guest collaboration, history and exports

**Lifetime:** No unlimited hosted lifetime plan; an offline template/export pack could be sold separately, with no MRR credit.

**Defensibility hypothesis:** Hypothesis: Customer support history and approved answers; mature incumbents have the same advantage

### Delivery and operating risk

**First useful workflow:** Receive and identify customer; Check contracted support terms; Assign and collaborate on response; Confirm customer resolution; Update reusable knowledge and service report; Roles, audit history, import/export and backup recovery.

**Build estimate:** 12–18 full-time builder-weeks, excluding discovery, security certification and external procurement.

**Pilot cash:** USD 30–80/month; exceeds the $50 envelope under the stated assumptions. Engineering estimate for 3–5 small design-partner accounts, not a measured quote; labor, tax, payment fees, acquisition and audits excluded. Pilot quota hypothesis: maximum five organizations, 250 active cases, 10000 structured child records, 2GB aggregate attachments, 20MB per file and 2000 transactional emails/month; no bulk marketing, video or uncapped AI. Exceeding a cap pauses new imports pending repricing; these limits are not measured capacity results.

**Cost drivers:** Reliable inbound/outbound email, searchable history and attachment storage; limited contacts and no voice/SMS/AI hosting.

**After revenue:** Up to $300/month could fund managed database/backups, monitoring and bounded integrations after paying usage; not enterprise service levels.

**Dependencies:** CSV imports; explicit email delivery.

**Global limits:** Global remote support; regional data transfers and consent rules apply, no automatic eligibility decisions

**Risks:** Strong free and mature paid incumbents; Reliable email threading and delivery are hard; Migration risks lost customer messages.

### Challenge and validation

**Independent challenge (−0):** Re-reviewed the new B2B support candidate; it is distinct from research evidence. Help Scout already offers a free inbox/docs tier and paid SLA/company capabilities, while email threading, entitlement identity and message retention require dependable operation. Those risks are already reflected in wedge one and low feasibility, so no additional deduction. The replay should test actual contract exceptions and paid continuation, not simply demonstrate a shared inbox. CSV plus outbound email does not yet specify reliable inbound synchronization.

**Test:** Replay one week of support with five teams; test three $99/month paid continuations tied to contract-specific needs

**Kill gate:** Stop if configuring Help Scout satisfies the contract workflow or buyers primarily want cheap autonomous AI

**Dimension scores:** pain 4/5, recurrence 5/5, budget 3/5, distribution 3/5, wedge 1/5, feasibility 2/5, global 4/5, cost 3/5.

**Score rationale:** Analyst judgment: recurrence and budget reflect the workflow; distribution/wedge discount established alternatives. Security and integration scope constrain feasibility.

### Commercial evidence

- [Expensify](https://help.expensify.com/articles/new-expensify/billing-and-subscriptions/explore-plans-subscriptions-and-pricing/Understand-Expensify-Pricing) — opened 2026-10-05. Collect bills unique active members; Control has annual commitments or pay-per-use pricing and conditional card discounts. **Observed price:** Collect USD5/active member/month; Control USD18/member/month annual commitment, USD36 overage or pay-per-use before qualifying discounts.

**Evidence limit:** Vendor features and price do not prove an underserved segment, our conversion, revenue, or retention. Confidence: medium: official category evidence only.

### Economics and distribution

**Proposed plan:** subscription, USD 99/month; organization/month; proposed USD, scope-capped pilot plan. **51 accounts** reach at least $5,000 gross MRR before churn, discounts, taxes, fees and costs.

**Free boundary:** 14-day trial with synthetic data

**Paid boundary:** Team workflows, guest collaboration, history and exports

**Lifetime:** No unlimited hosted lifetime plan; an offline template/export pack could be sold separately, with no MRR credit.

**Acquisition test:** Proposed: bookkeeping advisers serving remote agencies

**Retention:** Monthly reimbursement and close cycles

**Defensibility hypothesis:** Hypothesis: Accepted expense rules and approval history; weak standalone differentiation

### Delivery and operating risk

**First useful workflow:** Import receipts and claimant details; Check explicit policy rules; Route human approvals; Export approved reimbursement batch; Reconcile settled status; Roles, audit history, import/export and backup recovery.

**Build estimate:** 10–16 full-time builder-weeks, excluding discovery, security certification and external procurement.

**Pilot cash:** USD 30–80/month; exceeds the $50 envelope under the stated assumptions. Engineering estimate for 3–5 small design-partner accounts, not a measured quote; labor, tax, payment fees, acquisition and audits excluded. Pilot quota hypothesis: maximum five organizations, 250 active cases, 10000 structured child records, 2GB aggregate attachments, 20MB per file and 2000 transactional emails/month; no bulk marketing, video or uncapped AI. Exceeding a cap pauses new imports pending repricing; these limits are not measured capacity results.

**Cost drivers:** Receipt storage and optional OCR grow with claims; manual entry required for low-cost pilot.

**After revenue:** Up to $300/month could fund managed database/backups, monitoring and bounded integrations after paying usage; not enterprise service levels.

**Dependencies:** CSV imports; explicit email delivery.

**Global limits:** No card issuance, funds custody or tax calculation; employer owns policy validity

**Risks:** Low-price incumbents; OCR and bank sync expected; Country-specific receipts and taxes.

### Challenge and validation

**Independent challenge (−0):** The expense record is valid, but manual receipt entry weakens the proposed product against low-price mature expense systems. The original feasibility/cost/global scores already account for this, so no additional penalty. Employer policy configuration is not a replacement for local receipt requirements. Validate willingness to use manual entry before testing a price; otherwise the low-cost pilot tests a product buyers do not want.

**Test:** Replay a monthly expense batch with three firms and test $99/month

**Kill gate:** Stop if any buyer requires cards, automatic tax recovery or uncapped OCR to pay

**Dimension scores:** pain 4/5, recurrence 5/5, budget 3/5, distribution 3/5, wedge 2/5, feasibility 2/5, global 2/5, cost 3/5.

**Score rationale:** Analyst judgment: recurrence and budget reflect the workflow; distribution/wedge discount established alternatives. Security and integration scope constrain feasibility.

**Sensitivity:** distribution-first rank 33; feasibility-first rank 54.

## S094 — B2B receivables and dispute operations

**Rank 1/100 · Validate · final 76/100** (base 81, challenge −5). Category: Business administration and governance.

**Buyer:** Finance manager at a 10–100-person B2B agency or consultancy

**Job and payment hypothesis:** Resolve reasons invoices remain unpaid and keep agreed payment actions visible Hypothesis: Reduce repeated chasing and time spent reconstructing invoice disputes

**Current workaround:** Accounting aging report, inbox and promise-to-pay spreadsheet

**Core record:** Invoice dispute case with commitments, approvals and settlement evidence

**Full workflow:** Import open invoices → Triage disputes and missing evidence → Assign client/internal actions → Approve follow-up and payment plan → Reconcile receipts and close cases

**Roles:** Finance owner; Account manager; Customer finance contact.

**Entry hypothesis:** Dispute resolution and account-manager accountability for service invoices; no outsourced collections

**Expansion:** Portfolio cash-risk views and accountant multi-client workspaces

### Commercial evidence

- [Chaser](https://www.chaserhq.com/chaser-pricing) — opened 2026-10-05. Compact supports receivables workflows and four users; Care adds human collection services separately. **Observed price:** Compact from GBP199/month or GBP179/month equivalent with annual savings; revenue ceiling GBP4m; four users. Confirm term at checkout.
- [Upflow](https://upflow.io/pricing) — opened 2026-10-05. Starter includes collections communication, analytics, accounting integrations and unlimited seats. Discover provides free AR dashboards; paid plans scale by gross invoice value and invoice volume. **Observed price:** contact sales; paid plans have yearly commitments and invoice/GIV limits with unlimited user seats. Currency and monetary amount not verified; payment products and custom integrations may cost extra.

**Evidence limit:** Vendor features and price do not prove an underserved segment, our conversion, revenue, or retention. Confidence: medium: official category evidence only.

### Economics and distribution

**Proposed plan:** subscription, USD 149/month; organization/month; proposed USD, scope-capped pilot plan. **34 accounts** reach at least $5,000 gross MRR before churn, discounts, taxes, fees and costs.

**Free boundary:** 14-day trial with synthetic data

**Paid boundary:** Team workflows, guest collaboration, history and exports

**Lifetime:** No unlimited hosted lifetime plan; an offline template/export pack could be sold separately, with no MRR credit.

**Acquisition test:** Proposed: accountant/bookkeeper referral partners; inspect aged invoices with permission

**Retention:** Weekly receivables review and recurring billing disputes

**Defensibility hypothesis:** Hypothesis: Case resolution history and customer-specific payment commitments

### Delivery and operating risk

**First useful workflow:** Import open invoices; Triage disputes and missing evidence; Assign client/internal actions; Approve follow-up and payment plan; Reconcile receipts and close cases; Roles, audit history, import/export and backup recovery.

**Build estimate:** 10–16 full-time builder-weeks, excluding discovery, security certification and external procurement.

**Pilot cash:** USD 20–50/month; fits the $50 envelope under the stated assumptions. Engineering estimate for 3–5 small design-partner accounts, not a measured quote; labor, tax, payment fees, acquisition and audits excluded. Pilot quota hypothesis: maximum five organizations, 250 active cases, 10000 structured child records, 2GB aggregate attachments, 20MB per file and 2000 transactional emails/month; no bulk marketing, video or uncapped AI. Exceeding a cap pauses new imports pending repricing; these limits are not measured capacity results.

**Cost drivers:** Bound document storage, email and exports; no paid AI in core.

**After revenue:** Up to $300/month could fund managed database/backups, monitoring and bounded integrations after paying usage; not enterprise service levels.

**Dependencies:** Accounting CSV import/export first; one read-only accounting connector only after repeated paid demand; customer-approved email delivery.

**Global limits:** No funds custody, legal threats or credit decisions; local collection and messaging rules remain customer responsibilities

**Risks:** Accounting suites include reminders; Sync errors can damage client relationships; Buyers may want human collection services.

### Challenge and validation

**Independent challenge (−5):** Strong discovery candidate with identifiable finance ownership, but concierge resolution can confound software value with collection labor. Require enough recurring disputed invoices and measure owner follow-through and reconciliation, not recovered cash alone. Upflow also provides AR collaboration, integrations and free insights, further narrowing the entry. The distribution score four is ahead of evidence: accountants are a proposed channel with no demonstrated referrals. No automated outreach should occur in this research.

**Test:** Concierge-resolve 20 overdue service invoices across three firms; ask $149/month for repeated case workflow

**Kill gate:** Stop if reminder automation alone is valued or mandatory integrations exceed first-cohort demand

**Dimension scores:** pain 5/5, recurrence 5/5, budget 4/5, distribution 4/5, wedge 3/5, feasibility 3/5, global 3/5, cost 4/5.

**Score rationale:** Analyst judgment: recurrence and budget reflect the workflow; distribution/wedge discount established alternatives. Security and integration scope constrain feasibility.

**Sensitivity:** distribution-first rank 2; feasibility-first rank 7.

## S095 — Usage-based billing operations

**Rank 30/100 · Defer · final 66/100** (base 66, challenge −0). Category: Business administration and governance.

**Buyer:** Finance/engineering pair at a B2B API company introducing metered pricing

**Job and payment hypothesis:** Turn auditable usage and versioned customer terms into approved invoices and adjustment history Hypothesis: Reduce repeated billing reconciliation and make price changes auditable

**Current workaround:** Usage SQL, pricing spreadsheets and manually assembled invoices

**Core record:** Billing period ledger linking deduplicated usage, contract version, invoice lines and credits

**Full workflow:** Import bounded usage ledger → Apply versioned customer terms → Reconcile draft invoice → Approve and export invoice → Record corrections and close period

**Roles:** Billing engineer; Finance reviewer; Customer billing contact.

**Entry hypothesis:** Low-volume API businesses needing explainable batch reconciliation; no real-time high-volume metering or funds custody

**Expansion:** Versioned rate plans, customer cost preview and revenue-close exports

### Commercial evidence

- [Orb](https://www.withorb.com/pricing) — opened 2026-10-05. Orb Core includes event ingestion, hybrid/usage billing, price changes and invoicing; higher tiers add ERP/CRM and warehouse features. **Observed price:** Core, Advanced and Enterprise all use custom pricing; no public numeric amount verified.

**Evidence limit:** Vendor features and price do not prove an underserved segment, our conversion, revenue, or retention. Confidence: medium: official category evidence only.

### Economics and distribution

**Proposed plan:** subscription, USD 249/month; organization/month; proposed USD, scope-capped pilot plan. **21 accounts** reach at least $5,000 gross MRR before churn, discounts, taxes, fees and costs.

**Free boundary:** 14-day trial with synthetic data

**Paid boundary:** Team workflows, guest collaboration, history and exports

**Lifetime:** No unlimited hosted lifetime plan; an offline template/export pack could be sold separately, with no MRR credit.

**Acquisition test:** Proposed: API-founder communities and fractional SaaS finance advisers; reproduce historical invoice periods

**Retention:** Monthly invoicing, pricing changes and credit adjustments

**Defensibility hypothesis:** Hypothesis: Trusted deterministic billing ledger, requiring very high correctness and migration confidence

### Delivery and operating risk

**First useful workflow:** Import bounded usage ledger; Apply versioned customer terms; Reconcile draft invoice; Approve and export invoice; Record corrections and close period; Roles, audit history, import/export and backup recovery.

**Build estimate:** 16–26 full-time builder-weeks, excluding discovery, security certification and external procurement.

**Pilot cash:** USD 60–150/month; exceeds the $50 envelope under the stated assumptions. Engineering estimate for 3–5 small design-partner accounts, not a measured quote; labor, tax, payment fees, acquisition and audits excluded. Pilot quota hypothesis: maximum five organizations, 250 active cases, 10000 structured child records, 2GB aggregate attachments, 20MB per file and 2000 transactional emails/month; no bulk marketing, video or uncapped AI. Exceeding a cap pauses new imports pending repricing; these limits are not measured capacity results.

**Cost drivers:** Idempotent event storage, reconciliation, backup recovery and monitoring; security/correctness review costs excluded and must be separately funded.

**After revenue:** Up to $300/month could fund managed database/backups, monitoring and bounded integrations after paying usage; not enterprise service levels.

**Dependencies:** CSV imports; explicit email delivery.

**Global limits:** No payment processing, tax engine or accounting opinion; external providers handle regulated financial actions

**Risks:** Billing errors directly harm revenue and trust; Orb and Metronome already cover the full cycle; Customers may require production metering and enterprise support.

### Challenge and validation

**Independent challenge (−0):** Re-reviewed the new usage-based billing candidate; it is distinct from CFO planning. Bounded batches avoid real-time ingestion scale but still require deduplication, late-event corrections, price-effective versions and invoice reconciliation. These correctness costs are already heavily discounted, so no additional penalty. Proposed $249 must justify replacing scripts despite Orb and other mature billing systems. The pilot needs exact historical reproduction with documented edge cases; $300 infrastructure does not fund independent correctness review or production support.

**Test:** Reproduce three historical billing periods exactly with customer-approved edge cases; seek paid pilots only after independent correctness review

**Kill gate:** Defer if any unreconciled difference remains or required usage scale exceeds bounded batch processing

**Dimension scores:** pain 5/5, recurrence 5/5, budget 4/5, distribution 2/5, wedge 2/5, feasibility 1/5, global 3/5, cost 2/5.

**Score rationale:** Analyst judgment: recurrence and budget reflect the workflow; distribution/wedge discount established alternatives. Security and integration scope constrain feasibility.

**Sensitivity:** distribution-first rank 49; feasibility-first rank 67.

**Build estimate:** 14–22 full-time builder-weeks, excluding discovery, security certification and external procurement.

**Pilot cash:** USD 60–150/month; exceeds the $50 envelope under the stated assumptions. Engineering estimate for 3–5 small design-partner accounts, not a measured quote; labor, tax, payment fees, acquisition and audits excluded. Pilot quota hypothesis: maximum five organizations, 250 active cases, 10000 structured child records, 2GB aggregate attachments, 20MB per file and 2000 transactional emails/month; no bulk marketing, video or uncapped AI. Exceeding a cap pauses new imports pending repricing; these limits are not measured capacity results.

**Cost drivers:** Controlled access, storage and independent security review; prefer existing customer storage, professional reviews excluded.

**After revenue:** Up to $300/month could fund managed database/backups, monitoring and bounded integrations after paying usage; not enterprise service levels.

**Dependencies:** CSV imports; explicit email delivery.

**Global limits:** No legal or investment advice; data residency, transaction confidentiality and competition restrictions vary

**Risks:** Event-driven revenue at end buyer; Enterprise security expectations; Established data rooms bundle workflows.

### Challenge and validation

**Independent challenge (−0):** Existing VDRs already bundle request workflows, and base scores capture trust, episodic demand and weak acquisition. No extra deduction. Customer-owned storage reduces custody but does not solve permission drift, document-version references or Q&A visibility. An adviser-level portfolio is the plausible recurring buyer; one successful deal replay cannot establish adviser retention or acceptable procurement/security requirements.

**Test:** Replay one anonymized closed deal; test an adviser-level multi-deal subscription

**Kill gate:** Stop if demand is only one-time rooms or certified secure storage must be supplied by us

**Dimension scores:** pain 4/5, recurrence 3/5, budget 4/5, distribution 2/5, wedge 2/5, feasibility 2/5, global 2/5, cost 2/5.

**Score rationale:** Analyst judgment: recurrence and budget reflect the workflow; distribution/wedge discount established alternatives. Security and integration scope constrain feasibility.

**Sensitivity:** distribution-first rank 72; feasibility-first rank 75.
